=== PDF PAGE 11 ===
1 Introduction
1.1 Target group
This documentation is aimed at KUKA AMR integrators and/or operators
with the following knowledge and skills:
• Basic knowledge of the KMP (KUKA Mobile Platform)
• Programming skills in any programming language supported by Goo-
gle protobuf
• Knowledge of open-source technologies, in particular:
‒ Docker: The base of the Navigation Software infrastructure, pow-
ered by Linux container technology
https://docs.docker.com/
‒ gRPC: The APIs are network-based and rely on Google's open
source software.
https://grpc.io/docs/
‒ Protobuf: A serialization library transported by the gRPC servers
https://protobuf.dev/overview/
• For the installation of the Navigation Software:
‒ Basic knowledge of Bash Shell
• Basic knowledge of the KMP (KUKA Mobile Platform)
• Basic knowledge of KUKA.AMR OS
• Basic knowledge of KUKA.AMR Fleet
1.2 Representation of warnings and notes
Safety
These warnings are provided for safety purposes and must be observed.
DANGER
These warnings mean that it is certain or highly probable that death or
severe injuries will occur if no precautions are taken.
WARNING
These warnings mean that death or severe injuries may occur if no pre-
cautions are taken.
CAUTION
These warnings mean that minor injuries may occur if no precautions
are taken.
NOTICE
These warnings mean that damage to property may occur, if no precau-
tions are taken.
These warnings contain references to safety-relevant information or gen-
eral safety measures.
These warnings do not refer to individual hazards or individual precau-
tionary measures.

=== PDF PAGE 13 ===
KUKA.AMR Fleet KUKA fleet management system
(formerly KMReS)
KUKA.AMR Fleet consists of K-RCS (Robot Control System), K-
WCS (Workflow Control System) and K-ECS (Equipment Control
System).
KUKA.AMR OS KUKA AMR Operating System (formerly KarOS)
Operating system in the AMR’s IPC.
Location A pose that has been saved with a name. In addition to the pose,
the location can also contain laser measurement data.
MQTT Message Queuing Telemetry Transport
Open messaging protocol for transferring telemetry data between
devices, which can be used even with high delays and limited net-
works
MR Control Control stack of the KUKA KMR iisy product family
May be applied for additional KMR product families in the future.
RCS Robot Control System
SLAM Simultaneous Localization and Mapping
Method in which the mobile platform creates a map of its environ-
ment and simultaneously determines its position within that map
VDA 5050 Standardized interface for communication between automated gui-
ded vehicles (AGVs) and a master control system

Introduction

=== PDF PAGE 15 ===
2 Product description
2.1 Product description
KUKA.AMR Fleet is a fleet orchestration software that can perform colli-
sion-free high-level graph planning. It coordinates the AMRs in the fleet. It
exposes the Northbridge interface for application development and a Web
UI. It works together with warehouse equipment to complete business
tasks. KUKA.AMR Fleet is compatible with the iiQKA.Mobility software or
the KUKA.AMR OS software.
Information on the KUKA.AMR OS software can be found in the
KUKA.AMR Suite documentation.
Information for iiQKA.Mobility (for KMR iisy) can be found in the
iiQKA.Mobility documentation.
System interaction
Fig. 2-1: System interaction
KUKA.AMR Fleet consists of four services:
• RCS (Robot Control Service)
• WCS (Workflow Control Service)
• ECS (Equipment Control Service)
• KUKA interface (Part of KUKA.AMR Fleet and used for plugin man-
agement)
RCS
RCS is Robot Control Service, mainly used for:
• Robot management
• Container management
• Layout management
• Managing missions and resources (AMRs, containers, parking node,
chargers...), including selecting and locking
• Path planning and calculating dependencies with other AMRs
• Splitting mission into actions
• Sending actions to AMRs according to different protocols
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 15/719
Product description

=== PDF PAGE 16 ===
WCS
WCS is Workflow Control Service, mainly used for:
• Orchestrating business processes according to business needs using
workflows
• Configuring the actions of each process node and setting waiting times
• Monitoring and recovering the jobs from exceptions through the job
dashboard
• Pausing and resuming the jobs as needed
• Receiving job from KUKA interface or order engine or any other up-
stream system
ECS
ECS is Equipment Control Service, mainly used for:
• Interfacing with third-party hardware devices
• Configuring with UI
• Controlling the actual equipment (like PLC, auto door, elevator ...) with
corresponding protocol, like Modbus, S7...
KUKA interface
• Communicating with upstream system, like WMS (Warehouse Manage-
ment Service)
• Plugin management (A jar package that communicates between up-
stream system and KUKA.AMR Fleet)
• Mapping the request from the upstream system to WCS and sending
callback from WCS to upstream system
Application precondition
To start the basic configuration in KUKA.AMR Fleet, make sure the follow-
ing preconditions are met:
• The deployment environment meets the requirements.
Refer to the application deployment document of AMR.
• KUKA.AMR Fleet is installed.
Refer to ( >>> 3.5 "Installing KUKA.AMR Fleet with Docker in Debian
or Ubuntu Linux" Page 27 ).
• AMRs are configured or commissioned via KUKA.AMR Suite.
Refer to the KUKA.AMR Suite document.
• The environment is mapped if using SLAM map.
Refer to the KUKA.AMR Suite document.
• QR codes are fixed on the floor if using QR code map.
Refer to the KUKA.AMR Suite document.
• Fine localization models are created if applicable.
Refer to the KUKA.AMR Suite document.
• The application layout is created.
Refer to ( >>> 5.1 "Layout management" Page 159 ).
• The external device is created if applicable.
Refer to ( >>> 5.13 "Device management" Page 476 ).
• Missions are programed.
Refer to ( >>> 5.7.1 "Workflow management" Page 348 ).
• Mission trigger mechanism is configured.
Refer to ( >>> 5.7.1 "Workflow management" Page 348 ).
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
16/719 | www.kuka.com KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026
Product description

=== PDF PAGE 17 ===
2.2 Intended use and misuse
Intended use
KUKA.AMR Fleet is intended exclusively for controlling and managing a
fleet of types of Mobile Robots approved by KUKA.
Each version of KUKA.AMR Fleet is to be operated exclusively in accord-
ance with the specified system requirements.
Misuse
Any application deviating from the intended use is deemed to be misuse
and is not allowed. Misuse will result in the loss of warranty and liability
claims. The manufacturer is not liable for any damage resulting from such
misuse.

3 Installation
3.1 System requirements
Hardware
• PC with Intel Core i7 or Intel Core i9, or equivalent
• Memory:
‒ 10 platforms or fewer: 16 GB RAM
‒ 11 … 50 platforms: 32 GB RAM
‒ 51 platforms or more: 64 GB RAM
• 200 GB free hard disk space
• Network: no constraints
Software
• Operating system:
‒ Server with Debian Linux:
‒ Debian 11 or later, native or in a virtual machine
‒ Docker Engine 20.10.14 or later with Docker Compose
‒ Server with Ubuntu Linux:
‒ Ubuntu 20.04 desktop or later, native or in a virtual machine
‒ Docker Engine 20.10.14 or later with Docker Compose
‒ Server with Red Hat Enterprise Linux:
‒ Red Hat Enterprise Linux (RHEL) 9.4 or later, native or in a
virtual machine
‒ Podman 4.9.4-rhel with Podman Compose 1.2.0 or later
• Operator/integrator:
‒ Web browser:
‒ Firefox
‒ Google Chrome
‒ Microsoft Edge
• Publishing Network ports in firewall :
In order to access the KUKA.AMR Fleet server from external net-
works, the following ports must be opened:
‒ TCP 80: The web UI publishes on this port (http). This can be set
to a different port during installation.
‒ TCP 5000: Fleet UI publishes on this port (http). This can be set
to a different port during installation.
‒ TCP 5443: Fleet UI publishes on this port (https). This can be set
to a different port during installation.
‒ TCP 443: Fleet UI publishes on this port (https), if TCP 443 is
dedicated to KUKA.AMR Fleet.
‒ TCP 7998: WCS gRPC interface. It is optional when there is no
upstream system.
‒ TCP 8081: WCS Http interface. It is optional when there is no
PDA using with KUKA.AMR Fleet.
‒ TCP 8082: Used by ECS Swagger.
‒ TCP 8883: The robot manager uses this port to expose its MQTT
communication with the fleet of robots.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 19/719
Installation

=== PDF PAGE 20 ===
‒ TCP 10870: KUKA Interface port for upstream system. It is option-
al when there is no upstream system.
‒ TCP 10873: The Diagnostic service receives the diagnostic files
from the platforms.
‒ UDP 123: Network Time Protocol Server (optional, required when
there are AMRs with KUKA.AMR OS version < 2.4).
‒ UDP 1234: Network Time Protocol Server (optional).
‒ UDP 8988: The robot control service uses this port communicated
robots running KUKA.AMR OS. It is optional when there is no ro-
bot running KUKA.AMR OS.
3.2 Setting up a Debian server
This section explains how to set up a Debian server and make it ready to
deploy a new server installation. It is a guide to reach the following goals:
• Installing a Linux Debian operating system
• Installing docker and the docker compose plugin
• Setting up specific environment settings required by KUKA.AMR Fleet
3.2.1 Installing Debian
Linux operating system is installed for a new server. The server can be a
self-hosted machine or a virtual machine.
Precondition
• Debian is used as Linux version. It can run natively or in a virtual ma-
chine.
• A user "kuka" must be set up. Its password may be chosen by the lo-
cal IT.
Procedure
1. Download Debian ISO from
https://cdimage.debian.org/debian-cd/current/amd64/iso-cd/.
2. Install server based on Debian ISO file. The following documentation
provides a step-by-step description of the installation:
https://linuxsimply.com/linux-basics/os-installation/single-os/debian/.
• During the wizard process, create a user "kuka" and set its pass-
word to "kuka".
• Set up a proxy when prompted if needed (optional).
• It's recommended to select the installation of "SSH server" when
prompted. This is useful to share files or for diagnostics.
• Check the box "Officially supported (main)" when prompted.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
20/719 | www.kuka.com KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026
Installation

=== PDF PAGE 21 ===
Fig. 3-1: Check box “Officially supported (main)”
3. After the installation, remove the live CD/ISO source from the APT
configuration. Open a terminal and type:
$ sudo sed -i '/cdrom/d' /etc/apt/sources.list
3.2.2 Setting up specific linux configurations
The settings will enable the Mobile Robotics software.
Precondition
• "/opt" directory must belong to "kuka".
• "kuka" must be part of the Linux "sudoers" group.
Adding a user to the sudoers group grants them administrative privileg-
es, which can pose security risks if the user account is compromised.
Ensure that the KUKA user account is protected by a strong password
and access is strictly limited to authorized and trusted personnel.
Procedure
1. Assign /opt to kuka:
$ su root
$ chown -R kuka:kuka /opt
2. Add “kuka” to the sudoers group:
$ su root
$ usermod -aG sudo kuka
3. Change the password of the user "kuka" if needed.
$ su root
$ passwd kuka
3.2.3 Installing Docker and Docker Compose
Docker is a required software that the KUKA.AMR Fleet extensively uses
for its microservice container architecture.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 21/719
Installation

=== PDF PAGE 22 ===
Precondition
• The docker compose plugin must be installed and can be called with
docker compose up .
Procedure
1. Install Docker and the Docker Compose plugin using the official docu-
mentation:
https://docs.docker.com/engine/install/debian/
Make sure to install the Docker Compose plugin, not (!) the stand-
alone version.
The linked documentation describes the installation of the plugin.
If you are working behind a proxy, it is recommended to use the
method Install from a package , as it does not require complex proxy
settings.
2. Start the docker service:
$ sudo service docker start
3.2.4 Setting up specific Docker configuration for "kuka"
In order to use scripts and tools, the user “kuka” must be allowed to use
Docker without “sudo” (e.g. docker ps  instead of sudo docker ps ).
Procedure
• Add “kuka” to the Linux group “docker”:
$ sudo usermod -aG docker kuka
$ newgrp docker
3.2.5 Proceeding with server installation
The Linux system is now fully prepared to host the KUKA.AMR Fleet serv-
er. Refer to ( >>> 3.5 "Installing KUKA.AMR Fleet with Docker in Debian or
Ubuntu Linux" Page 27 ) for the installation of KUKA.AMR Fleet.

3.4 Setting up a Red Hat Enterprise Linux server
This section explains how to set up a Red Hat Enterprise Linux (RHEL)
server and make it ready to deploy a new server installation. It is a guide
to reach the following goals:
• Installing a Linux Red Hat Enterprise Linux operating system
• Installing docker and the docker compose plugin
• Setting up specific environment settings required by KUKA.AMR Fleet
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
24/719 | www.kuka.com KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026

=== PDF PAGE 25 ===
3.4.1 Installing Red Hat Enterprise Linux
Linux operating system is installed for a new server. The server can be a
self-hosted machine or a virtual machine.
Precondition
• Red Hat Enterprise Linux is used as Linux version. It can run natively
or in a virtual machine.
• A user "kuka" must be set up. Its password may be chosen by the lo-
cal IT.
Procedure
1. Register a Red Hat account to create the username and password
from
https://access.redhat.com/.
2. Log in to
https://developers.redhat.com.
3. Download Red Hat Enterprise Linux ISO from
https://developers.redhat.com/products/rhel/download.
4. Install server based on Red Hat Enterprise Linux ISO file. The follow-
ing documentation provides a step-by-step description of the installa-
tion:
https://docs.redhat.com/en/documentation/red_hat_enterprise_linux/9/
html/interactively_installing _rhel_from_installation_media/index.
• During the wizard process, create a user "kuka" and set its pass-
word to "kuka".
• Set up a proxy when prompted if needed (optional).
• It's recommended to select the installation of "SSH server" when
prompted. This is useful to share files or for diagnostics.
5. After installation, open the terminal, register the RHEL subscription,
and run the following command in the terminal:
$ subscription-manager register --username <your 
USERNAME> --password <your PASSWORD> --auto-attach
After running, verify whether the system has been successfully regis-
tered.
$ subscription-manager list --installed
Fig. 3-2: Subscription information
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 25/719
Installation

=== PDF PAGE 26 ===
3.4.2 Installing Podman
Podman is a required software that KUKA.AMR Fleet extensively uses for
its microservice container architecture.
Precondition
• The docker compose plugin must be installed and can be called with
podman compose up .
Procedure
1. Update the RHEL system software by running the following command
in the terminal:
$ sudo yum update -y
2. Install podman by running the following command:
$ sudo yum install -y podman
3. Install python3 and python3-pip:
$ sudo yum install -y python3
$ sudo yum install -y python3-pip
4. Install podman-compose:
$ sudo pip3 install podman-compose
5. Check if the installation is successful:
$ podman-compose --version
Fig. 3-3: Podman-compose version
3.4.3 Setting up specific configuration for running KUKA.AMR Fleet
Procedure
1. Switch to the root user, change the owner and group of the /opt direc-
tory to "kuka", and add execution permissions to the /opt directory.
$ su root
$ chown -R kuka:kuka /opt
$ chmod -R 755 /opt
2. In order to use the scripts and tools, the user "kuka" must be allowed
to use docker without "sudo" (e.g. podman ps instead of sudo podman
ps). Add "kuka" to the Linux group "podman".
$ su root
$ groupadd podman
$ usermod -aG podman kuka
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
26/719 | www.kuka.com KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026
Installation

=== PDF PAGE 27 ===
3. Modify the container configuration file and update the configuration
content.
$ vim /etc/containers/registries.conf
4. Add the following configuration information to prevent HTTP access er-
rors during image pulling:
[[registry]]
location = "localhost:5001"
insecure = true
5. Save and exit.
6. To prevent the proxy configured on the server from affecting the net-
work access of Podman containers, it is necessary to disable the
proxy within the container. This can be achieved by editing /etc/
containers/containers.conf:
$ vim /etc/containers/containers.conf
7. Add the following content to clear the proxy environment variables:
[containers]
env = ["http_proxy=", "https_proxy=", "HTTP_PROXY=", 
"HTTPS_PROXY=", "no_proxy="]
8. Save and exit. Restart the podman service to make effect.
$ systemctl restart podman
3.4.4 Proceeding with server installation
The Linux system is now fully prepared to host the KUKA.AMR Fleet serv-
er. Refer to ( >>> 3.6 "Installing KUKA.AMR Fleet with Podman in Red Hat
Enterprise Linux" Page 29 ) for the installation of KUKA.AMR Fleet.
3.5 Installing KUKA.AMR Fleet with Docker in Debian or Ubuntu Linux
Description
KUKA.AMR Fleet is running with docker in Debian or Ubuntu Linux sys-
tem. This section explains how to install KUKA.AMR Fleet with docker and
make it ready to start application.
KUKA.AMR Fleet needs license to get fully ready. Refer to ( >>> 3.7 "Li-
censing KUKA.AMR Fleet" Page 31 ).
Precondition
Server:
• With Debian or Ubuntu Linux operating system
• With Docker engine and Docker compose
Network:
• Wireless network connecting server and robot(s)
• These TCP ports on the server need to be exposed to the net-
work: 80, 8080, 8883, 5000, 5443
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 27/719
Installation

=== PDF PAGE 28 ===
• The UDP port on the server needs to be exposed to the net-
work: 8988
• For downloading the KUKA.AMR Fleet installation package:
‒ Internet connection
Software:
• For the server: KUKA.AMR_Fleet.tgz
Downloading the KUKA.AMR Fleet installation package
1. Downloading:
• Use the link in the delivery notice of KUKA.AMR Fleet software to
download the installation package.
• Alternatively:
1. Go to my.kuka.com.
2. Search for the specific version of KUKA.AMR Fleet.
3. Download the package.
2. Unpack the package.
The package contains the TGZ file required for installation.
Installing the server software
1. Copy KUKA.AMR_Fleet.tgz to any directory on the server's file sys-
tem (e.g. to /opt/temp).
2. Extract the TGZ file by calling:
$ tar -xvf KUKA.AMR_Fleet.tgz
3. Start the installation by calling the install script:
$ ./install.sh
This will install the necessary Docker images and start the modules of
the server as Docker containers.
The script can be called with parameters, e.g.
$ WEBUI_PORT=81 ./install.sh
After the successful installation, the script terminates with the message
Installation of server successful .
All server modules should be running.
4. Optional: Verify the state of the server by calling
$ docker ps
All modules listed should show Up in the status column.
5. If the client computer in same network uses KUKA.AMR Fleet before,
open the browser and press Ctrl+F5 on the keyboard to force fresh
for removing cache of the older version.
6. Open any browser (Chrome, Edge or Firefox) and access <server
ip>:5000. Installation is successfully when the login page displays.
The server is now ready to accept registration requests for AMRs and to
receive commands for them.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
28/719 | www.kuka.com KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026
Installation

=== PDF PAGE 29 ===
Proceeding to licensing KUKA.AMR Fleet
Refer to ( >>> 3.7 "Licensing KUKA.AMR Fleet" Page 31 ) for how to li-
cense KUKA.AMR Fleet.
3.6 Installing KUKA.AMR Fleet with Podman in Red Hat Enterprise Linux
Description
KUKA.AMR Fleet is running with Podman in Red Hat Enterprise Linux
(RHEL) system. This section explains how to install KUKA.AMR Fleet with
podman and make it ready to start application.
KUKA.AMR Fleet needs license to get fully ready. Refer to ( >>> 3.7 "Li-
censing KUKA.AMR Fleet" Page 31 ).
Precondition
Server:
• With Red Hat Enterprise Linux (RHEL) system
• With Podman and Podman compose
Network:
• Wireless network connecting server and robot(s)
• These TCP ports on the server need to be exposed to the net-
work: 80, 8080, 8883, 5000, 5443
• The UDP port on the server needs to be exposed to the net-
work: 8988
• For downloading the KUKA.AMR Fleet installation package:
‒ Internet connection
Software:
• For the server: KUKA.AMR_Fleet.tgz
Downloading the KUKA.AMR Fleet installation package
1. Downloading:
• Use the link in the delivery notice of KUKA.AMR Fleet software to
download the installation package.
• Alternatively:
1. Go to my.kuka.com.
2. Search for the specific version of KUKA.AMR Fleet.
3. Download the package.
2. Unpack the package.
The package contains the TGZ file required for installation.
Installing the server software
When deploying KUKA.AMR Fleet with Podman on the Red Hat system,
root user must be used. Otherwise, licensing process will fail due to in-
sufficient permissions.
1. Switch to root user.
$ su root
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 29/719
Installation

=== PDF PAGE 30 ===
2. Copy KUKA.AMR_Fleet.tgz to any directory on the server's file sys-
tem (e.g. to /opt/temp).
3. Extract the TGZ file by calling:
$ tar -xvf KUKA.AMR_Fleet.tgz
4. Start the installation by calling the install script:
$ ./install.sh
This will install the necessary Docker images and start the modules of
the server as Docker containers.
The script can be called with parameters, e.g.
$ WEBUI_PORT=81 ./install.sh

Installation of server successful .

Podman auto-start configuration
1. Verify podman-compose installation and path:
$ which podman-compose
If the command returns no output, install podman-compose first before
proceeding (>>> 3.4.2 "Installing Podman" Page 26 ).
2. Create and edit a systemd service unit file (such as fleet-podman-com-
pose.service).
$ su root
   
$ vim /etc/systemd/system/fleet-podman-compose.service
3. Enter the following content:
[Unit]
   
Description=Podman Compose auto start fleet
   
Requires=podman.service
   
After=podman.service
   
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
30/719 | www.kuka.com KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026
Installation

=== PDF PAGE 31 ===
[Service]
   
Type=forking
   
WorkingDirectory=/opt/server
   
# Update this path according to `which podman-compose` 
output
   
ExecStart=/usr/local/bin/podman-compose -f docker-
compose.yml up -d
   
ExecStop=/usr/local/bin/podman-compose -f docker-
compose.yml down
   
TimeoutStartSec=360
   
TimeoutStopSec=120
   
Restart=on-failure
   
[Install]
   
WantedBy=multi-user.target
4. Reload Systemd configuration and enable the auto-start service.
$ systemctl daemon-reload 
   
$ systemctl enable fleet-podman-compose.service
Proceeding to licensing KUKA.AMR Fleet
Refer to ( >>> 3.7 "Licensing KUKA.AMR Fleet" Page 31 ) for how to li-
cense KUKA.AMR Fleet.
3.7 Licensing KUKA.AMR Fleet
Description
For intellectual property protection, KUKA.AMR Fleet should run with li-
cense. An 8-day trial license is provided with the initial installation of
KUKA.AMR Fleet.
Each computer or server has its own unique certificate file for authentica-
tion. *.ksi file is used in KUKA get the unique information of the computer.
A request via an email is needed to get the corresponding KUKA.AMR
Fleet license file (*.kcert) .
Then after importing the KUKA.AMR Fleet license file (*.kcert) with a pri-
vate key, KUKA.AMR Fleet is ready for use.
If KUKA.AMR Fleet is running in a virtual machine (debian or ubuntu) of
windows, get *.ksi file in a virtual machine.
If there is no license key in the delivery notice, proceed with the following
sections.
If a private key is already offered, refer to specific procedures on licensing
KUKA.AMR Fleet.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 31/719
Installation

=== PDF PAGE 32 ===
Getting ksi file
1. Find "license-generator" in KUKA.AMR_Fleet_[version].tgz
\KUKA.AMR_Fleet_[version].tar.
2. Switch to root user and input password
$ sudo su
3. Run "start.sh" to get ksi file of linux server.
$ sh start.sh
or
$ . start.sh
4. The ksi file is generated in same path.
Sending a request for licensing
Send an email for licensing to License.AMR@kuka.com. The ksi file
should be attached.

License for KUKA.AMR Fleet – [PO-Number]
Dear KUKA-Team,
Please generate the license for order below.
Find attached the *ksi-File.
License Type Official License/Trial License
KUKA Order-Number [PO-Number]
Expected Period of License unlimited
Server Info File (*ksi) Upload to the attachment
Thank you.
Best regards
Licensing KUKA.AMR Fleet
1. If KUKA.AMR Fleet is installed for the first time or has an expired li-
cense, an error occurs when logging in KUKA.AMR Fleet.
2. The response of the license request contains a private key and a
*.kcert as attachment.
Private key looks like: 2Hygm7TfByEqWk+6ZZuDfw==
3. Click Expired at the bottom of login page.
Fig. 3-4: Uploading license file
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
32/719 | www.kuka.com KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026

=== PDF PAGE 33 ===
4. Input private key and upload .kcert file.

into Perpetual, then the licensing succeeds. License information can

Fig. 3-5: System authorization
Licensing errors
There are three possible licensing errors:
• When the ERROR prompt shows the license cannot be used in this
server, please check whether the server information matches , con-
firm that the ksi file should be created in the server where the
KUKA.AMR Fleet is deployed.
Fig. 3-6: Error of mismatch server information
• When the ERROR prompt shows Incorrect private key , confirm
whether the private key is inputted incorrectly.
Fig. 3-7: Error of incorrect private key
• When nothing shows, confirm whether KUKA.AMR Fleet is starting
well by using docker ps -a  command to see if all services are run-
ning properly. When running the first time, it will cost time to start-up
KUKA.AMR Fleet. So it is recommended to wait for more than three
minutes before licensing KUKA.AMR Fleet after starting KUKA.AMR
Fleet.

3.8 Upgrading the KUKA.AMR Fleet
Precondition
• Use the command docker ps  to check whether KUKA.AMR Fleet
keeps running when backup.
• New KUKA.AMR Fleet package KUKA.AMR_Fleet.tgz is ready.
Backing up all databases and files from older version
1. Copy KUKA.AMR_Fleet.tgz to any directory on the filesystem of the
server (e.g. to /opt/temp).
2. Extract it by calling
$ tar -xvf KUKA.AMR_Fleet.tgz
3. Execute backupAll.sh to back up all necessary files and databases.
$ cd /opt/temp/backup
$ chmod +x *.sh
$ ./backupAll.sh
Then a compressed file named server-backup-<timestamp>.tgz
is generated. Example: server-backup-20250408-030108.tgz. It
is recommended to copy or export this file to another location to ar-
chive.
4. It is recommended to check the size of all files in the backup package
to make sure everything is included in the backup. The databases,
SLAM maps, certificate, config files, docker-compose.yml and .env
file are all included in the resulting server-backup-<time-
stamp>.tgz.
Installing newer KUKA.AMR Fleet
Make sure backup steps are executed and backup files are completed
before executing ./install.sh.
1. Start the installation process by calling the install script:
$ cd /opt/temp
$ ./install.sh
This will install the necessary Docker images and start the modules of
the server as Docker containers.
After the successful installation, the script terminates with the message
Installation of server successful .
All server modules should be running.
2. Optional: Verify the state of the server by calling
$ docker ps
If KUKA.AMR Fleet is running in Red Hat Enterprise Linux server, ex-
ecute:
$ podman ps
All modules listed should show Up in the status column.
3. If the client computer is in the same network and has used
KUKA.AMR Fleet before, delete the browser cache of the old version.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
34/719 | www.kuka.com KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026
Installation


=== PDF PAGE 35 ===
Open the browser and press Ctrl+F5 to force a fresh which removes
the cache.
4. Open any browser (Chrome, Edge or Firefox) and access <server
ip>:5000. Installation is successfully when the login page displays.
Restoring the databases from older version
1. Go to "/opt/server" and stop KUKA.AMR Fleet.
$ cd /opt/server
$ ./server-services.sh stop
2. Start the database only.
$ sudo docker compose up -d krcs-basic-data-db postgres
If KUKA.AMR Fleet is running in Red Hat Enterprise Linux server, ex-
ecute with root user:
$ podman compose up -d krcs-basic-data-db postgres
3. Go to the backup folder and restore the database from an older ver-
sion. Assume that the backup folder is /opt/temp/backup and the
scripts restoreAll.sh and restore.sh are located there. The
backup package is server-backup-<timestamp>.tgz, for exam-
ple: server-backup-20250408-030108.tgz. Allow overwriting the
database although database already exists.
$ cd /opt/temp/backup
$ chmod +x *.sh
$ ./restoreAll.sh server-backup-<timestamp>.tgz
4. Because the docker-compose.yml  and .env might be different in
a different version, they are not copied back. Compare the newer one
and the older one in server-backup-<timestamp>.tgz to see if
any configurations are added. Re-add the configurations into the new-
er docker-compose.yml and .env.
5. Restart KUKA.AMR Fleet.
$ cd /opt/server
$ ./server-services.sh start
6. After upgrading the license is not valid anymore. Redo the licensing
steps using the previous license. Refer to ( >>> 3.7 "Licensing
KUKA.AMR Fleet" Page 31 ).
7. Upgrading finished.
8. Go to <server-ip>:5000 and click Clear Cache  in login page be-
fore login.

3.11 Security
3.11.1 TLS certificate support
Description
KUKA.AMR Fleet supports TLS (Transport Layer Security) 1.3 version
Certificate in PEM (Privacy Enhanced Mail) format. Deploying a TLS certif-
icate ensures that all data exchanged within the fleet management system
is confidential, authenticated, and protected against unauthorized access.
Users can use https://<domain> instead of http://<server-
ip>:<server-port> to access KUKA.AMR Fleet.
This section introduces the following:
• Overview of TLS certificate and PEM format
• Enabling TLS certificate support in KUKA.AMR Fleet
• Updating TLS certificate
• Calling the KUKA-interface standard API with HTTPS
TLS certificate overview
A TLS certificate is a digital certificate used to establish a secure, encryp-
ted connection between a client and a server. It verifies the server’s iden-
tity and enables encrypted data transmission, protecting sensitive informa-
tion from interception or interference during communication.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 39/719
Installation

=== PDF PAGE 40 ===
TLS is the successor to SSL (Secure Sockets Layer) and provides en-
hanced security and improved encryption algorithms. SSL was the origi-
nal protocol for securing network communications, it is now considered
obsolete and insecure.
PEM format overview
The PEM format is a widely adopted text encoding format for SSL/TLS
certificate deployment, with the following technical characteristics:
• Format specifications:
‒ It uses Base64 ASCII encoding, with -----BEGIN CERTIFICATE-----
and -----END CERTIFICATE----- as delimiters.
‒ It supports full certificate chain inclusion (Root CA + Intermediate
CA + End Certificate).
‒ Certificate files (.crt) contain the server public key and subject in-
formation.
‒ Private key files (.key) are encapsulated using the PKCS#8 stand-
ard, supporting RSA/ECC algorithms.
• File structure example:
-----BEGIN CERTIFICATE-----
MIIDdzCCAl
+gAwIBAgIEAgAAuTANBgkqhkiG9w0BAQsFADBaMQswCQYDVQQGEwJD
-----END CERTIFICATE-----
Obtaining a TLS certificate
A TLS certificate can be obtained in the following ways:
• Apply to a trusted Certificate Authority (CA): Submit a Certificate Sign-
ing Request (CSR) to a CA, such as DigiCert, GlobalSign, or Let's En-
crypt. After approval, you will receive the certificate and private key in
PEM format.
• Obtain a TLS certificate in PEM format from the IT department or in-
ternal CA: If the company has its own internal CA or IT security de-
partment, users can request and obtain a PEM format certificate ac-
cording the user's needs.
Due to KUKA.AMR Fleet is an on-premises application running in
the internal network, it is recommended to ask the IT department to
obtain a TLS certificate in PEM format.
• Generate a self-signed certificate for testing or internal environments:
Generate a self-signed certificate using tools like OpenSSL.
Example:
openssl req -newkey rsa:2048 -nodes -keyout server.key -
x509 -days 365 -out server.crt
The generated server.crt and server.key files are the TLS certif-
icate in PEM format.
Logging in to KUKA.AMR Fleet with HTTPS
Precondition
• KUKA.AMR Fleet version 2.11 or later
• TLS certificate in PEM format is ready
• Port 443 is not occupied by other services on the same server
Procedure
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
40/719 | www.kuka.com KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026
Installation


=== PDF PAGE 41 ===
1. Create a TLS Certificate in PEM format with OpenSSL in the default
folder, as described above. Assuming that the installation path
is /opt/server.
The certificate and private key files are created:
/opt/server/cert/kmres.crt
/opt/server/cert/kmres.key
2. The default port is 5443. KUKA.AMR Fleet can be accessed in the
browser with the following URLs: https://<server-ip>:5443 or
https://<server-domain>:5443. Depending on the browser, the
following warning messages might be returned when accessing the
page:
• "Warning: Potential Security Risk Ahead" in Mozilla Firefox
Fig. 3-13: "Warning: Potential Security Risk Ahead" in Firefox
• "Your connection isn't private" in Microsoft Edge
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 41/719
Installation

=== PDF PAGE 42 ===
Fig. 3-14: "Your connection isn't private" in Edge
• "Your connection is not private" in Google Chrome
Fig. 3-15: "Your connection is not private" in Chrome
The TLS certificate created with OpenSSL might not be trusted by
browsers because it is not generated in an official way.
3. For testing, select one of the following, depending on the browser:
• In Mozilla Firefox: Advanced > Accept the Risk and Continue
• In Microsoft Edge: Advanced > Continue to <server-ip> (unsafe)
• In Google Chrome: Advanced > Proceed to <server-ip> (unsafe)

configuration can be changed in the docker-compose.yml file

home/kuka/backup.
2. Rename server.key to kmres.key, and server.crt to
kmres.crt.

the new kmres.key and kmres.crt.
Fig. 3-16: TLS certificate location

Fleet, the port configuration can be changed in the docker-com-
pose.yml file in /opt/server.
Change it from:
fleet-web:
   ...
   ports:

      - "5000:5000" # index
      - "5443:5443" # https
   ...
to
fleet-web:
   ...
   ports:
      - "5000:5000" # index
      - "443:5443" # https

4 Operation
4.1 Logging in and out
Description
Users can log in to the KUKA.AMR Fleet with corresponding accounts.
Access is granted only after a successful login. The login page also sup-
ports uploading and verifying a license key. Even with correct credentials,
access is denied if the license has expired. Additionally, KUKA.AMR Fleet
will automatically return to the login page after a period of inactivity, ex-
cept on some editing pages.
Logging in
1. Enter the login address in the browser (http://:5000/). The browser dis-
plays the login page.
2. If the system is officially licensed or in trial mode, enter the username
and password, then click Sign in  to verify the credentials. If correct,
access is granted to KUKA.AMR Fleet.
The default admin username/password combination is admin/
Admin@001.
3. Checking Remember Me  will auto-fill the username and password on
future visits.
Fig. 4-1: Login page
Logging out
To log out, move the mouse to the user icon  at the top-right corner of the
page and click the Log Out  button to return to the login page.
Fig. 4-2: Log out button
4.2 Switching between Chinese and English
Click the Language (globe icon) to switch the language in real-time. Cur-
rently, only simplified Chinese and English are supported.

4.3.9 Configuration export and import
Description
The import and export item can be set under System > Misc > Configu-
ration item import and export .
Configuration Import and Export is applicable to RCS, WCS, and ECS si-
multaneously.
The RCS export function cannot export layout files and base map files.
Only files exported from the same version can be imported.
Fig. 4-29: Interface of configuration import and export
After exporting, do not modify the file name. Otherwise, KUKA.AMR
Fleet will not recognize which system the import file belongs to during
import.
During import, KUKA.AMR Fleet will validate based on the configuration
name. If the configuration has the same name, it will be overwritten.
KUKA.AMR Fleet will also validate the version number of the import file
and the current system version. If they do not match, the import will fail.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 57/719
Operation

=== PDF PAGE 58 ===

Description
RCS only provides the option to export all configurations.

All RCS configurations include all no-entry areas, charging strategies, box
shelves(models + slots), buildings, obstacle avoidance plan, containers

mobile box shelves, and robots (mainly manually added third-party robots).

automatically downloaded.

be imported.

4.3.12 Tandem Robot
Description
Tandem Robot is used to arrange dual robots of the same model as a
fleet to complete the same mission together.
Tandem Robot is applicable to certain lift robots. For availability, please
contact KUKA.
Precondition
• The robot model supports the Tandem Robot function.
• Two robots of the same available types are brought online to
KUKA.AMR Fleet and entered into the corresponding layout.
Adding a tandem area
1. Navigate to Scheduling > Layout > Area > New Area  to add a tan-
dem area.
2. Set up the Master Robot Stay Node  and Slave Robot Stay Node .
The two robots will be directed to these nodes before teaming up.
3. Click Confirm to save the area and ensure it's enabled in the area
list.
Robot tandem
1. Navigate to System > Tandem > Robot Tandem to configure the ro-
bot tandem.
2. Fill in the required fields on the robot tandem.
• Area Selection: Select the already created and enabled tandem
area.
• Master Robot (Orange): Select an on-site robot of the appropriate
type, which will proceed to the Master Robot Stay Node in the tan-
dem area.
• Slave Robot (Blue): Select the same robot type as the master ro-
bot, which will proceed to the Slave Robot Stay Node in the tan-
dem area.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 69/719
Operation

=== PDF PAGE 70 ===
• Tandem Robot ID: Enter the ID of the robots after teaming. By de-
fault, it's composed of the master and slave robot IDs. It must be
less than 2147483647. And it cannot be set as the robot ID that
already exists KUKA.AMR Fleet.
• Tandem Robot Pose: Select Train or Side-by-Side (SBS). The
pose represents the relative positions of the two robots.
‒ Train: One robot is located in front of or behind the other robot.
‒ Side-by-Side: One robot is located to the left or right of the
other robot.
• Orientation: Select the robot orientation after teaming. The optional
settings for orientation are determined by the relative positions of
the Master Robot Stay Node and the Slave Robot Stay Node in
the tandem area and the tandem robot pose.
• Gap (m): Select the distance between the edges of two robots.
The gap of a specific robot type in KUKA.AMR Fleet is unique. If
two robots of a specific type have already teamed with a specific
tandem robot pose in KUKA.AMR Fleet, the gap cannot be
changed when forming a fleet with the same type of robots. To
modify the gap, delete the teamed robot type first.
• Mode: Select the master robot to be in the back/right or front/left
of the salve robot. The mode is determined by the tandem robot
pose and orientation.
• Type After Tandem: The robot type after tandem is determined by
the robot type of the master-slave robots and the tandem robot
pose.
• Size After Tandem (m): The dimensions of the robots after tandem
is calculated based on the length and width of the master-slave ro-
bots, the tandem robot pose, the orientation, and the gap.
‒ Side-by-Side:
‒ Robot length after teaming: The length of the master/slave
robot
‒ Robot width after teaming: Twice the width of the master/
slave robot plus the gap
‒ Train:
‒ Robot length after teaming: Twice the length of the master/
slave robot plus the gap
‒ Robot width after teaming: The length of the master/slave
robot
Fig. 4-59: Robot tandem configuration
3. Click Move mission sent  to move the master robot to the Master Ro-
bot Stay Node and the slave robot to the Slave Robot Stay Node re-
spectively.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
70/719 | www.kuka.com KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026
Operation

=== PDF PAGE 71 ===
Fig. 4-60: Move mission before tandem
4. Click Confirm Tandem after the master and slave robots move to
their nodes. The two robots will adjust, team up, and be removed from
the layout. Then, the robots after tandem automatically go online and
enter the layout.
Clicking Confirm Tandem closes the Robot Tandem pop-up window.
Fig. 4-61: Confirming the tandem
Fig. 4-62: formFormation mission finished
The tandem robot can be moved areas outside the tandem area in
the map monitoring page. Within the tandem area, the tandem robot
doesn't receive missions from the upstream system.
Disbanding
1. Move the robots to an enabled tandem area.
2. Select the robot ID, click Dissolve to send the dissolveFormation mis-
sion to the tandem robot. The robot is automatically removed from the
layout, and the master and slave robots automatically go online and
enter the layout.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 71/719
Operation

=== PDF PAGE 72 ===
Fig. 4-63: Disbanding the tandem robot
Mission configuration for the tandem robot
The mission configuration for the tandem robot is generally consistent with
the configuration of other lift robots.
Special attention is needed for the obstacle avoidance plan. The master-
slave robot configuration takes effect after tandem and can only use the
obstacle avoidance plans specific to the master-slave robots.
Fig. 4-64: Obstacle detection plan
For example, when configuring the custom obstacle plan for a robot after
tandem on a specific edge, an obstacle avoidance plan using the master-
slave robot type as the Robot Type in the Obstacle Avoidance settings
should be created first. When editing the layout edge, select the master-
slave robot type and select one of the obstacle avoidance plans for this
robot type.

4.3.13 Fire management
Description
Fire management is used to manage fire-related configuration items (such
as evacuation zones) and trigger fire alarm functions.
Fire alarm
When a fire alarm is triggered, KUKA.AMR Fleet pauses all AMR mis-
sions, and the AMRs stay outside the evacuation zone. Once the site is
restored, the alarm should be canceled in KUKA.AMR Fleet, and the
AMRs will automatically resume their regular missions.
There are two types of fire alarm:
• Automatic alarm: By default, KUKA.AMR Fleet automatically triggers
the fire alarm through device signals. For related alarm device config-
urations, refer to the relevant sections of the ECS.
• Manual alarm: There are two ways to trigger the fire alarm manually.
‒ Method 1: Click the fire alarm icon at the top-right of KUKA.AMR
Fleet and select the alarm district.
Fig. 4-66: Fire alarm icon
‒ Method 2: Navigate to System > Fire > Manual Alarm.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 73/719
Operation

=== PDF PAGE 74 ===
Fig. 4-67: Manual alarm entry
Fig. 4-68: Manual alarm interface
• Cancel alarm: There are two ways to cancel the fire alarm.
‒ Method 1: Find the fire alarm icon at the top-right of KUKA.AMR
Fleet and select the current alarming district.
‒ Method 2: Navigate to System > Fire > Cancel Alarm.
Fig. 4-69: Cancel alarm interface
• Emergency condition record: The history of fire alarms can be viewed.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
74/719 | www.kuka.com KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026
Operation

=== PDF PAGE 75 ===
Fig. 4-70: Emergency condition interface
Evacuation zone
The evacuation zone is a passage for quick and safe evacuation during a
fire and for firefighters to rescue. It automatically opens when a fire alarm
is triggered. AMRs are not allowed to enter, and any robots inside will be
moved to the outer edge.
• Adding an evacuation zone:
1. Navigate to System > Fire > New Area  to add a new area.
2. Select the target map and click Confirm to enter the area configu-
ration page.
3. Fill in the required fields.
Fig. 4-71: Adding an evacuation zone
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 75/719
Operation

=== PDF PAGE 76 ===
4. Click Confirm to add a new area. The evacuation zone is disabled
by default.
• Enabling/Disabling the evacuation zone: Evacuation zones automatical-
ly open when a fire alarm is triggered and close when the alarm is
canceled, usually without manual handling. Click Enable or Disable to
open or close the evacuation zone.
Fig. 4-72: Evacuation zone list

Main differences between Editor 2.0 and Editor 1.0
• Uses a new graphics rendering engine to replace the original render-
ing solution and optimizes memory usage. Significantly improves ren-
dering performance, especially when handling large maps.
• Enhanced interaction design, supporting smoother zoom and pan op-
erations, streamlining editing workflows.
• Supports data persistence during editing, implementing local version
control.
• Enhanced data search capabilities and optimized visualization.
New features in Editor 2.0
• Property editor: After multi-selection, open a tabular property editor to
directly edit basic information of nodes and edges.
Fig. 4-76: Property editor
• Display tools: To check whether nodes and edges have special config-
urations, and whether edges have pre or post device interactions.

4.4 Dashboard
4.4.1 Job board
Description
Job board is used to display jobs executed in the last half hour and moni-
tor the job status. Jobs are instances of workflows. The job board pro-
vides information such as job ID, number, name, job source, robot id, start
node, target node, external(from), external(target), container code, opera-
tor, create time, end time, execution status.
It can be found under Dashboard > Jobboard.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 79/719
Operation

=== PDF PAGE 80 ===
Fig. 4-81: Job board interface
Fig. 4-82: Job list
4.4.1.1 Viewing jobs
Description
Clicking a single job displays:
• The execution status of each node in the workflow
• The workflow configuration of the job
• The workflow configuration log
• The action execution status of each node
Ongoing jobs can also be canceled. The job board archives every half
hour, with archived statuses including completed, canceled, and startup
exceptions. Other statuses remain displayed on the job board.
Job Detail
Job details include the job ID, robot type executing the workflow, job type
(internally triggered workflows default to "-"; external API calls can custom-
ize the job type), robot ID executing the workflow, material name and ID
involved, container code, start and target nodes, workflow creation time,
workflow priority, and the creator of the workflow.

the workflow configuration.

Handling methods
There are four handling methods, including:
• Business-related handling methods correspond to errors such as Emp-
ty Box Job and Second Repositioning:
‒ Manually handled, request KUKA.AMR Fleet to retry the task.
‒ Manual adjustments have been made on-site, such as adjust-
ing the container placement, requiring to retry the task.
‒ Manually handled, request KUKA.AMR Fleet to forcibly complete
the task, and the AMR continues to work.
‒ Manual adjustments have been made on-site, requiring to forci-
bly complete the task, setting the AMR to an idle state, waiting
for the next task.
• Non-business-related handling methods are used for hardware failures
or network issues:
‒ Error cause to be determined, manually moved the AMR, request
task retry.
‒ When the AMR reports a non-business-related error, personnel
need to move the AMR to another area for troubleshooting. Af-
ter resolving the error, select this method to retry the task.
‒ Error cause to be determined, manually moved the AMR and han-
dled the container, forcibly complete the task.
‒ When the AMR reports a non-business-related error, personnel
need to move the AMR to another area for troubleshooting.
Select this method to forcibly complete the task.
Related operation
By default, related operations are unchecked. If checked, a node can be
selected by entering the node number in the text box or using the node
selector.
During manual handling, the AMR can be manually moved to the corre-
sponding node, and the AMR location can be updated. The related opera-
tions differ based on whether the AMR is in a lifting or non-lifting state:
• If the AMR is in a lifting state, the operations can either keep it in the
lifting state or directly lower the shelf.
Fig. 4-91: Updating robot location and lifting status
When a task is suspended or in execution (unresponsive for a long time),
manual intervention can forcibly end the task. "Manual Handle" can be
marked in RCS, and the workflow can be canceled on the task board.
"Manual Handle" is only displayed in the WCS module.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 87/719

4.4.3 Dashboard
Description
Dashboard is used to display operational data, jobs, AMRs, and other re-
lated information.
Precondition
• The layout is enabled.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
92/719 | www.kuka.com KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026
Operation

=== PDF PAGE 93 ===
• The enabled layout contains at least one district.
Scenario
• Multi-terminal usage scenario: Supporting access only on PC brows-
ers.
• Multi-district layout: One district corresponds to one dashboard.
• Multiple clients displaying the dashboard: Each independent tab
opened in the browser is considered an independent connection to
KUKA.AMR Fleet, independently exchanging data.
Opening multiple independent tabs of the dashboard in the browser will
significantly occupy the I/O resources of the server. Carefully set and
open the maximum number of dashboards.
Basic operations
• Click the + button to create a new dashboard.
• Click the Configure Cards  button to enter a new page for dashboard
configuration.
• Click the Open button to open the dashboard in a new page.
• Click the Delete button to delete the dashboard.
• In the dashboard list, click the editing icon for the name and layout -
district. After editing, click ✓ to save.
• Click the Setting button to set the Traffic Waiting Time and Obstacle
Time.
• Enter the search conditions and click Search to query, or Reset to
clear the conditions.

Card operations
Three types of cards are provided:
• General cards can be configured with general functions by combining
different options. ( >>> 4.4.3.1 "General cards" Page 96 )
• Each business compound card represents a fixed compound business
and can be directly configured with fixed functions. ( >>> 4.4.3.2 "Busi-
ness compound cards" Page 101 )
• Custom cards are non-standard version custom content and are not
recommended for use.
Basic card operations include:
• Selecting a card: Select a card from the left card group, and drag it to
the right canvas. A card of that type will be created on the canvas
based on its built-in size. The first creation will prompt a window to
configure specific parameters.
• Editing card parameters: Click the edit icon to open a window for con-
figuring specific parameters.
• Changing the card size: Click the resize icon to adjust the card size
within its maximum and minimum limits.
• Deleting a card: Click the cross icon to remove the card.
• Moving a card: Drag the card by its border when the cursor changes
to a move cursor.

4.5 Layout monitor 1.0
Description
The layout monitor is used to monitor the current status, location, tasks,
and other related information of AMRs.
Selecting Monitor automatically jumps to the most recently opened layout.
Layout Monitor 1.0 is retained for compatibility with older versions and can
be used simultaneously with Layout Monitor 2.0. However, Layout Monitor
2.0 is recommended.
Fig. 4-149: Entering layout monitor
On the layout monitor page, the layout can be zoomed in and out by
scrolling the mouse wheel. Double-clicking the canvas fits the layout to
the screen.
The layout monitor page includes three parts:
• Operation buttons  (left): Floor, Legend, Commands, Tasks, Virtual
Buttons, Exit ( >>> 4.5.1 "Operation buttons" Page 109 )
• Real-time layout  (center): Displaying the layout floor, district alias,
node ID (optional), and real-time layout
• Parameters (right): Displaying relevant parameters and information of
the selected AMR, container, node, or edge. Objects can be searched
and located on the layout by Robot ID, container code, node code(last
node), or node label(last node). ( >>> 4.6 "Layout monitor 2.0"
Page 118)

Robot status Description
Offline The robot is offline. It may lose connection or be in
manual/debug mode.
Idle The robot has no assigned mission and no exception.
Executing The robot is executing a mission.
Charging The robot is in a charging state as reported by the
robot.
Updating The robot is updating.
Waiting The robot is idle with a mission or job. It is either
waiting for another robot or waiting for another mis-
sion or job.
Exception (mid
level)
The robot has a self-recoverable mission exception.
Exception (ad-
vanced level)
The robot has a fatal mission exception or an ad-
vanced level robot exception, which needs to be re-
covered manually.
OBST The robot has obstacles on its path.

Five supported AMR operations include:
• Move
• Charge
• Cancel Charge
• Carry
• Single Command

AMR

and related operations for the task (debug mode, manual handle)
‒ Submission Info: Displays all sub-tasks of the current task,
updates the execution status of sub-tasks in real-time, and allows
viewing dependencies
• Run Info: Displays the real-time command ID and command status of
the current robot, usually showing important robot exception informa-
tion
• Robot Status Info: Basic information, speed information, component
status. All are parameters reported by the robot itself. The running
time is the time the robot has been running since the last startup
Container
• Basic Information: Container status, Number, Model Code, Container
type, Load/No load, position (X, Y), occupancy status, Direction, stay

‒ Angle Accuracy Info: Angle offset, Container stop Angle, AMR stop

=== PDF PAGE 123 ===
Legend
Fig. 4-179: Legend
Robot status Description
Offline The robot is offline. It may lose connection or be in
manual/debug mode.
Idle The robot has no assigned mission and no exception.
Executing The robot is executing a mission.
Charging The robot is in a charging state as reported by the
robot.
Updating The robot is updating.
Waiting The robot is idle with a mission or job. It is either
waiting for another robot or waiting for another mis-
sion or job.
Exception (mid
level)
The robot has a self-recoverable mission exception.
Exception (ad-
vanced level)
The robot has a fatal mission exception or an ad-
vanced level robot exception, which needs to be re-
covered manually.
OBST The robot has obstacles on its path.

4.7 Interface platform

• Manage plugins: Display plugins, import new plugins, and enable/disa-
ble/delete plugins.
• Update the parameter configuration of existing plugins.
• Display detailed historical records of third-party API calls.

up message will appear for a confirmation before the deletion is exe-

the deletion.

proceeding with the deletion.

4.8 PDA Android client application
Description
A PDA is a handheld mobile device, typically running the Android operat-
ing system, primarily used by end users such as factory line workers.
Supported PDA applications include:
• Basic functions are pre-set and include calling shelves, free carry, un-
binding and binding, empty/full queries, scan triggers, and empty/full
configuration.
• Workflow functions are linked to the PDA configuration
(>>> 5.15 "PDA configurations" Page 605 ) and can display custom
workflows in the Android client application.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
136/719 | www.kuka.com KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026
Operation

=== PDF PAGE 137 ===
The Android client application is a simple Android app installed on PDA
handheld devices. The main operators are end users, such as factory line
workers. Supported functions include:
• Scanning to insert/remove shelves
• Triggering the start workflow
• Releasing workflows
• Querying and configuring shelf status
Precondition
• The WCS HTTP port 8081 is open.
• The PDA runs Android OS version 9.0 or above.
• The AMR is connected to the same local area network as KUKA.AMR
Fleet.
• User management should include both admin and operator users for
PDA usage.
Procedure
1. Configure the server IP.
2. Log in to the app as the admin user.
3. Set the basic and workflow buttons to be displayed on the homepage.
4. Refer to ( >>> 5.15 "PDA configurations" Page 605 ) for using workflow
functions.
5. Log in to the app as a non-admin user to use its functions.
4.8.1 Basic operations
Server IP
Upon first login or after upgrading the version, click server IP configura-
tion to set the server IP.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 137/719

5 Programming and configuration
5.1 Layout management
Description
Layout management is used to manage and maintain layout information,
essential for all workflow tasks and linked with all modules.
Click Layout to expand the Layout Management menu by default and
open the District Management  interface.
Under layout management, five modules can be found:
• District Management ( >>> 5.1.3 "District management" Page 166 )
• Area Management ( >>> 5.1.5 "Area management" Page 210 )
• Building Layout Management ( >>> 5.1.1 "Building layout management"
Page 160)
• Map Management ( >>> 5.1.2 "Map management" Page 165 )
• QR Code Management ( >>> 5.1.6 "QR code management" Page 228 )
Fig. 5-1: Layout management overview
Layout information
In KUKA.AMR Fleet, layout information is organized into four levels:
• Area
• District
• Layout
• Building
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 159/719
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 160 ===
Areas are created based on districts, districts are created based on lay-
outs, and layouts are created based on buildings.
Information Description
Area One or more regions within a district with different
business attributes, such as restricted areas, dedica-
ted vehicle areas, controlled areas, storage areas,
etc.
The maintenance of areas can also be used only as
background information for display and marking in the
layout monitor, providing different background colors,
font sizes, and other style settings.
District A complete AMR operating range within a layout, like
a certain floor within a building.
Layout Buildings within a plant, containing multiple districts.
Building Used only in large integration projects to distinguish
different plants or geographical locations.
5.1.1 Building layout management
Description
Building layout management is used to manage building and layout-level
information on site, enabling unified management of usable plant and floor
data in KUKA.AMR Fleet.
Basic functions include: querying, add buildings, importing layout files, ed-
iting, deleting, and other specific functions.
Basic information includes: layout code, layout name, building code, re-
marks, status and related operation buttons.
Related operations for layouts: viewing layout details, adding layouts, im-
porting layout files, querying, adding districts, editing, exporting, enabling,
disabling and deleting.
Fig. 5-2: Building layout management function overview
Adding a building
1. Click New Building  and enter the information in the pop-up window.
2. After filling in required fields including building address, building code,
and building name, click Confirm to save.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
160/719 | www.kuka.com KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026

• building address

• Remarks

ration, device management, and other settings.
Buildings with enabled layouts cannot be deleted.

Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 162 ===
Fig. 5-5: Deleting a building
Importing layout files
KUKA.AMR Fleet supports importing layout files in *.json format. It is used
for
• importing layout backups
• copying layouts to other servers
• copying layouts to the current server for backup or other purposes
The following preconditions should be met to import layout files:
• The layout file format is JSON, and the parameters in the file comply
with the Fleet export file format.
• A layout with the same mapCode as the layout file *.json has been
created.
1. Choose how to handle duplicate data:
• Prompt import failure: Strictly check for duplicate data (external co-
des, etc.). If duplicates are found, the import will fail. Then re-up-
load the layout file.
• Automatically clear duplicates: Automatically clear empty duplicate
data in the file. For example, if an external code is duplicated, the
external code information of the node will be cleared, but other in-
formation of the node will be uploaded successfully.
2. Click Import Layout File , and a local file explorer will pop up.
3. Select the corresponding [mapfloor] json or [floor] json file.
• mapfloor.json imports the entire layout including all districts.
• floor.json imports the layout containing only a specific district.
4. Click Open to import.
Fig. 5-6: Local file explorer
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
162/719 | www.kuka.com KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 163 ===
Fig. 5-7: Importing layout
Adding a layout
1. Click New Layout.
2. Fill in the layout information in the popup, where building name, layout
code, and layout name are required fields.
The layout code cannot be default and cannot contain -.
3. Click Confirm to save. The newly added layout defaults to the "Disa-
bled" state.
Fig. 5-8: Adding a layout
Fig. 5-9: Required fields for a new layout

  to modify the layout name and remark of a layout.

Querying a layout
The query function supports filtering queries with multiple conditions.

• Layout code
• Layout name
• Building name
1. Click Query to search.

Deleting a layout
1. Click Delete on the layout.
2. Select whether to delete in the confirmation box. After deletion, related
districts of the layout will also be deleted.
Fig. 5-12: Deleting a layout
Export a layout
1. Click Export on the layout, and click Confirm in the popup to quickly
generate JSON format files related to the layout.
2. The browser will automatically download a file named map_xxxx.json
to the local. This file contains all district information and layout codes
of the layout. It can support the later use of the "Import Layout File"
function to quickly create layouts.
Fig. 5-13: Exporting a layout
Enabling/Disabling layout
The AMRs can only insert and execute missions in an enabled layout. On-
ly one layout can be enabled in a building at a time.
The following conditions should be met to enable or disable a layout:
• All configurations, including layouts, AMRs, containers, workflows and
equipment, are completed and running normally.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
164/719 | www.kuka.com KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 165 ===
• Users can operate and monitor layouts, AMRs, containers, workflows,
and equipment.
• To enable a layout:
‒ The layout must have at least nodes and edges.
‒ No other enabled layout is in the same building.
• To disable a layout:
‒ No AMR in the layout is occupied with tasks.
1. Click Enable/Disable to switch the layout's status information. It can
be checked in the Status.
When the layout status is enabled, districts cannot be added or edi-
ted, and the layout cannot be deleted. The enabled layout can only
be exported and disabled.
Fig. 5-14: Enabling/diabling layout
5.1.2 Map management
Description
Map management page is used to manage and maintain map information,
providing reference for AMR positioning and node edge drawing in the lay-
out.
Basic functions include querying, uploading, refreshing, downloading, view-
ing and deleting maps.
Map management is functionally linked with district management. To use a
map for district matching and layout drawing, it must first be maintained in
Map management.
Fig. 5-15: Map management
Uploading a map
Click Upload Map  to upload the map to KUKA.AMR Fleet.
Map uploading format only supports .png, .omap2, .omap3, .pgm.

5.1.3 District management
Description
District management is used for:
• Maintaining the operating range of all AMRs, including SLAM maps,
business maps, and topological maps
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
166/719 | www.kuka.com KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 167 ===
• Editing the topological map, including drawing and editing paths and
nodes
• Displaying the main background information in the layout monitor
Basic functions include adding, deleting, deleting, editing and querying a
district, and exporting QR code map data for printing.
Precondition
• At least one building and layout are created.
Adding a district
1. Click New District  on the layout and fill in the district information,
where district number, floor, version number, width, and length are re-
quired fields.
Fig. 5-21: Adding a district
2. Click Confirm to save.
A new district can be added only in a disabled layout.
Fig. 5-22: New district information
Editing a graph
Edit Graph is used to create nodes, edges, and configurations for a dis-
trict on a layout.
1. Click Edit Graph  to enter the layout editor. Be it a disabled or enabled
layout, it can be edited.
If enabled, changes are applied immediately upon clicking Save, which
may cause ongoing tasks to replan and running AMRs to pause brief-
ly.
Because graph editing in an enabled layout may cause ongoing tasks to
be automatically replanned and running robots to pause briefly, operate
the AMRs with caution in a production environment

in use, changes can be saved as drafts in the form of archives ( Graph
Editor > Draft Archive). When the layout status is changed to disabled,
click Load Draft, select the archive, click Apply and the previously
changed content will be updated to the layout.

thumbnail of the changed layout; click View and a new page will display a
thumbnail of the changed layout.
Rename: Click to enter edit mode in the Name column, edit, and click on
the right side of the text box to rename; click again to cancel renaming.
Delete: Click ✓ to select whether to delete the draft.

KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 189/719
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 190 ===
Fig. 5-60: Distance deviation
1 The AMR reaches the node accurately.
2 The AMR reaches the node inaccurately.
When the AMR reaches the node accurately:
• Node coordinates(X,Y): 1,1
• AMR coordinates(X,Y): 1,1
When the AMR reaches the node inaccurately:
• Node coordinates(X,Y): 1,1
• AMR coordinates(X,Y): 1.15,1
• Distance deviation range: 0.1
• Maximum recovery distance: 0.2
If the stopping error of the AMR is less than the maximum recovery dis-
tance and the distance deviation range, the AMR is considered to have
reached the node accurately, and subsequent actions will be executed
normally.
If the stopping error of the AMR is greater than the distance deviation
range but less than the maximum recovery distance, a small recovery ac-
tion will be generated.
If the stopping error of the AMR is greater than the maximum recovery
distance, the task will be suspended due to an exception, and an error
message will be reported: Unable to recover/Can not recover.

190/719 | www.kuka.com KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 191 ===
Functional properties
Property Description
Workstation
node
Function: Node for increasing AMR traffic for task execution.
Auto Follow: Used in goods-to-person scenarios
Precondition:
• AMR queuing distance is reduced to within 20 cm by default (determined
by obstacle avoidance plan settings).
• There must be at least one queuing node (i.e., related node).
• The AMR model supports Auto Follow to add related nodes.
Operation:
• Effect: Click the icon to add a related node form.
• Enabling Auto Follow: The movement distance between two AMRs is
based on the set sensor obstacle avoidance plan distance.
Click Select Relative Node  on the related node form and the Select up-
dates to Selecting.
• Disabling Auto Follow: The movement of two AMRs is determined by the
dependency relationship caused by collision calculation.
Click the pre-related node on the canvas to select it. The node number
will be displayed on the form.
• Related node settings: When there are multiple related nodes, after select-
ing one, it will automatically move to the next related node. The user can
continue clicking the pre-related nodes on the canvas.
Multiple associations can be set based on the distance from the queuing
node to the workstation (near to far) and listed sequentially in the related
node settings column (top to bottom). To delete a related node, click the
trash can icon on the related node form.
When an AMR is working at a workstation node, other AMRs will queue
at the queuing node, waiting for the AMR in front to finish.

5.1.5 Area management
Description
Area management is used to manage and maintain area information for
unified on-site business management.
Areas can be used for display to mark business information and find rele-
vant information quickly, or for special purposes.
Basic functions include: querying, adding, disabling, editing, and deleting
areas.
Area usage
Supported area usages include No-Push Target, No-Push, No Push On-
to Path, Robot Zone, Forbidden Area, Robot Limitation, Transit Zone
Traffic Limit, Tandem Area and Workflow Area.
If Forbidden Area is selected, other strategies cannot be selected, and
vice versa.
Area usage Description
No-Push Target Used for path planning of idle moves to prevent entry
into this area.
No-Push A robot located in the area will not be pushed away.
No Push Onto
Path
If a robot is pushed from this area, the push target
cannot be on paths of other robots. The robot can
still pass through paths of other robots.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
210/719 | www.kuka.com KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026
Programming and configuration


=== PDF PAGE 211 ===
Area usage Description
Robot Zone Resource scheduling in this area will follow special
strategy constraints. It can be used to select parking
nodes, charging nodes, and robot resources.
Forbidden Area Robots are prohibited from entering this area.
Supports configuring various strategies, such as ef-
fective time periods, entry permissions, cycles, and
other detailed parameters.
Workflow Area A collection of nodes used for workflow configuration
or specified by upstream systems.
Robot Limitation Only a certain number of robots can enter this area
to execute business-related missions (excluding park-
ing and charging missions).
When the capacity is exceeded, missions will enter a
queued state.
Transit Zone
Traffic Limit
Only a limited number of robots can enter this real-
time restricted area for flow control purposes.
When the capacity is exceeded, the robot executing
the mission will wait outside the area.
Tandem Area Used for teaming up and disbanding a formation.
Workflow Area A collection of nodes used for workflow config or
specified by upstream systems.

KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 215/719
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 216 ===
• Rest Area Selection :
‒ Limit: When the robot is in this area and an automatic rest task is
generated, it will only choose rest nodes within this area. If no
parking nodes are available, the task will queue.
‒ No Limit: The robot will choose all available parking nodes in the
layout.
• Charging Area Selection :
‒ Limit: When the robot is in this area and an automatic charging
task is generated, it will only select charging nodes within this
area. If no charging nodes are available, the task will queue.
‒ No Limit: The robot will choose all available charging nodes in the
layout.
• Target Area Selection :
‒ Limit: When a task is issued without specifying a robot and the tar-
get node is within this area, the area's strategy will be used to se-
lect the robot. There are four strategies:
‒ Idle first in area: The task will first try to select an idle robot
within this area. If no robot is available, the task will queue.
The robot executing a parking mission or an auto charging
mission is unable to be interrupted in this type of area.
‒ Distance first in area: The task will try to select a robot within
this area, expanding the selection range incrementally until it
covers the entire area. "Expansion Radius" is the additional ra-
dius added each time, in meters. "Expansion Interval" is the
time between expansions, in seconds. If no robots are availa-
ble, the task will queue. The robot executing a parking mission
or an auto charging mission is able to be interrupted in this
type of area.
‒ Idle first in layout: The task will first try to select an idle robot
within the area. After a set number of attempts ("Attempt
Count"), if no robot is available, it will try to select an idle robot
from the entire layout. If no robots are available initially or
later, the task will queue. The robot executing a parking mis-
sion or an auto charging mission is able to be interrupted in
this type of area.
‒ Distance first in layout: The task will try to select a robot within
the area, expanding the selection range incrementally until it
covers the entire area. If no robot is available after a set num-
ber of attempts ("Attempt Count"), it will try to select an idle ro-
bot from the entire layout. "Expansion Radius" is the additional
radius added each time, in meters. "Expansion Interval" is the
time between expansions, in seconds. If no robots are availa-
ble initially or later, the task will queue. The robot executing a
parking mission or an auto charging mission is able to be inter-
rupted in this type of area.
‒ No Limit: Any available robot in the layout will be selected.

No-Entry Strat-
egy
If unchecked, the default strategy is used. Hover over

• Default Strategy: Robots or robots about to enter
the area (within 5 straight path nodes of the area)
are not allowed to enter.
• Checked Strategy: Robots can enter the area but
cannot move. Tasks are paused, and no new
tasks are received, except for rest and charging
tasks, which will be in a no-path state.

Transit zone traffic limit area
The Traffic threshold refers to the maximum number of robots allowed in
a specific area. Once the number of robots in the area reaches this limit,
any additional robots attempting to enter will stop and wait outside until
another robot exits, at which point the waiting robots will be permitted to
enter.

Robot limitation and Transit zone traffic limit comparison
A single area cannot simultaneously enforce both Robot Limitation  and
Transit Zone Traffic Limit . Unlike Robot Limitation, the Transit Zone
Traffic Limit focuses on controlling robot flow in a small section of a path
to prevent congestion. For a detailed comparison between Robot Limita-
tion and the Transit Zone Traffic Limit , refer to the comparison table be-
low.

Example scenario: Multi-level robot traffic control for hospital logistics system
Scenario Description
The multi-level control strategy effectively balances efficiency and safety,
preventing congestion, deadlocks, and resource conflicts in hospital logis-
tics systems.
Hospital logistics operations span multiple functional zones, each requiring
different flow control:
• Pharmacy workstations: Robot Limitation
• Corridors: Transit Zone Traffic Limit
• Elevator lobbies: Transit Zone Traffic Limit
• Ward areas: Robot Limitation
In this example, the above mentioned two area purposes fulfill the follow-
ing tasks:
• Robot Limitation: Controls robot density in work zones (e.g. pharma-
cies, wards).
• Transit Zone Traffic Limit : Ensures smooth traffic flow (e.g. corridors,
elevator lobbies).
Detailed control rules

Tandem area
1. Select an area on the map.
2. Fill in the Area Name, and select the Tandem Area checkbox in Area
Purpose.
3. Choose the Master Robot Stay Node  and Slave Robot Stay Node
from the drop-down lists.
4. Click Confirm to generate a Tandem Area.
The Master Robot Stay Node  represents the stop node of the main robot
before team formation, and the Slave Robot Stay Node  represents the
stop node of the slave robot before team formation.
A node must be reserved between the Master Robot Stay Node  and the
Slave Robot Stay Node  for the robots to stay after the team is formed.
