Workflow area
Fig. 5-85: Workflow Area
Area Types
• container storage area : An ordinary area that can be used in work-
flow design. It can be composed of any node type, and is normally
composed of nodes with Node Type being "Rack Node."
• temporary storage area : The default carry strategy should be "first in
first out." The temporary storage area is used with the action strategy
of the workflow node's exception handling strategy, "queue up at tem-
porary storage area." When using this strategy, if the target node is
occupied, the robot will go to the temporary storage area to wait. Dur-
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 223/719
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 224 ===
ing the wait, the robot can be set to keep the container lifted or low-
ered.
• dense storage area : Composed entirely of container nodes, used only
for storing containers. The nodes must be adjacent to each other. The
schematic diagram is as follows. Ensure that any node in the area can
transport goods out without encountering obstacles. Therefore, it
needs to be used with the buffer area to temporarily store containers
considered as "obstacles." The default handling strategy for the
"Dense Storage Area" is "first in first out."
• buffer area: Used with the dense storage area to temporarily store
obstacles from the dense storage area (obstacles refer to containers
that block the robot's path when picking up goods in the dense stor-
age area). After the original pick-up task is completed, the containers
in the buffer area will be returned to the dense storage area. Specifi-
cally, as shown in the following figures. The red area is the dense
storage area, and the blue area is the buffer area. The workflow
needs to transport the container from node 157 to node 151. First, de-
tect that there are obstacles in the dense storage area going to node
157. The robot will first transport the obstacle at node 158 to an emp-
ty node in the buffer area. Second, the robot will transport the contain-
er at node 158 to the target node 151. Third, the robot will transport
the obstacle in the buffer area back to its original node 158 in the
dense storage area. This completes the entire transport task in the
dense storage area.
Fig. 5-86: Buffer area and dense storage area
1 Dense storage area 2 Buffer area
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
224/719 | www.kuka.com KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026

=== PDF PAGE 225 ===
Fig. 5-87: Transporting obstacles to the buffer area

KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 225/719
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 226 ===
• exclusive area: Only allows one robot to execute the workflow in the
exclusive area at a time. If the supervision area node is the last node
of the workflow, the robot will release the supervision area occupancy
when the workflow ends, allowing other robots to enter the supervision
area to execute the workflow.
• forklift handling area : Used for forklift robot operations, associated
with forklift smart (de) stacking and forklift normal (de) stacking strat-
egies. Determines whether the forklift performs stacking operations
when handling within the area.
Handling Strategies
• first in first out : When a container is moved into the area, it is placed
in the order of node priority from high to low. When a container is
moved out of the area, the container is selected for removal based on
the update time from earliest to latest.
The update time changes when the container's position or status is
updated, or when it is inserted or removed.
• carry as priority (first in first out) : When a container is moved into
the area, the node is selected for placement based on the node priori-
ty from high to low. When a container is moved out of the area, the
container is selected for removal based on the node priority from high
to low.
• carry as priority (first in last out) : When a container is moved into
the area, the node is selected for placement based on the node priori-
ty from high to low. When a container is moved out of the area, the
container is selected for removal based on the node priority from low
to high.
• prioritize selecting idle AMR within the area : When a task requires
moving a container out of the area, the container with a robot under-
neath it in the area is selected first.
• forklift smart (de)stacking : Used only for forklift robot operations.
Containers can be stacked, and the system globally configures the
stacking limit (PICK_CARRY_MAX_LAYER). When a container is
moved into the area, the node with the smallest current stack is selec-
ted first, provided that the forklift's current handling stack count + the
node's current stack count <= the system's stacking limit. If exceeded,
the exception handling logic is followed. When a container is moved
out of the area, all containers stacked on the high-priority node are
moved first, based on the node priority from high to low.
• forklift normal (de)stacking : Used only for forklift robot operations. If
a node has a container, it is considered occupied. When a container is
moved into the area, the high-priority idle node is selected first. When
a container is moved out of the area, the high-priority node with a
container is selected first.
• choose node by container model : When the robot is carrying a con-
tainer and preparing to enter this area, it will match the container mod-
el of the container being carried by the robot with the container model
configured at the nodes within the area. This is applicable in scenarios
where there are various different container model nodes mixed within
the area.
Default Container Type
Used only for specific business projects. Currently, it has no other special
function.
Node List and Order

5.2.1 Robot list
Description
Robot list is used to display the parameters and status of all registered ro-
bots, including simulated ones, and provides related operations.
Basic operations include:
• Searching robots
• Viewing details
• Checking historical exceptions
• Removing robots from a layout
• Inserting and updating robots on a layout
• Deleting robots
Batch operations are supported for removing and inserting robots.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
232/719 | www.kuka.com KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 233 ===
Fig. 5-98: Robot list
Precondition
• The IP address of the KUKA.AMR Fleet needs to be configured to ro-
bot using KUKA.AMR Suite.
• Set the robot port in KUKA.AMR Fleet to match the server's port
where the KUKA.AMR Fleet is located. For example, the default RCS
port for a robot with KUKA.AMR OS is 8988.
• Ensure that the robot and the KUKA.AMR Fleet are on the same local
area network (LAN) and can connect via the UDP protocol.
• The robot type must exist in Robot Types in KUKA.AMR Fleet.
Robot information
After a robot successfully registers with KUKA.AMR Fleet, the robot list
will automatically display its information and status, including the following
details:
• Robot ID/SN
• Type Name
• Layout District
• Current Position [x, y direction]
• Position Reliable
• Robot Status
• Occupy Status
• Last Node Number
• Battery Level [%]
• IP Address
• OS Version
• Battery Health

Adding a simulated robot
A simulated robot can be added to monitor task execution.
1. Enable a layout.
2. Click New Sims  and fill in the required information of a robot.
3. Click OK to add the robot. Its status is off-map.

5.2.3 Charging management
Description
The AMR can be charged by a conductive charging station or inductive
charging station. KUKA.AMR Fleet supports multiple charging modes:
• Conductive charging: The AMR is charged by a conductive charging
station. In the KUKA.AMR Fleet layout, this requires configuring a
charge enter node and a charging node. ( >>> 5.2.3.1 "Conductive
charging" Page 239 )
• Inductive charging: The AMR is charged by an inductive charging
station. In the KUKA.AMR Fleet layout, this requires a wireless charg-
ing node. ( >>> 5.2.3.2 "Inductive charging" Page 244 )
• Manual charging: A charging mission with a specified Charge Target
Level can be dispatched through the map monitor. The AMR stops
charging once the target level is reached. Manual charging missions
cannot be interrupted by other missions. ( >>> 5.2.3.3 "Manual charg-
ing" Page 245 )
• Automatic (Auto) charging : Automatic charging strategies can be set
based on layout, time, and AMR type. An automatic charging blacklist
can prevent AMRs from generating automatic charging missions during
specific time range. Automatic charging missions can be interrupted by
other missions except rest missions. ( >>> 5.2.3.4 "Automatic (Auto)
charging" Page 245 )
• Forced full charging : To calibrate the battery, the AMR (with
KUKA.AMR OS) needs a periodic full charging. The frequency and
maximum number of forced full charging missions per day can be set.
• Opportunity charging: If node configuration allows, AMRs supporting
wireless charging will automatically charge when idle. Charging discon-
nects automatically when a new command is received.
(>>> 5.2.3.5 "Opportunity charging" Page 249 )
• Flow charging: Charging missions can be incorporated as a node
within a job. The AMR executes missions sequentially and decides on
charging based on configuration. Once the battery level requirements
are met, it proceeds to the next mission. Flow charging missions are
not interrupted by other missions, support cancellation, and allow con-
tinuation of the next mission after cancellation. ( >>> 5.2.3.6 "Flow
charging" Page 261 )
Precondition
• The conductive or inductive charging station is compatible with the
AMR.
5.2.3.1 Conductive charging
In the KUKA.AMR Fleet layout, conductive charging requires configuring
the charge enter node and charging node, specifying the robot direction
during charging, and the robot type.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15

5.2.3.3 Manual charging
Clicking the Charge button on the map monitoring page to dispatch man-
ual charging missions.
Manual charging supports both conductive and inductive charging. It is not
affected by automatic charging strategies and will not be interrupted by
operational missions. The default target charge level for manual charging
is 95%.
Fig. 5-118: Dispatching a manual charging mission on the map moni-

5.2.3.4 Automatic (Auto) charging
Custom charging strategies can be configured based on different layout,
time and AMR type.
Enabling auto charging
Click the switch to enable or disable auto-charging. When enabled, the
AMR will follow the configured charging strategy. When enabled, the AMR
will follow the configured charging strategy to generate a AutoCharge mis-
sion. When disabled, select whether to stop the current charging mission,
and then all the AMRs will not generate AutoCharge missions automatical-
ly.

2. Click OK to confirm.
3. The corresponding Layout Strategy tab will be displayed. Switch the

A specific time strategy can be added to the selected layout. All AMR

(Whole Day).

Fig. 5-122: Time range whitelist (enabled auto charging)

Adding time range
1. Click Select Time  and the text on the icon changes to OK. If the
Same Day is enabled, the start time on the left cannot be equal to or
later than the end time on the right.
‒ If the Same Day is enabled, the start time on the left cannot be
equal to or later than the end time on the right. It is recommen-
ded to select the end time first, then the start time.
‒ If the Cross Day is enabled, the start time can be later than the
end time.
2. Select whether to check Mark as no charge time range . If checked,
the set time range will prohibit auto charging. If unchecked, the set
time range will allow auto charging.
3. Click OK to create a new time range.
The selected time range must not overlap with existing time ranges.
The whitelist for the new time range uses the default charging strat-
egy, while the blacklist has no charging strategy.
The blacklist affects automatic charging but not manual charging. Man-
ual charging missions can still be executed in two scenarios:
• Manually dispatching a charging mission to the AMR within the
blacklist time range
• Manually dispatching a charging mission outside the blacklist range
that then enters the blacklist time range
Deleting a time range
Click X on the right side of the specific time range. Confirm the deletion in
the confirmation box. The default time (Whole Day) charging strategy can-
not be deleted.
Configuring charging threshold
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 247/719
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 248 ===
Modify the values in the corresponding input fields and click Save to com-
plete the changes. Clicking Cancel before saving will reset the modifica-
tions and revert tothe previously saved strategy.
Fig. 5-124: Configuring charging strategy - Threshold Config
Based on the selected layout and timespan, fill in the following parameters
to configure a customized charging strategy:
• High Level: When the AMR reaches this battery level, it stops charg-
ing and returns to the standby node. Default: 95%.
• Idle Level: When an idle AMR's battery level falls below this thresh-
old, it will go to charge. Default: 80%.
• Safety Level: When the battery level exceeds the safety level, it can
be interrupted by a new mission or another charging mission below
the Min Running level. Default: 50%.
• Min Running Level : The minimum level required for the AMR to start
accepting missions. Default: 30%.
• Force Charge Level : When the AMR's battery level is below this
threshold and other conditions for Forced Full Charge are met, a
Forced Full Charge mission is generated. Default: 20%.
• Forced Full Charge : When the AMR is executing a forced full charge
mission, it cannot be interrupted until the battery level reaches 100%.
‒ Single AMR accumulated charging times: When the accumulated
charging times reaches this value, it triggers a forced full charge.
Default: 30 times.
‒ Each day times of mandatory full charge tasks is allowed: The
maximum number of forced full charge missions an AMR can exe-
cute in a day. Default: 1 time.
AMR type
Specific charging strategies can be configured for different AMR types in
the selected layout. When Specified is enabled for a particular AMR type,
the specified charging strategy will be applied to that type within the cur-
rent layout and time range, instead of the strategy in Threshold Config.
Fig. 5-125: Configuring charging strategies for different AMR types

charging strategy to all AMR types.

5.2.3.5 Opportunity charging
Description
Opportunity charging allows idle AMRs to charge during or between mis-
sions, using intelligent scheduling and real-time monitoring to avoid affect-
ing missions.
Opportunity charging is only applicable to AMRs that support inductive
charging. Inductive charging stations are usually installed in places where
AMRs may stop, such as warehouses and production lines. Opportunity
charging also supports charging loaded AMRs.
If the AMR configured with opportunity charging receives a new mission
during charging, it will stop the charging and continue with the mission.
Opportunity charging includes:
• System auto opportunity charging ( >>>  "System auto opportunity
charging" Page 249 )
• External control opportunity charging ( >>>  "External control opportuni-
ty charging" Page 251 )
System auto opportunity charging cannot use nodes set as opportunity
charging. Therefore, wireless charging nodes should be set on the
graph to enable both manual and auto charging.
Precondition
• Applicable AMRs should be equipped with KUKA.AMR OS and induc-
tive charging modules, such as KMP 1500P-EU-DIC diffDrive and
KMP 3000P-EU-2-D-M omniMove baseVersion.
Opportunity charging is not applicable to AMRs that inherently support
automatic wireless charging, such as KMR iisy.
System auto opportunity charging
Description
If an AMR supporting inductive charging remains idle for a period of time
at a node configured with the system automatic opportunity charging at-
tribute, it will receive signals from KUKA.AMR Fleet and enter the induc-
tive charging mode when inductive charging conditions are met.
In this mode, the AMR can continue to perform tasks such as moving and
lifting as usual.
Procedure
1. Navigate to Scheduling > Layout > District > Edit Graph  to enter
the graph editing page.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 249/719
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 250 ===
Fig. 5-126: Entering the graph editing page
2. Right-click on the desired node to configure System Automatic Oppor-
tunity Charging. It can be any functional node (except for charging en-
try node and charging node).
3. Click Edit, and select Enable(System Auto)  for the opportunity charg-
ing property.
Fig. 5-127: Opportunity charging property
4. Click Save to save the node configurations.
5. Click the Manual Save  icon 
 or use the shortcut Ctrl+S to save the
graph, then close the page.

skip this step.

ning bolt appears in the upper left corner of the node configured for

9. Move the AMR to this opportunity node. If this AMR remains idle for
more than 20 s by default, it will start charging.
The robot stop angle should be set to ensure that the AMR can reach
the node and charge successfully.
Error handling
• The AMR that does not support inductive charging will not start charg-
ing at the node configured with the System Auto opportunity charging
property.
• If the inductive charging station is not powered on, the AMR will re-
main idle and not start charging at the node configured with the Sys-
tem Auto opportunity charging property.
• If a node is configured with the System Auto opportunity charging
property but lacks a wireless charging device, the AMR will remain
idle and not start charging.
External control opportunity charging
Description
If an AMR supporting inductive charging remains idle for a period of time
at a node configured with the system automatic opportunity charging at-
tribute, KUKA.AMR Fleet will not automatically send a charging signal.
Instead, KUKA.AMR Fleet provides device event settings for external devi-
ces (such as button boxes, PLCs) to control charging.
Only idle AMRs can be controlled by events. AMRs executing missions or
in an abnormal state cannot be event-controlled.
Graph editing

the graph editing page.

nity Charging. It can be any functional node (except for charging entry
node and charging node).
3. Click Edit, and select External Control  for the opportunity charging
property.

Error handling
There are four errors on the device events:
• Case 1: If the Trigger Code configured in the device event detailed
configuration page is not the External Control Opportunity Charging
node due to graph editing, an error will occur when the button box is
used to trigger the start or stop of opportunity charging.
Fig. 5-145: Opportunity charging property changed into disable
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
258/719 | www.kuka.com KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 259 ===
Fig. 5-146: Unchanged trigger code
• Case 2: If there are no idle AMRs at the nodes configured with Exter-
nal Control Opportunity Charging, an error will occur when the button
box is used to trigger the start or stop of opportunity charging.
Fig. 5-147: No idle AMR at the external control opportunity charg-
ing node
• Case 3: If the Trigger Codes configured in the device event detailed
configuration page include the External Control Opportunity Charging
nodes and other nodes, and there no idle AMRs at the external con-
trol opportunity charging nodes, an error will occur when the button
box is used to trigger the start or stop of opportunity charging.
In this case, if there are idle AMRs at the external control opportuni-
ty charging nodes, the AMRs will start opportunity charging when
the button box is used to trigger the start or stop of opportunity
charging.
• Case 4: If the nodes configured with the Trigger Code in the device
event include external control opportunity charging nodes and others,
and there is an idle AMR at one of the external control opportunity
charging nodes, no equipment exception will be returned when using
the button box device to trigger the start/stop of opportunity charging.
The idle AMR can perform opportunity charging normally.

When device events encounter errors, the device status can be checked
in Equipment > Device Monitor.
1. Click Trigger click event  to check which rule chain node has the er-
ror.
Fig. 5-148: Device monitor

Fig. 5-149: Rule chain node error
3. Select Retry or Skip this step.
Fig. 5-150: Rule chain node error handling
4. Navigate to Equipment > Log and click Detail to check detailed error
information.

5.2.3.6 Flow charging
Description
Flow charging enables the AMR to perform a charging mission in the mid-
dle step of the business job, ensuring sufficient battery power to perform
subsequent missions of the job.
Precondition
• There is at least one Charge Enter Node (with a Charging Node) or
Wireless Charging Node set on the layout, which is reachable by the
AMR.
Procedure
Refer to ( >>>  "Configuring the charge step" Page 375 ) for details.
Mission handling
The AMR in a flow charge mission is locked by the job, so the flow
charge mission will not be interrupted.
• To finish the flow charge mission ahead of schedule and enable the
robot to continue performing subsequent missions of the job, click
Cancel for this flow charge mission on the mission list page or on the
map monitor page. The AMR will remain locked by this job until the
job is completed.
• To cancel the flow charge mission and the entire job, click Force Can-
cel for this job on the Job Board. The job will be canceled and the ro-
bot will be unlocked from this job.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 261/719

page

page

5.2.4 Auto parking
Description
The Auto Parking feature allows an idle robot to automatically move to a
designated node after a period of inactivity. This feature generates a park-
ing mission for the robot, ensuring efficient use of resources.
The Auto Parking feature requires specific configurations both on the set-
tings page and within the docker-compose.yml file.
Precondition
• The Enable the Idle Parking Function  switch is enabled.
• A sufficient number of nodes that are allowed for parking are set.
If no available nodes are found for the robot to park, the parking
mission will not be created.
Procedure
1. Set Enable the Idle Parking Function  to Yes to enable the Auto
Parking feature for the chosen layout.
The feature can be enabled globally or individually for each layout.
The settings for a specific layout take precedence over the default lay-
out settings.
Fig. 5-156: Enabling the idle parking function on the Layout De-
fault
Fig. 5-157: Enabling the idle parking function on the certain lay-
out
2. Set Enable rack resting  to Yes to enable the node with the parking
function.
In the docker-compose.yml file, under the krcs- mission-manager
environment, the configuration is:
robot.rest.rackResting.needRack: "false"
• "false": The parking feature is activated regardless of rack pres-
ence. The default value is "false".
• "true": The parking feature is activated only if a rack is present.
There are several types of nodes that allow robots to park:
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 263/719
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 264 ===
• Parking node: This node type explicitly allows robots to park and
can specify which robot types are permitted. If a specific robot
type is set, only that type can park at the node; otherwise, all
types are allowed.
Fig. 5-158: Parking node
• Rack node: The node type can be configured to enable parking,
contingent upon additional settings in the docker-compose.yml
file and the presence of a rack at the node.
Fig. 5-159: Enabling rack resting on the bracket node
• Bracket node: The node type can be configured to enable park-
ing, contingent upon additional settings in the docker-com-
pose.yml file and the presence of a rack at the node.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
264/719 | www.kuka.com KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 265 ===
Fig. 5-160: Enabling rack resting on the bracket node
Only robot types that are compatible with the rack model or fine
localization model configured at the designated nodes are al-
lowed to proceed to these nodes.
3. Set the Idle Parking Support  node switch and the idle time before
generating a parking mission.
• Disabled: A robot leaves the node immediately after being re-
leased
• Enabled: The robot waits for a specified time before leaving.
Fig. 5-161: Setting Idle Parking Support on a node
The time an idle robot waits before generating a parking mission de-
pends on both global layout settings and node-specific configurations.
The waiting time is set in the layout configuration and can be custom-
ized for different layouts.
The configuration is labeled Idle Time for Parking , with the unit
measured in seconds.

5.2.5 Obstacle avoidance plan
Description
Obstacle avoidance plan is used to manage obstacle avoidance plans
based on the robot type. Various detection range thresholds during the ro-
bot motions can be set and sent to the robot for path planning.
Each obstacle avoidance plan is specific to one robot type, but a robot
type can have multiple plans.
• Under normal conditions, the robot uses the default obstacle avoid-
ance plan for its type, with a code similar to "xxxxoap0".
• In special conditions, the robot uses a special obstacle avoidance plan
corresponding to the specific condition.
When robots have conflicts on adjacent nodes, such as on parallel rack
nodes or at parallel charge station nodes, it is recommended to change
the map by increasing the node distance first to avoid conflicts. If map ed-
iting is not allowed due to physical restrictions or customer demand, and
there is no risk of collision when moving objects around, then consider
setting an obstacle avoidance plan with smaller safe distances to avoid ro-
bot conflicts and safety envelope overlapping.
Smaller safe distance may result in a safety PLC error (KMP P series)
or even a collision with obstacles.
The operations for the obstacle avoidance plan include creating, querying,
viewing details, editing, copying, setting as default, and deleting.
Additionally, custom obstacle avoidance plans can be created and config-
ured at specific edges or nodes on the layout.
Fig. 5-163: Obstacle avoidance plan page
Special conditions for SLAM navigation :
• Rotation: By default, the OA (obstacle avoidance) plan with the code
ROTATE_DEFAULT for the specific AMR type will be used.

for that node will be used.
Fig. 5-166: OA plan configured on the node
• Unloaded Entry/Exit of Bracket Nodes : By default, the obstacle
avoidance plan with the code HOLDER_UNLOAD_DEFAULT is used
for the specific AMR type.

3. If the current safety distance setting is below the minimum standard, a

The minimum safety distance is often used in scenarios with small
node or edge spacing, such as side-by-side racks or charging no-
des. To achieve this, the parameter values in the OA plan need to
be adjusted as small as possible.
‒ For QR code nodes, set the safety distance to -50mm in the
parallel driving direction (left and right) of two AMRs on the
edge.
‒ For regular topology or texture nodes, set the safety distance to
-100mm in the parallel driving direction (left and right) of two
AMRs on the edge.

• The default obstacle avoidance plan cannot be deleted.
• Deleting an obstacle avoidance plan already set on a layout or con-
tainer model will affect the layout and container model.
Setting an obstacle avoidance plan as default
An AMR model can have only one default obstacle avoidance plan. Set-
ting a plan as default changes the previous default plan to non-default.
The default plan will be used unless a specific plan is set in the layout.
Plans with a safety distance below the minimum standard cannot be set
as "default."
The role of obstacle avoidance plan parameters in path planning
Switch
The following parameter in docker-compose.yml under the environ-
ment of krcs-algorithm-service decides whether the safe distance
of the obstacle avoidance plan is used in path planning.
as.collision.use-safe-distance: "true"
• "true": Not only robot's dimensions and positioning control errors, but
also obstacle avoidance errors and safe distance from obstacle avoid-
ance plan will be considered in envelope calculation of path planning.
‒ Case: Usually Used. The factors considered in path planning by
KUKA.AMR Fleet are the same as those of robot during the actual
execution. This will avoid situations where the Fleet plans a path
with no collision, but the robot encounters obstacles during execu-
tion.
• "false": Robot's dimensions and positioning control errors will be con-
sidered in envelope calculation of path planning.
‒ Case: Seldom used.
‒ When the obstacle avoidance function of the robot is switched
off.
‒ When the obstacle avoidance function is working and the map
is specially designed.
‒ Find the minimum node distance for two robots arriving at
neighbor nodes at the same time. When the setting is
"true", the distance is d1. When it is "false", the distance is
d2.
‒ Distances between nodes should be greater than d1 or
smaller than d2.
Safe distance value
OA plan can be set on edges and nodes. The priority of OA plans is: OA
plan specifically configured on the edge or node > and the default OA
plan for the AMR type > the default safe distance.
Basicline600, Basicline1000 and all robot types whose type names start
with K have default OA plan and can set specific OA plan. Other robot
types can only use default-safe-distance as their front/back/left/right safe
distance on edges and nodes.
as.collision.default-safe-distance.global: 0.1
• Meaning: The global default safe distance applies to all directions and
all robot types, except those explicitly defined in subsequent settings.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
276/719 | www.kuka.com KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026
Programming and configuration


=== PDF PAGE 277 ===
• Use: If safe distances vary for front/back/left/right/rotate, specify 5 val-
ues in this order (e.g., 0.4, 0.15, 0.2, 0.2, 0.3 ). The default
value 0.1 implies all omitted values will inherit the last specified
value. Values 1 to 5 can be defined accordingly.
as.collision.default-safe-distance.robotTypeX: front, back, 
left, right, rotate
• Meaning: This is a template which sets default safe distances for a
specific robot type differing from the global value. Fill it with the actual
value and add one line for each robot type.
• Use: Replace robotTypeX with the actual robot type code (e.g., KMP
1500P-EU-D diffDrive). Replace the directional placeholders with spe-
cific values. The value rules align with the above global setting. For
example, if the KMP 1500P-EU-D diffDrive requires a front safe dis-
tance of 0.5, 0.2 for back, and 0.1 for left, right, and rotate, add the
following settings:
as.collision.default-safe-distance.KMP 1500P-EU-D 
diffDrive: 0.5, 0.2, 0.1
Special cases
For the following scenarios:
• Entering and exiting a rack.
• Entering and exiting an elevator.
If a path cannot be planned, it is recommended to configure a specific ob-
stacle avoidance plan for the corresponding edge, with parameter values
smaller than those of the corresponding xxxx_DEFAULT obstacle avoid-
ance plan.
For scenarios involving in-place rotation: configure a specific obstacle
avoidance plan for the corresponding node if the robot type allows to do
so. Otherwise, change the 2 parameters above about as.colli-
sion.default-safe-distance.
Scenario: Entering a rack
A rack with code 1500P_mode_code is placed at node 15. The unloaded
AMR, KMP 1500P-EU-D diffDrive, moves from node 16 to node 15 using
a special obstacle avoidance plan, with safety distances defined in the
for_move_to_rack plan.
Fig. 5-191: Scenario: Entering a rack
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 277/719

5.3 Task
5.3.1 Mission list
Description
The Mission List feature displays the historical missions executed by the
robot, including those issued from the monitoring page and from upstream
sources. If a mission is issued from upstream, one mission in the mission
list corresponds to a step task in the upstream job. Clicking on a mission
row allows viewing of the sub-missions' execution information. Different
operations can be performed on missions under different modes. And mis-
sions can be exported in .xlsx file format.
Each mission includes the following information: ID, mission code, job
code, template name, AMR number, target rack, target node, mission sta-
tus, related layout, mission error code, mission exception information, lock
status, creation time, end time, manual handling, and corresponding oper-
ations.
Mission details:
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 279/719
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 280 ===
Mission details Description
ID • The ID is automatically generated by KUKA.AMR
Fleet.
• Missions can be queried by ID.
Mission Code • Except for charging missions and rest missions,
when the mission is issued from the monitoring
page, the mission code is the timestamp when
the mission is issued. When missions are issued
from upstream, the code format is defined by the
upstream. The general code format is defined as
W/C-Timestamp-ProcessNumber-Mission Se-
quence Number.
• Missions can be queried by mission code. Fuzzy
queries can be performed based on the Process
Number(-Process Number-)  in the mission code
to find missions that have been issued in the
same job.
• The mission code supports copying by clicking
the small icon.
Job Code
• The job code displayed as “-” indicates that the
mission is not issued from upstream; The job
code displayed as a specific code, such
asT000000007 indicates that the mission is is-
sued from upstream.
• Missions can be searched by the job code. The
search results show the missions that have been
issued in the job.
• Clicking on a specific Job Code  redirects to the
corresponding Job Board. If the job board does
not display the job, it indicates that the job has
been archived to the Job History, where the job
can be searched based on the job code.
Template Name • The template of the mission
• The type of resources required for the mission
can be determined based on the mission tem-
plate. For example:
‒ Move:
‒ When unloaded, only AMR resources are
needed.
‒ When loaded, both AMR and container re-
sources are needed.
‒ Lift Up/Lift Down: both AMR and container re-
sources are needed.
‒ Rest: both AMR and rest node resources are
needed.
‒ Charging: both AMR and charging node re-
sources are needed.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
280/719 | www.kuka.com KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 281 ===
Mission details Description
AMR Number • The AMR related to the mission
• When the mission is in the queued state, it dis-
plays all candidate AMRs designated for the mis-
sion.
• When the mission enters the planning phase and
subsequent states, it displays the AMR number
executing the mission.
• Missions can be queried based on the AMR num-
ber.
Target Rack
• The container code related to the mission
• Missions can be queried based on the container
code. The query results provide historical mission
information for the container.
• The historical missions can be used to preliminar-
ily determine the lift status of the container in
KUKA.AMR Fleet. Normally, if the last mission re-
lated to the container involves liftUp, the lift status
of the container in the system is Lift.
Target Node
• The target node of the mission
• Missions can be searched based on the target
node.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 281/719
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 282 ===
Mission details Description
Mission Status
• Under normal circumstances, the status changes
of the mission are as follows: Queued-Dispatch-
ing-Dispatched-Ready-Planning-Planned-Exe-
cuting-Finished.
• In exceptional cases, if the mission dispatching
resources fail, the status will briefly change from
Queued to Suspended, and KUKA.AMR Fleet will
continuously attempt to allocate resources auto-
matically until the mission successfully get resour-
ces, at which point the mission will enter the
Planning status.
• In exceptional cases, if the mission fails to plan
path, the status changes from mission Planning to
Suspended, and KUKA.AMR Fleet will continu-
ously attempt to plan the path for the mission un-
til the path planning succeeds, at which point the
mission enters the planned state.
• When the Pause button is clicked, the mission
status changes from Executing to Pausing, and
then updates to Paused.
• When the Resume button is clicked, the mission
status changes from Paused to Resuming, and
then to Executing.
• When the Cancel or Force Cancel  button is
clicked, the mission status changes from the
initial state to Canceling, and then to canceled.
• The final states of a mission are either canceled
or Finished. If the AMR is executing a step task
within a job, a canceled mission will unlock the
AMR, and the AMR will no longer execute related
tasks of that job, resulting in the job being can-
celed as well. Finished missions will not unlock
the AMR; the AMR will continue to execute the
next step task of the job until the job ends, at
which point the AMR will be unlocked. Canceling
a mission that is already in the final state is an
invalid action.
• The mission can be searched by:
‒ Selecting Mission Status. Multiple selections
are supported.
‒ Selecting Switch Status to display all missions
or only display incomplete missions.
Related Layout • The map where the mission target node is loca-
ted.
• The mission can be searched by the target map.
Mission Error-
Code
Whether the mission has an anomaly and the catego-
ry of the anomaly
Mission Excep-
tion Information
• The specific error information of the mission
anomaly
• The execution status of the mission and corre-
sponding actions can be determined based on the
mission exception information.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
282/719 | www.kuka.com KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 283 ===
Mission details Description
Lock Status
• Whether the mission will lock the AMR.
• Value Range:
‒ Locked: After the mission is finished, the
AMR will remain locked to facilitate the execu-
tion of the next task in the same job, effec-
tively locking the AMR to the job. The locked
AMR can only be assigned tasks from the
current job.
‒ Unlocked: After the mission is finished, the
AMR will be unlocked and can be assigned
other missions.
• When the status of the AMR's last mission is
Locked and Finished, it is recommended to navi-
gate to the corresponding job board to check the
job execution status, as the next task in the job
may not have been successfully issued.
Creation Time • The time when the mission was created.
• Missions can be queried based on a time range.
Query results include missions where both the
creation and end times fall within this range.
End Time
• The time when the mission ended.
• The mission may have ended due to cancellation
or normal completion.
• Missions can be queried based on a time range.
Query results include missions where both the
creation and end times fall within this range.
Mission mode:
Mission mode Description
Normal mode Supporting mission operations: manual handling, details, pause and
retry.
Debug mode Supporting mission operations: manual handling, details, pause, retry,
cancel, force cancel, force complete and change robot.
Mission status:
Mission information Description
Queued The mission is in KUKA.AMR Fleet, waiting for resources allocation.
If a mission is always in a queued state, it may be missing resources
(such as AMR, container, rest node, charging node), check the re-
source status according to the error message.
Dispatching KUKA.AMR Fleet is allocating resources for the mission.
Dispatched KUKA.AMR Fleet has allocated, locked mission resources for the mis-
sion or the mission is waiting to lock the resource.
Ready The resources have been locked by the mission.
Planning KUKA.AMR Fleet is planning executable paths and action data for the
mission.
Planned KUKA.AMR Fleet plans executable paths and action data for the mis-
sion.
Executing KUKA.AMR Fleet is executing the mission actions.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 283/719
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 284 ===
Mission information Description
Finished All mission actions have been fully executed.
Pausing KUKA.AMR Fleet is pausing the mission execution.
A mission that is being paused cannot be clicked to pause again.
Paused KUKA.AMR Fleet pauses the mission execution.
Resuming KUKA.AMR Fleet is resuming the execution of a paused mission.
Suspended An exception occurs, and KUKA.AMR Fleet suspends the mission,
awaiting further handling.
There are multiple reasons for a mission being suspended, such as
the AMR reporting a high-level exception during mission execution,
path planning failure, and the AMR autonomously adjusting its route.
• When a mission is suspended due to the AMR reporting a high-
level exception during task execution, the task will not automati-
cally retry until the AMR reports a Commandable state.
• When a mission is suspended due to path planning failure, the
mission will retry a certain number of times until a path is plan-
ned.
• When the AMR autonomously adjusts its route, the mission will be
briefly suspended, and the AMR will continue to execute the pre-
viously issued commands. The presented AMR and mission status
may be: the mission is in a suspended state, while the AMR (dis-
played as yellow) is executing commands. In most cases, the task
will quickly return to a normal execution state.
Canceling KUKA.AMR Fleet is canceling the mission execution.
A mission that is being canceled cannot be clicked to cancel again.
Canceled KUKA.AMR Fleet cancels the mission execution.
Fig. 5-195: Mission list - normal mode
Fig. 5-196: Mission list - debug mode

Programming and configuration

Fig. 5-197: Mission list - manual handling
Querying a mission
The query function supports filtering based on multiple conditions, includ-
ing mission code, robot number, container code, job code, mission ID,
mission status, target map, target node, and time selection (start time and
end time). The search conditions can be expanded or collapsed by click-
ing the top-right corner.
Fig. 5-198: Querying a mission
1. Enter the filter value for the search item.
2. Click Search.
3. Click Reset to clear the text box and display all missions.
To find the mission list for a specific job, perform a fuzzy search using
the job ID in the mission code, such as "-1234-".
Pausing/Resuming a mission
Click the Pause button to pause the execution of subsequent actions for
this mission. The mission execution can be resumed later by clicking Re-
sume.
Retrying a mission
Click the Retry button to resume the non-parking missions that were sus-
pended due to system processing errors.
Canceling a mission
Click the Cancel button to cancel the current mission and its subsequent
actions.
A canceled mission cannot be resumed. Unlike a forced cancellation, a
regular cancellation requires sending a cancellation command to the AMR
and confirming its successful execution before completion.
Force completing mission
Click the Force Complete  button to forcefully complete the suspended
mission. KUKA.AMR Fleet will mark it as completed and move on to the
next mission, ensuring business continuity.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 285/719
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 286 ===
Force completion of a mission is recommended when a mission encoun-
ters an exception and cannot be recovered. Manual intervention, such
as moving the AMR to the target node, is required before clicking the
Force Complete  button.
Force canceling mission
Click the Force Cancel  button to forcefully cancel the queued, executing
or suspended mission and its subsequent actions.
The task cannot be restored. Unlike a regular cancellation, a forceful can-
cellation does not require sending a cancellation command or confirmation
to the AMR.
It is recommended to use forceful cancellation when regular cancellation
fails (e.g., AMR is unresponsive or disconnected).
Differences between Force Cancel and Cancel (Monitor page/Mission list)
• For commands that the AMR supports cancellation, such as move/
rotate:
‒ Click Cancel: KUKA.AMR Fleet will request the AMR to cancel the
command being executed, and the mission will be updated to can-
celed only after the AMR reports the cancellation is complete.
‒ Click Force Cancel: KUKA.AMR Fleet will not request the AMR to
cancel the command but will clear the commands that have not
yet been issued in KUKA.AMR Fleet. After clearing, the mission
will be updated to Canceled, and the issued commands will con-
tinue to be executed by the AMR.
• For commands that the AMR does not support cancellation, such as
liftUp:
‒ Click Cancel: KUKA.AMR Fleet will wait for the AMR to execute
the liftUp command until the AMR reports completion, then update
the container to the Lift state and update the mission to canceled.
‒ Click Force Cancel: KUKA.AMR Fleet will not wait for the AMR to
complete the liftUp command, directly update the mission status to
canceled, and not update the container's lift state. Assuming the
current command is a liftUp, if the AMR can normally execute the
command, the following situation will occur: In KUKA.AMR Fleet,
the container is in the Drop state, but the actual AMR is holding
the container.
• Force Cancel often accompanies manual handling of the AMR, en-
suring that KUKA.AMR Fleet data is consistent with the AMR's sta-
tus and container status.
• Cancel allows KUKA.AMR Fleet data to be consistent with the re-
sults of the AMR's execution.
• It is recommended to use Cancel in the mission list/monitoring page
to cancel individual missions, and only attempt to click Force Can-
cel if Cancel fails. Force Cancel  is a function set up to prevent the
AMR from experiencing anomalies when responding to cancel re-
quests, reducing the impact on the fleet system at the mission level.
Manual handling
Refer to ( >>> 4.4.1.1 "Viewing jobs" Page 80 ).

=== PDF PAGE 287 ===
Changing an AMR
To remove the AMR currently performing the mission and allocate another
AMR to execute it, the following preconditions should be met:
• The mission must be in a suspended status.
• Not applicable to HCS2.0, only applicable to certain types.
1. Click Change Robot  to enter the robot change process.
2. When AMR 1 is carrying container 111001, press the emergency stop
button on AMR 1. In Debug Mode, perform the Change Robot opera-
tion for this mission.
3. Select sync update container 111001 position :
• If checked, enter the node number or select a node on the map.
• If unchecked, the container position remains unchanged, and on-
site personnel must manually move the container to the specified
position.
4. Click OK: AMR 1 will be removed, and KUKA.AMR Fleet will automat-
ically dispatch AMR 2 to execute the mission.
• If AMR 2 has a path to the change position, it will automatically
proceed and execute the mission.
• If AMR 2 does not have a path, manually press the emergency
stop button, move it to the change position, release the emergency
stop, and press reset. The AMR will then continue the mission.
Canceling the AMR change
Click the Cancel button to cancel the AMR change operation.
Exporting the mission list
Click Export, select Time Range and Export Scope, click Confirm,
an .xlsx file will be generated.
For exported missions, filtering can be done in two ways:
• Time range
• Whether the mission has experienced any anomalies
Exported mission information includes: mission id, mission code, mission
label, MissionType, robotId, robotTypeCode, templateCode, templateName,
mapCode, floorNumber, mission priority, mission status, statusInfo, source,
mission beginTime, mission endTime, errorCode, errorInfo, lockStatus,
manualStatus, errorLevel, targetContainerCode, targetNodeNumber, crea-
tedBy, lastUpdatedBy, createTime, lastUpdateTime, jobCode, errorTag, er-
rorTime, dealTime, extraInfo.
Manual/Auto refresh
Click the Manual/Auto Refresh  switch to toggle the status.
• When the switch is in Manual Refresh status, click query to refresh
the mission list.
• When the switch is in Auto Refresh status, the mission list will auto-
matically refresh every 5 seconds based on the filled filter criteria.

Parameter Description
robotTypeClass Category of robots for executing the mission.
Can be omitted or a single value can be passed.
If robotTypeClass doesn't pass, there are no re-
strictions on the category of AMRs executing the mis-
sion.
If robotTypeClass passes, the candidate AMRs will
be filtered to include only those belonging to the
specified category.
• Lift AMR
• Conveyor AMR
• Carton Transfer Unit
• Forklift AMR
• Cabinet AMR
• Composite AMR
• SMT AMR
• Tugger AMR
robotTypeCode Candidate robot type codes for executing the mission.
Optional or multiple values can be passed, separated
by commas.
Not passing robotTypeCode indicates no restriction
on the robot type codes for executing the mission. If
robotTypeCode is passed, KUKA.AMR Fleet will filter
out robots that belong to the specified robot type co-
des from the candidate robots.
rackToward Specifies the angle of the container after it is lowered
to the target node. Optional. This parameter must not
conflict with the Container Stop Angle  configured at
the target node, meaning the parking angle at the tar-
get node is either not set or includes the value of this
parameter. If this parameter is provided and does not
conflict with the Container Stop Angle configured at
the node, the mission will follow this parameter. If this
parameter is not provided, the mission will follow the
Container Stop Angle configured at the node.
targetNodes The node labels of the target nodes for container low-
ering. Node labels are mandatory. The target node
can set the orientation of the robot before it places
the container, which is goalOrientations , usually
only one angle is provided, or it can be omitted. This
parameter must not conflict with the AMR Stop Angle
configured at the node, meaning the robot parking
angle at the node is either not set or the set angle in-
cludes the value of this parameter. If this parameter
is provided and does not conflict with the AMR Stop
Angle configured at the node, the mission will follow
this parameter. If this parameter is not provided, the
mission will follow the AMR Stop Angle configured at
the node.
templateCode Mission template. Required.
upstreamNum-
ber
Mission code. Required and unique.

5.3.3 Customized actions
Description
Customized actions can be added and configured in Graph, Workflow, or
triggered via the WCS gRPC interface for a flexible action management.
The default protobuf files for KMR iisy are located in the installation pack-
age under <package>/server/config/apis or in the main path of
KUKA.AMR Fleet at /opt/server/config/apis. Further information on
each command can be found in the protobuf files.
Custom actions can be defined using the same "data provider" and "action
executor" interfaces and set in the graph or workflow.
Fig. 5-201: Customized actions page
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 295/719
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 296 ===
Precondition
• The feature supports robot with KUKA.Navigation only, such as KMR
iisy.
Use case 1: Going through a door or narrow road
When the AMR needs to move through a door or narrow road, a special
safety configuration is required.
In the scenario where edge B -> C is under the door, the AMR activates
the special safety configuration and starts honking when it leaves the
node B. After passing through the door and reaching the node C, the
AMR deactivates the safety configuration and stops honking.
If the AMR at node B needs to move to node C, the action list should be:
1. SafetyConfig
2. HonkCommand
3. VirtualLineMotion (B -> C)
4. DeactivateSafetyConfig
5. HonkCommand
Fig. 5-202: Move mission from node B to C
In KUKA.AMR Fleet, there are several ways to achieve this mission:
1. Set on Node:
• Create two customized actions with parameters on the customized
actions page:
‒ code1: SafetyConfig and HonkCommand
‒ code2: DeactivateSafetyConfig and HonkCommand
• Set code1 as Customized Action [Leave] in the advanced settings
at node B.
• Set code2 as Customized Action [Reach] in the advanced settings
at node C.
2. Set on Edge:
• Define a custom command and provide protobuf files, such as
"MyCustomCommand." Implement data provider and action execu-
tor for this command. It can be an external device or a command
to control one of the components of the AMR.
• Register the action executor on the AMR.
• Copy the protobuf file to the server path: "/opt/server/config/apis."
• Restart KUKA.AMR Fleet.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
296/719 | www.kuka.com KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 297 ===
• Set MyCustomCommand as a Customized Action with parameters
on the Customized Actions page:
‒ MyCustomCommandCode: MyCustomCommand
• Set MyCustomCommandCode as a customized action in the ad-
vanced settings on edge B->C.
3. Set on Workflow:
• Create two customized actions with parameters on the customized
actions page:
‒ code1: SafetyConfig and HonkCommand
‒ code2: DeactivateSafetyConfig and HonkCommand
• Create a workflow with two procedures:
1. Node B with component command (when departed)
customized action and choose code1.
2. Node C with component command (when arrived) customized
action and choose code2.
4. Use WCS gRPC:
• Ensure KwcsMissionService.proto and customized_actions.proto
are added to the application.
• Construct two custom protobuf objects CustomizedActions, which
can be CustomizedActionsCode or CustomizedCommandList.
‒ If using CustomizedActionsCode, ensure it is configured on the
Customized Actions page in the UI.
‒ If using CustomizedCommandList, configuration in the UI is not
required, but ensure the protobuf files are added to the appli-
cation.
• Set the two objects as parameters of WcsGenerateMissionRe-
quest.
Use case 2: Docking at a workstation with high accuracy and execute an arm applica-
tion
KMR iisy must dock at a workstation with high accuracy to execute an
arm application. In the scenario, node C is the workstation, and node B is
the starting point for high-accuracy movement. When KMR iisy reaches
node B, it should execute the following commands:
1. SafetyConfig
2. A Custom Command (Execute fine positioning and relative motion to
make sure AMR is on node B with high accuracy)
3. VirtualLineMotion (B -> C)
4. ExecuteApplicationCommand
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 297/719

=== PDF PAGE 298 ===
Fig. 5-203: Docking at a workstation and executing an arm appli-
cation
In KUKA.AMR Fleet, there are several ways to achieve this mission:
1. Define a MyCustomCommand and set on Node:
• Define a custom command and provide protobuf files, such as My-
CustomCommand, which includes a fine positioning loop for multi-
ple adjustments. The loop contains fine localization and relative
motion.
• Register the action executor on the AMR.
• Copy the protobuf file to the server path: /opt/server/config/
apis.
• Restart KUKA.AMR Fleet.
• Set MyCustomCommand as a customized action with parameters
on the customized action page:
‒ MyCustomCommandCode: MyCustomCommand
• Set MyCustomCommand as Customized Action [Leave] in the ad-
vanced settings at node B.
• Create a workflow: Node C with component command (when ar-
rived) iisy application with the name of the arm application.
5.3.3.1 Customized action management
Description
Customized actions with specific parameters can be created. Multiple
commands can be added in sequence within one customized action. The
actions are executed in the specified order on the AMR.
1. Navigate to Scheduling > Task > Customized Actions.

KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
300/719 | www.kuka.com KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 301 ===
Fig. 5-208: Adding multiple commands
Customized action on graph
Customized actions can be set on nodes or edges and are considered in
path planning. The cost parameter in the customized actions is used for
path planning calculations.
• Set on node: Two types of customized actions can be set on nodes.
‒ REACH: Actions are executed when the AMR reaches the node. If
the AMR needs to rotate at the node, actions are executed before
the rotation.
‒ LEAVE: Actions are executed when the AMR leaves the node. If
the AMR needs to rotate at the node, actions are executed after
the rotation.
If the AMR continues in the same direction, the actions set on the
node are also executed.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 301/719
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 302 ===
Fig. 5-209: Customized actions Reach and Leave
• Set on edge: KUKA.AMR Fleet requires each topology edge to have
at least one command to move the AMR from the starting node of the
edge to its target node. When customized actions are set on the
edge, they replace the default movement command, which is the virtu-
al Line Motion Command for KMR iisy.
Customized actions set on the edge must move the AMR from the
start node to the end node of the topology edge. Otherwise, the
AMR will fail after executing the customized action.

5.4 Model
Description
Model management is used to manage the following models:
• Container model ( >>> 5.4 "Model" Page 305 )
• Box-shelf model ( >>> 5.4.2 "Box-shelf model" Page 308 )
• Fine localization model ( >>> 5.4.3 "Fine localization model" Page 309 )
5.4.1 Container model
Description
The container mode is used to set the specifications and dimensions of
the containers and the applicable types of AMRs.
For container-type containers, the obstacle avoidance strategy can be set
separately.
Each container model is used for containers of the same nature, and con-
tainer entities created based on the container model can be carried.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 305/719
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 306 ===
Fig. 5-215: Container model
Adding a container model
Click New Model  to add a container model and fill in three kinds of infor-
mation on the container model:
Fig. 5-216: Adding a container model
• Basic information: including model type, rack type (specific to Hik
AMR), model code, model name, and remarks.
‒ Fields marked with an asterisk (*) are mandatory.
‒ The model name is used to set the container model for the func-
tional node matching during layout drawing, such as rack nodes
and workstation nodes.
‒ Once set, model type and model code cannot be changed.
‒ Available model type options are: container, tray (AMR), tray (Fork-
lift), and mobile box-container.
‒ For mobile box-container models, the container structure must be
set, including the number of layers, columns, and the height of
each layer.

• Advanced information: including AMR type and obstacle avoidance
plan.
‒ AMR type: The container of this container model can only be
transported by compatible AMR types, with multiple selections sup-
ported.
‒ If KMP 600S-2 diffDrive is selected as a compatible AMR type,
a model file must be uploaded; otherwise, the container cannot
be created.
The process is as follows: after confirming and saving the con-
tainer model, click Upload Model File  to import a locally com-
pliant file.
‒ Obstacle avoidance plan :
‒ It should be set for the AMR movement in the following two
scenarios:
‒ Moving unloaded to the bottom of the rack
‒ Moving unloaded out from under the rack
‒ If no OA plan is configured, the default plan with model code
"RACK_DEFAULT" will be used. If settings are configured, they
will take precedence.
‒ The number of OA plans is determined by the AMR type. Each
AMR type can have only one corresponding obstacle strategy.
It is possible to set strategies for some AMR types and not for
others.
• Parameter information: The specifications and dimensions of the
model, which vary depending on the model type.
‒ Fields marked with an asterisk (*) are mandatory.
‒ Rack: rack length (m), rack width, container height, rack leg
length, rack leg width, chassis height, model weight (kg), model
load.
‒ Tray (AMR): tray length (m), tray width, tray height, model weight
(kg), model load.
‒ Tray (Forklift): tray length (m), tray width, tray height, model weight
(kg), model load, lift height (height of the fork arms after lifting the
tray and before lowering it).
‒ Mobile Box-container: rack length (m), rack width, container height,
model weight (kg), model load.
Querying a container model
Single or multiple queries can be performed based on model code, model
name, model type and create datetime span.

5.4.2 Box-shelf model
Description
The box-shelf model is used to set specifications for immovable conveyor
lines or storage locations used for loading, unloading, or warehousing.
The box-shelf model is only used for the robot type of Carton Transfer
Unit (only in the Chinese market).
The operations on the box-shelf model include creating, querying, viewing
details, editing, and deleting.
Fig. 5-218: Box-shelf model
Adding a box-shelf model
The box-shelf information includes model name, model code, layer height,
and overall shelf dimensions. Fields marked with an asterisk (*) are man-
datory.
Model size includes total length (mm), width, height, ground clearance,
and the number of layers.
Layer height allows selecting whether layers are of equal height under lay-
er type and setting the height of each layer.
The ground clearance of the first layer is equal to Ground Clearance.
The ground clearance of the second layer is Ground Clearance + height
of the first layer, and so on.
Querying a box-shelf model
Single or multiple queries can be performed based on model name, model
code, and create datetime span.
Viewing the details of a box-shelf model
The box-shelf model details include model code, model name, total length,
total width, total height, ground clearance, layer height, height of each lay-
er, created by, created time, last edited by, and last edited time.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
308/719 | www.kuka.com KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026
Programming and configuration


=== PDF PAGE 309 ===
Fig. 5-219: The details of a box-shelf model
Editing a box-shelf model
Click Edit to modify the box-shelf model information and save the
changes.
If the box-shelf model is already used in a map, it is recommended to
enter the map editing page and click Save again after updating the box-
shelf model information to ensure the changes take effect.
Deleting a box-shelf model
Click Delete and confirm to remove the box-shelf model. Check and up-
date the Box-Shelf Node settings configured for this box-shelf model.
5.4.3 Fine localization model
Description
The fine localization model is used for AMR types with the KUKA.AMR
OS, such as the KMP 600i and KMP 1500P-EU-D. It supports object mod-
el recognition and reflective sticker recognition. The configured fine locali-
zation model can be set at the bracket node ( >>> 5.1.4.1 "Node proper-
ties" Page 185 ) or the fine positioning guidance at the edge (see Propriet-
ary Configuration) in the advanced configuration.
Fig. 5-220: The list of fine localization model

KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 309/719

=== PDF PAGE 310 ===
Precondition
• The AMR should be equipped with the KUKA.AMR OS, such as the
KMP 600i and KMP 1500P-EU-D.
• The AMR model library must include model files with filenames match-
ing the fine localization model code.
Adding a fine localization model
Click New, select Fine Location Type, fill in Model Code, Model Name,
Remark (optional), select Adapted AMR Type, and click OK.
• Fine Location Type: Object Model Recognition, Reflective Tape Recog-
nition.
• Model Code: At least five characters, allowing Chinese and English
characters, numbers, hyphens, and underscores.
• Send Rack Model If Rack On Top: When there is a rack on the brack-
et node or at the end of the edge configured with the fine localization
model, the model parameter passed to the AMR is the rack model or
the fine localization model. The default is the rack model.
Fig. 5-221: Fine localization model

el Name, Model Code, Adapted Robot Type, Remark, Create Time, Fine
location Type and Model File.
Editing a fine localization model
Click Edit to edit Remark, Model Name, or Adapted Robot Type of a fine
localization model.
Setting a fine localization model on a bracket node
Select the node function as bracket node. Once the fine localization model
is set to the bracket node, the AMR will perform fine localization model
recognition each time it moves to the bracket node.

5.5.1 Container
Description
The container page is used to manage and monitor various types of con-

Fig. 5-225: Container page

entrance node number, container direction, full/empty status, on/off site
status, occupancy status, lift status, and operation buttons.
Basic operations include querying, adding new containers, viewing details,
removing, inserting, updating, deleting, and batch processing.
Batch processing includes batch removal, batch insertion, batch deletion,
and batch creation.

• A container model is created in Container Model.
• A building layout with districts is created, nodes are plotted (recom-
mended to set nodes as functional and configure the corresponding
container model), and the layout is activated.
Adding and inserting a container
containers can be added and inserted simultaneously. Therefore, when
creating new containers, layout info and container info must be filled in. If
the selected container model is a mobile box-container, slot code manage-
ment must also be completed.

district.
3. Select and enter container information: container model, container
code, validation code, container direction, enter-node type, entrance
node number, current node number, return by default, full/empty sta-
tus, remarks.

It's pre-configured in Model > Container Model.
