5.6 Traffic control and path planning
5.6.1 Dependency relation between robots
Description
When the paths of robot A and B conflict, the robot that reaches the con-
flict area first usually executes its actions first, while the one that arrives
later usually executes its actions later. If A moves first and B moves later,
in KUKA.AMR Fleet, it is called that the actions of the robot B depend on
the completion of actions of robot A.
Long waits due to dependencies
If robot B experiences a long wait for robot A in a certain location, it may
be due to the following reasons:
• Robot A reports an error information on its path before the conflicted
area and has to wait to be recovered, causing a delay.
• Robot A arrives late due to inaccurate cost calculation.
• Robot A depends on robot C and robot C arrives late due to different
reasons, robot A will delay accordingly.
• The target of robot B conflicts with the path of robot A. If robot B ar-
rives at its target first and does not leave, robot A cannot pass
through the conflict area. Therefore, robot B should wait for robot A to
pass first.
Avoiding long waits
KUKA.AMR Fleet can trigger a dependency timeout re-planning in the
docker-compose.yml file under the environment of krcs-mission-
manager:
• To avoid long waits for robot B when robot A is in the above-men-
tioned scenario 2 and 3 with no error.
‒ Set the max wait time for robot B when robot A is normal, in milli-
seconds.
mission.planning.dependency.optimization.force.timeout:
 30000
‒ Set the number of robots that depend on robot B, which will trigger
its replan when robot A is normal. Set to 0 if re-planning is neces-
sary even if robot B does not affect the paths of other robots.
mission.planning.dependency.optimization.force.dependan
t-resource-count: 2
• To avoid long waits for robot B when robot A is in the above-men-
tioned first scenario.
‒ Set max wait time for robot B when robot A reports an error, in
milliseconds.
mission.planning.dependency.optimization.timeout: 5000
For all settings in docker-compose.yml , this document provides
the default value. If the entry does not exist, the default value is
used and the entry can be added and modified. To apply the new
settings, execute docker compose up -d  in command line.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 321/719
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 322 ===
5.6.2 Dependency reversal scenarios
Description
Current robot A depending on other robot B means robot A waits for robot
B to go first. If reversed, robot B will wait for robot A to go first.
In the following two scenarios the dependency can be reversed:
• Attempt to change the waiting relationships between robots to mini-
mize the total time spent by all robots.
• If errors occur due to conflicts with the target node of the current robot
or the starting nodes of other robots, or multiple robots experience
deadlocks or dependency cycles, then dependency reversal must be
used to calculate the path.
Prompted reasons for dependency reversal failure
If the path planning fails due to dependency reversal failure, the following
reasons will be prompted:
• Dependence reverse failed, action has been sent to dependent ro-
bot B.
‒ Meaning: Actions sent to robot B must be executed first and can-
not be retracted and wait for actions of robot A.
‒ Handling: No action is needed. Once robot B completes its actions
and is idle, robot A can find a path if there are no conflicts. If con-
flicts persist, robot B will be pushed away.
• Dependence reverse failed. Other robot B conflicts with the target
of current robot A.
‒ Meaning: The target node of robot A conflicts with the path of ro-
bot B. If robot A reaches its target node first, robot B cannot go
through its path. So robot B must go first.
‒ Handling: No action is needed, and wait for robot B to go first.
• Dependence reverse failed. Conflict with the start of other robot
B.
‒ Meaning: The starting node of robot B conflicts with the path of ro-
bot A. If robot A goes first, it will collide with robot B. So robot B
must go first.
‒ Handling: No action is needed, and wait for robot B to go first.
• Dependence reverse failed. Irreversible edge.
‒ Meaning: If the dependency originates from the order of queue no-
des and the relationship between the queue node and the worksta-
tion, such as the robot A moving from queue node 2 to queue
node 1, depending on the robot B moving from queue node 1 to
the workstation, this dependency cannot be inverted.
‒ Handling: No action is needed, and wait for the robot B to enter
the workstation and free up queue node 1.
5.6.3 Releasing dependency in advance
If the action of robot A moving from point 1 to point 2 depends on the ac-
tion of robot B moving from point 2 to point 3, and the edge from point 2
to point 3 is very long, robot A will have to wait for a long time.
Releasing dependency in advance means that robot A starts moving when
robot B has left point 2 by a distance X but has not yet reached point 3.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
322/719 | www.kuka.com KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 323 ===
The default value of X in meters can be modified in the environment of
krcs-mission-manager.
action.executing.safeDistance.value: 3
5.6.4 Queue nodes
Description
When no queue node is added to a node, it can only be the target node
of one robot such as robot A. If a second robot X is planned to go to this
node, a dependency reversal failure will be reported, indicating a conflict
with the target node of previous robot A.
To handle multiple tasks going to node 1, queue nodes 2, 3, 4, etc.,
should be set sequentially on the map for node 1. When the first robot
has not left node 1, subsequent robots will wait at the queue nodes in the
set order. For example, the fourth robot going to node 1 needs to pass
through 4 -> 3 -> 2 -> 1.
If queue nodes are added but the number of issued tasks exceeds the
number of queue nodes + 1, the error message Node X runs out of
queue node. No action is required, please wait for replanning.  will ap-
pear.
Precondition
• The distance between two adjacent queue nodes, and the distance
between the queue node and the target node, must ensure that the
actions of two robots at these nodes do not conflict.
Fig. 5-240: Queue node and target node distance
1 Distance between two adjacent queue nodes
2 Distance between the queue node and the target node
• If a robot needs to wait at a queue node with a load before entering
the target node, the distance between the queue node and the target
node, as well as between queue nodes, must consider the container
size to ensure that the actions of two robots at these nodes do not
conflict.
Adding queue nodes
The types of target nodes that support adding queue nodes include work-
station node, bracket node, rack node, conveyor node, and CTU loader
nodes. When drawing the map, add queue nodes in the function tab dur-
ing node editing. Ensure the orders of the queue nodes are correct. For
specific methods of adding queue nodes, refer to ( >>> 5.1.4.1 "Node
properties" Page 185 ) for details on functional properties.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 323/719
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 324 ===
Ordinary tasks and tasks to the target node of queue nodes
Task to the target node of queue nodes may experience long waiting time
at queue nodes and long working time at target nodes, which are not cal-
culated in the task time and may cause wrong dependency relations.
Thus, the paths of the two type of tasks should be drawn as independent-
ly as possible to prevent long waits of ordinary tasks.
• Blacklist mechanism:
as.graph-planner.use-waiting-blacklist: "true"
This can be configured in the docker-compose.yml file under the
environment of krcs-algorithm-service.
‒ "true": For queue node 2 and its target node 1, tasks whose target
node is not 1 will add node 2 and node 1 to the path planning
blacklist. If node 1 is a workstation, regardless of whether there
are queue nodes or not, node 1 will be added to the path planning
blacklist. So the paths of the two types of tasks will have fewer
common nodes and fewer dependency relations.
‒ Case: Passing queue nodes and their target node is not the
only path choice and choosing other paths will not cause a sig-
nificant cost increase.
‒ "false": Queue nodes and their target can be passed by any task
and the paths of two types of tasks may have more dependency
relations.
‒ Case: Passing queue nodes and their target has much less
cost than other paths or it is the only path. Usually used when
there are queue nodes and their target on a loop.
‒ Extra function: Assume that robot A plans to move to node 3
and robot B is moving to node 1 that is the target of queue
node 2. If the normal plan and robot-pushing trial of robot A
fail because of robot B, robot A can plan from start to node 1
first. After robot A arrives at node 1, it plans the path from
node 1 to node 3. This increases the probability of a success-
ful plan.
• Drawing requirements:
‒ Avoid drawing common nodes between queue nodes as much as
possible. If a common node 5 is drawn, and the order of nodes is
4 -> 5 -> 3 -> 2 -> 1, avoid having ordinary tasks whose path
needs to pass through the queue 4 -> 3 -> 2 -> 1. This may
cause dependency cycles, making it impossible to calculate the
path.
‒ Ensure that the distance between queue nodes and their target
nodes and surrounding nodes is large enough to avoid conflicts
between the path of tasks passing queue nodes and the path of
tasks passing surrounding nodes which are not queue nodes.
Queue node configuration
There are three functional configurations, each consisting of a "switch"
and "supported target node types":
• Queue jumping
• Following robot
• Using queue nodes as target nodes
These configurations are modified in the docker-compose.yml file un-
der the environment of krcs-algorithm-service.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
324/719 | www.kuka.com KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 325 ===
The "switch" is off by default, and the default target node types are work-
station (SINGLE_POINT_WORKSTATION), rack node (SHELF_POINT),
bracket node (HOLDER_POINT), pipeline relative node (PIPELINE_RELA-
TIVE_POINT), and CTU loader node (CTU_LOADER_NODE_POINT).
The "supported target node types" only take effect when the "switch" is
set to true.
• If the default target node types are empty, all five default types are
supported.
• If the default target node types are configured, only the configured tar-
get node types are supported.
Queue jumping
Queue jumping means that the order in which tasks are issued to a node
is independent of the order in which robots reach that target node.
Configuration:
as.mission.queue-jumping-config.enabled: "false"
as.mission.queue-jumping-config.node-types: 
SINGLE_POINT_WORKSTATION, SHELF_POINT, HOLDER_POINT, 
PIPELINE_RELATIVE_POINT, CTU_LOADER_NODE_POINT
Use:
• Queue jumping switch is false
‒ Meaning: Queue jumping is not allowed. The order in which tasks
are issued matches the order in which robots reach their target no-
des, regardless of the distance from the starting node to the target
node.
‒ Example: If tasks to target node 1 are issued to robot A, B, and C
in sequence, robot A goes directly to target node 1; robot B goes
to node 2 first and then to node 1; and robot C goes to node 3
first, then to node 2, and finally to node 1. If robot B encounters
an issue and stops at node 2, causing robot C to be canceled in a
cascade, robot C cannot jump the queue to go directly to 1 even if
robot A has left node 1 and robot C has a path from node 3 to
node 1. Robot C must check if there are any static robots at the
preceding queue nodes. If there are, it will report an error. Robot
C can only re-plan its path after robot B has recovered and plan-
ned its path.
‒ Errors:
‒ If the starting node of robot C is a common node:
Check the queuing robot to node 1. Wait for replanning if
there's no issue.
‒ If the starting node of robot C is a queue node:
Check the queuing robot in front to node 1. Continue wait-
ing for replanning if there's no issue.
‒ Scenario: Tasks with sequential steps, such as assembly, where
the second part must be installed before the third part, and the
third part cannot be used first.
• Queue jumping switch is true
‒ Meaning: Queue jumping is allowed. The order in which tasks are
issued is independent of the order in which robots reach their tar-
get nodes.
‒ Scenario: Tasks with no sequential requirements, such as trans-
porting identical goods, where task completion efficiency is priori-
tized. If a task in the queue encounters an issue or is canceled
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 325/719
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 326 ===
and needs re-planning, other tasks going to the target node can
still plan paths that bypass the problematic robot.
• Special scenarios: If there are common nodes between queue nodes
or between queue nodes and the end of the queue, the switch must
be true to allow queue jumping. Therefore, if queue jumping is not al-
lowed, do not insert common nodes in between.
‒ Example: If there is a common node 5, and the order of nodes is
4 -> 5 -> 3 -> 2 -> 1, and nodes 1, 2, and 3 are idle, robots A
and B at nodes 4 and 5, respectively, are going to 1. If the robot
at node 5 is canceled, it will cause a cascade cancellation for the
robot at node 4.
‒ If queue jumping is allowed, the robot at node 5 can re-plan its
path to 1. Then the robot at node 4 can also plan its path suc-
cessfully.
‒ If queue jumping is not allowed: The robot at node 5 will report
an error Check the queuing robot to node 1. Wait for re-
planning if there's no issue.  because there is a stationary ro-
bot at a queue node.
‒ If robot A can plan a path bypass robot B, robot A will go
to 1 first, and robot B will go to 1 later, resulting in queue
jumping, which contradicts the configuration.
‒ If robot B is on the only path of robot A, robot B needs to
be moved to create a path.
‒ If robot B can be pushed away node 5: robot A will
plan a path to 1 first, and then robot B will plan a path
to 1 later. The order is reversed, resulting in queue
jumping, which contradicts the configuration.
‒ If robot B cannot be pushed away from node 5: robot A
has no path. Robot A and robot B are deadlocked be-
cause they cannot calculate a path due to each other's
location.
Follow Robot
Follow Robot means that the robot doesn't need to stop at a node to wait
but can follow the previous robot closely. The actions between the target
of queue nodes and the queue node are independent, and the robot stops
at a safe distance from another robot using the obstacle avoidance func-
tion, not necessarily at a node, resulting in smaller robot spacing.
Precondition:
• The target node of queue node must support follow robot. Now only
the workstation node supports it on the map. For the method to con-
figure it on the map, refer to ( >>> 5.1.4.1 "Node properties" Page 185 )
for details on functional properties.
• The robot model must support Follow Robot.
• The actual distance between queue nodes at the workstation should
be at least greater than the robot length.
Configuration:
as.mission.follow-robot-config.enabled: "false" 
as.mission.follow-robot-config.node-types: 
SINGLE_POINT_WORKSTATION, SHELF_POINT, HOLDER_POINT, 
PIPELINE_RELATIVE_POINT, CTU_LOADER_NODE_POINT
Use:
• Follow robot switch is false
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
326/719 | www.kuka.com KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 327 ===
‒ Meaning: Follow robot is not allowed. Robots stop at nodes when
waiting.
‒ Scenario: Suitable for most scenarios when the current pace of
reaching the target node meets the takt time of the target node.
• Follow robot switch is true
‒ Meaning: Follow robot is allowed. The actions between the work-
station and the queue node are independent. Robots stop at a
safe distance from another robot using the obstacle avoidance
function, not necessarily at a node, resulting in smaller robot spac-
ing.
‒ Scenario: Suitable when the current pace of reaching the target
node does not meet the takt time of the target node. Common in
picking workstations, where the goods on one robot are picked im-
mediately after the goods of the previous robot are picked, pre-
venting long waiting times for the next robot to arrive.
‒ Requirement: Paths around the target of queue node should have
no conflicts with robots to that target. For example, in the figure
below, there is one path from queue nodes to workstation for ro-
bot1 (dashed). Robot2 (solid) moves on a parallel path and will ro-
tate near the target of queue node. If robot2 conflicts with robot1
while rotating and triggered an obstacle avoidance, robot1 will not
able to leave the target of queue nodes.
Fig. 5-241: Paths around the target of queue nodes
1 Robot 1 (Node 79 > 78)
2 Robot 2 (Node 95 > 94 > 110)
Using queue nodes as target nodes
Queue nodes can be set to use as target nodes.
Configuration:
as.mission.waiting-node-goal-config.enabled: "false" 
as.mission.waiting-node-goal-config.node-types: 
SINGLE_POINT_WORKSTATION, SHELF_POINT, HOLDER_POINT, 
PIPELINE_RELATIVE_POINT, CTU_LOADER_NODE_POINT
Use:
• Queue node as target switch is false
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 327/719
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 328 ===
‒ Meaning: Path planning does not support queue nodes as target
nodes. An error Goal X in a queue.  will be reported according to
the blacklist rules.
‒ Scenario: Suitable for most scenarios, as tasks are generally mis-
takenly sent to queue nodes.
• Queue node as target switch is true
‒ Meaning: Path planning supports queue nodes as target nodes.
‒ Scenario: Suitable for scenarios with high efficiency requirements
for the target node of queue nodes. When a queuing robot breaks
down, it needs to drop off the goods, be pushed away for repair,
and KUKA.AMR Fleet needs to send a task to the queue robot's
location immediately to lift the goods and continue queuing.
Differences between Queue Nodes and Temporary Storage Area
In some scenarios, Temporary Storage Area  can function as Queue No-
des. Refer to ( >>> 5.1.5 "Area management" Page 210 ) for details on the
temporary storage area type of the workflow area. But they have the fol-
lowing differences:
• Blacklist
‒ Difference: Queue nodes have blacklist restrictions, while the tem-
porary storage area does not. All tasks can pass through nodes
within the temporary storage area and its target node normally.
‒ Scenario: Use the temporary storage area when flexible paths that
can pass through queue node and target nodes are needed.
• Task order
‒ Difference:
‒ When queue jumping is not supported, tasks at queue nodes
are executed in the order they are issued.
‒ In the temporary storage area, the first two tasks are executed
in order:
The first goes directly to the target node, and the second must
first go to the node with the highest priority within the tempora-
ry storage area, then poll the target node to see if it is free be-
fore generating a task to go to the target node.
The order of subsequent tasks is independent of the order in
which they reach the target node. For example, if the first
robot leaves the target node and the second robot has not yet
occupied the target node, the third task can go directly to the
target node.
‒ Scenario: Use queue nodes and do not allow queue jumping when
tasks need to be executed in order.
Special scenarios
When a robot needs to reach the target node in order, and the queue no-
des and target nodes are the only paths for common tasks, there are two
ways to handle:
• Map editing method : Connect the node before the queue node and
the node after the target node of queue nodes, creating a parallel path
that bypasses the queue nodes and its target node. If possible, avoid
overlapping the original edges. This may result in longer waiting times
depending on the task to the target of queue nodes.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
328/719 | www.kuka.com KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 329 ===
• WCS process modification method : Split tasks passing through the
queue node or its target node into two tasks: One to the queue node
or the target of queue node and another task to its real target.
If the task first goes to the queue node, in docker-compose.yml
under the environment of krcs-mission-manager, set as.mis-
sion.waiting-node-goal-config.enabled to "true".
5.6.5 Robot pushing
Description
Automatic robot pushing will be used when the KUKA.AMR Fleet cannot
calculate the path of the current robot due to certain errors, and the robot
pushing feature is enabled.
Errors to trigger robot pushing
• No path to Goal node X.
• Check the queuing robot to node X. Wait for replanning if there's no
issue.
• Check the queuing robot in front to node X. Continue waiting for re-
planning if there's no issue.
• Dependence reverse failed. Conflict with the target of current robot B.
• Dependence reverse failed. Conflict with the start of other robot B.
• Dependence reverse failed. Irreversible edge.
• Conflict with static robot X.
• Queue node/Goal X has an obstacle.
Configuration
The following configurations are modified in the docker-compose.yml
file.
• Switch: Whether to allow robot pushing where "true" means allowed.
‒ Configuration in the environment of krcs-algorithm-serv-
ice.
as.graph-planner.push-idle-robots: "true"
‒ Configuration in the environment of krcs-mission-manager.
mission.robotPushing.enable: "true"
The two configurations need to have the same value.
• Robot Pushing Target Types : The types of nodes that can be used
as target nodes, configured in the environment of krcs-algo-
rithm-service section.
‒ For scenarios with shelf parking nodes, add SHELF_POINT.
‒ For scenarios with many long channels, remove COMMON_POINT
to prevent multiple robot pushings for one robot and long waits for
pushing tasks to long channels.
as.graph-planner.push-target-node-type: COMMON_POINT, 
AVOIDANCE_POINT, REST_POINT
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 329/719
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 330 ===
• Push Target Avoid Other Robot's Path : Whether the target of a
pushed robot avoids other robots' paths, configured in the environ-
ment of krcs-algorithm-service section.
When the targets of pushed robots are typically on main roads where
push-target-node-type usually includes COMMON_POINT, the tar-
get may lie on another robot's path. The current robot must wait for
others to move first. If other robots are still far from the conflict area,
both the pushed robot and its pushing robot must wait for an extended
period. Avoiding paths of other robots may require pushing the robot
further but saves time.
‒ Switch
as.graph-planner.push-avoid-other-path.enable: "false"
‒ "true": Avoid paths of other robots.
‒ "false": No need to avoid paths of other robots.
‒ Applied range when switch is set to "true"
as.graph-planner.push-avoid-other-path.for-all-nodes: 
"false"
‒ "true": Applies to all nodes.
‒ "false": Applies only to enabled "No Push Onto Path" areas.
• Pushed Robot Moves First : When the current robot has a dependen-
cy relationship with a pushed robot, whether to make this relationship
irreversible (prioritizing the pushed robot's movement). Configured in
the environment of krcs-algorithm-service section.
as.graph-planner.push-move-first: "false"
‒ "true": The pushed robot moves first. When push-avoid-other-
path is not enabled for all nodes, a subsequent robot passing the
pushed robot's target will conflict and must reverse to move first.
With irreversible dependencies, the later robot reports a "no path"
error and waits for replanning. This ensures the pushed robot and
its original pushing robot move first, avoiding prolonged waits for
main road traffic.
‒ "false": The pushed robot moves later. When push-avoid-oth-
er-path is enabled for all nodes or push-target-node-type
typically excludes COMMON_POINT, targets usually are not on oth-
ers' paths. Reversible dependencies allow the system to find opti-
mal dependency relationships between robots.
• No-Push Target Area : The blacklist for target nodes of robot pushing.
These nodes can only be passed through, not used as target nodes of
robot pushing. Refer to ( >>> 5.1.5 "Area management" Page 210 ).
• No-Push Area: Robots in this area cannot be pushed away. Refer to
(>>> 5.1.5 "Area management" Page 210 ).
• No Push Onto Path Area : Robots in this area cannot be pushed onto
paths of other robots when as.graph-planner.push-avoid-oth-
er-path.enable: "true"  and as.graph-planner.push-
avoid-other-path.for-all-nodes: "false" . However, they
can still pass through paths of others robots. Refer to
(>>> 5.1.5 "Area management" Page 210 ).
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
330/719 | www.kuka.com KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 331 ===
• Allow Pushing Queued Robots in Mission Manager : Configured in
the environment of krcs-mission-manager and "true" means en-
abled.
For scenarios with multiple tasks issued in a short time, KUKA.AMR
Fleet calculates a maximum of five tasks at a time by default. Subse-
quent tasks in mission manager are queued for future planning but
may be assigned to robots. If such robots block the path, they can al-
so be pushed to increase the possibility for other robots to find a path.
Therefore, this setting is usually kept with its default value "true".
mission.robotPushing.push-planning-in-queue.enable: "true"
• Allow Pushing Replanning Robot : Configured in the environment
of krcs-mission-manager and "true" means pushing is enabled.
When a robot encounters a planning error or a cancellation caused by
another robot, it will wait for replanning while remaining stationary.
mission.robotPushing.push-planning-error.enable: "true"
Scenarios for different settings are as follows:
‒ "false": The robot has a higher probability of finding a valid path
during replanning, so manual pushing is typically not needed. How-
ever, due to limited number of target points, relocating the robot
might result in a longer route to the original destination, potentially
causing task timeouts.
‒ "true": If replanning fails to find a path for a robot with planning er-
rors, waiting becomes ineffective. Pushing increases the current ro-
bot's chance to find a path. This setting is preferable when numer-
ous pushable target points exist, as it reduces timeout risks.
Robot pushing results
The results of robot pushing are as follows:
• The path for the current robot A is the shortest after removing the ro-
bots that can be pushed.
• Only robots B, C, and D that conflict with the path of the current robot
need to be pushed. They will be moved to the nearest node that
meets the robot pushing configuration.
• If A cannot find a place to push B, A will have no path, and A will re-
port Need to push agent B, but it has no path so the original task
no path {the no path reason} .
• The robot pushing path has no conflict with other stationary robots E
and F. Therefore, if the only pushable path of robot B is blocked by
robot E, robot B will not push robot E in a chain but report the
above error.
• To prevent the no path situation for subsequent plans after robot
pushing, it is necessary to check the connectivity of all target nodes
of robot pushing to prevent those nodes from being entry-only no-
des, including:
‒ Whether the node support rotation.
‒ The robot direction on the outgoing edge of the node.
‒ Load restrictions on the node.

KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 331/719
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 332 ===
5.6.6 Rack rotation
Description
When the target node is set with both the robot stop angle and the con-
tainer stop angle, the robot may perform relative or absolute rotations dur-
ing path planning.
A single normal path planning may sometimes fail to find a path with cor-
rect stop orientations for both the robot and the rack. In such cases, a
rack rotation node is used. The robot first moves to the rack rotation node
and adjusts either the robot or the rack to the appropriate angle at this
node, ensuring a suitable path planning with correct target stop angles.
Precondition
• Rack rotation only supports top-lifting rotating robot types (LIFT_RO-
TATE), including relative rotation types, such as KMP 600i, and abso-
lute rotation types, such as KMP 1500P-EU-D. The two types have dif-
ferent rack rotation behaviors.
Relative rotation robot types
Whether the robot performs a relative or absolute rotation depends on the
rotation type set at the node. When normal path planning cannot satisfy
both the robot and container target stop angle, the robot first plans a path
to the rack rotation node. At the rack rotation node, the lifting mechanism
rotates to adjust the rack to the appropriate angle (static robot and rotat-
ing rack). Finally, the robot plans a path from the rack rotation node to the
target node.
Absolute rotation robot types
The angle between the robot and the rack remains unchanged during
movement, and the rack can only rotate with the robot performing abso-
lute rotation. When the stop angle of the robot and the rack does not
meet the requirement at the target node, the robot adjusts the angle be-
tween itself and the rack at the rack rotation node through a combination
of actions (drop the rack, rotate the robot, and lift the rack). For example,
if the initial angle of the robot and rack is 0 degree, and the target node
requires the robot to be at 90 degrees and the rack at 0 degree, the robot
will first move to the rack rotation node with the rack, drop the rack, rotate
90 degrees, then lift the rack and move to the target node.
Rack rotation node configuration
Map setting:
Set up rack rotation nodes on the map according to on-site requirements.
Rack rotation nodes are the original "rotation nodes."
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
332/719 | www.kuka.com KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 333 ===
Fig. 5-242: Setting rack rotation node
Configurations:
The following settings are available in docker-compose.yml file under
the environment of krcs-mission-manager.
• Rack rotation node selection strategy: Options are start, target, total.
as.graph-planner.turntable.min-cost-for: total
‒ start: Select the rack rotation node with the minimum cost from
the starting node to the rack rotation node.
Scenario: When the rack must enter the main road from the rack
node at the correct angle (e.g., the narrow side of the rack face
forward on the main road), the nearest rack rotation node to the
starting node should be found for rack rotation. Typically, rack rota-
tion nodes need to be set near the starting node.
‒ target: Select the rack rotation node with the minimum cost from
the rack rotation node to the target node.
Scenario: When the rack needs to maintain a certain angle while
moving on the main road, rotate it before entering the target node.
Typically, rack rotation nodes need to be set near the target node.
‒ total: Select the rack rotation node with the minimum total cost
from the starting node to the rack rotation node and then to the
target node.
Scenario: When the rack rotation nodes on the map are not close
to either the starting node or the target node, it is recommended to
choose this option.
• Consider other robots as obstacles: When selecting rack rotation no-
des, whether the current state of static robots and target state of ro-
bots with plans are considered as obstacles.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 333/719
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 334 ===
as.graph-planner.turntable.consider-others-as-obstacles: 
"true"
‒ "true": When selecting rack rotation nodes, other robots are con-
sidered as obstacles, and KUKA.AMR Fleet selects an accessible
rack rotation node.
‒ Advantage: Lower probability of no path.
‒ Disadvantage: If the shortest path has robot obstacles, a rack
rotation node with a higher path cost is chosen, although the
obstacle robot could potentially be pushed away.
‒ Scenario: When the rack rotation node on the path with the
lowest cost has an obstacle robot that cannot be pushed, the
system can choose the rack rotation node resulting in the sec-
ond lowest cost, avoiding the situation of no path.
‒ "false": When selecting rack rotation nodes, other robots are ignor-
ed, and KUKA.AMR Fleet chooses the theoretically lowest cost
rack rotation node.
‒ Advantage: The rack rotation node with the theoretically lowest
cost can be chosen.
‒ Disadvantage: If there are obstacle robots on the only path, no
path can be planned.
‒ Scenario: When finding the shortest path is desired.
• Whether all rotation angles should be tried when calculating the path.
It is one of the standards for breaking the loop during rotation angle
checks. There are 4 rotation angles to check at the turntable node: 0°,
90°, 180° and -90°.
 as.planner-service.try-all-angles-for-turning-table: 
"false"
‒ "true": All available rotation angles are tried to find a path. The
path with the minimum cost will be used.
‒ Advantage: The path with the minimum cost is found.
‒ Disadvantage: It takes more time.
‒ Scenario:
‒ The map is simple and small so trying all angles won't take
much time.
‒ Cost difference between paths is significant and the unopti-
mized path may cause the task to be overdue.
‒ "false": After the first available path of a rotation angle is found,
the calculation breaks.
‒ Advantage: Less calculation.
‒ Disadvantage: The cost of the path may not be the minimum.
‒ Scenario:
‒ For complex large maps where trying all angles will be
time-consuming, which may delay the movement of the ro-
bot.
‒ Finding the optimized path is not required.
• For the second sub-plan (from the turntable node to the target),
whether having no collision with others serves as one of the standards
for breaking the loop during rotation angles checks.
as.graph-planner.turntable.sub-plan2-collision-check: 
"true"
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
334/719 | www.kuka.com KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 335 ===
‒ "true": The collision of the second sub-plan with other paths needs
to be checked.
Logic:
‒ If a second sub-plan with no collision is found and try-all-
angles-for-turning-table is set to "false", use this
angle and stop checking remaining rotation angles.
‒ If the second sub-plan with current rotation angle has collision
with other robots, proceed to the next rotation angle.
‒ After all four angles are checked but all have collisions, select
the one with the minimum cost.
Scenario: The map is small or has few tasks, allowing sufficient
time to find an optimal path without delaying other tasks.
‒ "false": No need to check collisions with other paths. Only try-
all-angles-for-turning-table determines the loop termina-
tion.
Scenario: The map is large or has many tasks, requiring faster
pathfinding where optimality is not critical.
• The cost ratio from the turntable node to the target when min-cost-
for is set to total. When multiple paths share the same minimum
total cost (start > turntable > target), this parameter defines the prefer-
ence:
as.graph-planner.turntable.total-cost-ratio: 0.5001
‒ Total cost = start * (1 - ratio) + target * ratio.
‒ Ratio = 0.4999: Selects the turntable node closest to the start
node.
‒ Ratio = 0.5001: Selects the turntable node closest to the target
node.
‒ Default rationale: With no user preference, 0.5001 prioritizes short-
er second sub-plan paths, which reduces calculation time for all
four angles.
Explanation of rack rotation related exceptions
• Input parameter validation :
‒ When the robot stop angle of the task does not match that of the
target node on the map, a related error No path since robot stop
angles mismatch between task and graph  is reported. For ex-
ample, if a task is issued with a robot stop angle of 0 degrees, but
the robot stop angle of the target node is 90 degrees.
• Rack angle adjustment : If the rack angle needs to be adjusted but
no rack rotation node is available, an error No path satisfies contain-
er goal angle from request  is reported.
5.6.7 Weight
Description
Weight: Action time × weight = cost. The default value is 1. And the
weight can only be increased, resulting in a cost that is higher than the
actual action time. During path planning, the algorithm selects the path
with the minimum cost.
Dynamic Weight: Instead of manually adjusting the weight of edges on
the map, the algorithm automatically adjusts the weights of various paths
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 335/719
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 336 ===
based on the current occupancy of all paths on the map. This strategy
aims to achieve path avoidance and reduce waiting times between robot
paths.
Weight calculation method configuration
This is the method for calculating path weights which in the docker-
compose.yml file under the environment of krcs-mission-manager.
as.mission-graph.weight-calculation-method: DYNAMIC_COST
The options are MANUAL, DYNAMIC_WEIGHT and DYNAMIC_COST.
• MANUAL:
‒ Meaning: During path planning, only the weights configured for
each edge on the map are used to calculate the cost.
‒ Scenario: When there are multiple paths between two areas, the
paths can be planned not according to the rule of the minimum
path cost but according to the rules set in the channels. The path
selection can be changed by modifying the weight parameter of
the edges in the channel.
For example, if upper and lower areas are connected by left and
right dual channels and right-hand driving is desired, the weight of
the edges in the left channel going upwards should be set to a
larger value, and the weight of the edges in the right channel go-
ing downwards should be set to a larger value.
Normally, robots will follow the right-hand driving rule, moving up-
wards in the right channel and downwards in the left channel.
However, if one side of the main road is blocked by a robot, the
two robots can still use the other path, avoiding the inflexibility of
one-way channels.
• DYNAMIC_WEIGHT:
‒ Meaning: The weights are automatically adjusted based on the
spatial conflict in the path of all robots without considering tempo-
ral conflicts. Spatial conflicts mean path overlap and temporal con-
flicts mean that the two robots will meet at a certain node on the
path. By calculating the occupancy of edges on the map (how of-
ten they are used and occupied by other robots), the weights are
changed based on edge usage. The more an edge is used by oth-
er robots in a single path planning, the more dependencies and
waiting times may occur on the action of that edge. By automati-
cally increasing the cost of that edge, that edge is avoided to re-
duce waiting times.
‒ Scenario: When upper and lower areas are connected by left and
right dual channels, if the robot 1 is moving upwards in the right
channel, without manually changing the edge weights, the left
channel will be selected by the robot 2 during the downward path
planning.
• DYNAMIC_COST:
‒ Meaning: The weights are automatically adjusted based on both
spatial and temporal conflicts in the paths of all robots. Consider-
ing both conflicts can narrow the calculated conflict interval, avoid-
ing unnecessary detours.
Due to various factors affecting the actual movement speed of
robots, the calculation may differ from the actual time two robots
reach the predicted meeting node. This calculation method may
be inaccurate and unsuitable for complex large maps.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
336/719 | www.kuka.com KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026
Programming and configuration


=== PDF PAGE 337 ===
‒ Scenario: When upper and lower areas are connected by left and
right dual channels, if robot 1 is moving upwards in the right main
road, and robot 2 is planning a path downwards:
‒ If two robots are predicted to meet in the channel, the path
through the left main road will be selected
‒ If two robots are not predicted to meet in the channel, either
the left or right main road may be selected (if the right path
downwards is the shortest, KUKA.AMR Fleet will select the
right path).
Weight calculation mixed method configuration
If the setting above is not MANUAL, whether to use the weight on the
edge at the same time can be set.
as.mission-graph.weight-calculation-mixed: true
• "true": Use the weight on edges. Thus, both the manual preference
and the cost of conflicts are considered.
• "false": Don't use the weight on edge and consider the weight as 1.
Thus, manual preference will be ignored.
5.6.8 Avoid node
Description
If two robots are moving towards each other in the same channel, only
one can pass and the other needs to avoid in advance.
• If there are no branches on the channel, robots need to avoid others
before entering the channel.
• If there are branches, the following settings in the docker-com-
pose.yml file under the environment of krcs-algorithm-serv-
ice need to be configured to use nodes on branches of a channel for
avoidance path calculation.
Avoid Node Function Switch
This switch controls the single-channel avoid node function. Enabling this
function will result in additional computation during path planning, so it
should only be enabled when necessary.
as.graph-planner.single-channel-avoidance.enabled: "false"
• Meaning: The switch to use nodes on branches or not.
• "true": Use nodes on branches.
• "false": Do not use nodes on branches.
Avoid Node Target Point Selection
This specifies which types of nodes can be used as avoid nodes.
as.graph-planner.single-channel-avoidance.use-avoidance-
points: "false"
• Meaning: The type of node to use for nodes on branches.
• "true": Only use avoidance nodes.
• "false": Use all types of nodes.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 337/719
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 338 ===
In some project sites where there is not enough space to set up
"Avoid Node". Normal "Node" beside intersections or in front of
working spots can be used for avoidance.
Drawing avoid nodes
1. Select a path node on the passage and add an avoid node next to it.
2. Use bidirectional edges to connect the path node and the avoid node.
3. Check whether the parameters of the newly added avoid node and
edges are reasonable.
• The edges cannot be set to move backward, or set for device inter-
action.
• The node type must be the avoid node, and the rotation type of the
avoid node and the adjacent path node must be the same.
• Ensure the distance between the avoid node and the adjacent path
node is large enough to prevent collisions due to insufficient spac-
ing.
5.6.9 Imported curve angle tolerance
The system supports importing xmap files generated by a third-party draw-
ing tool. However, an imported curve and its reversed curve may not be a
100% match where the start angle of a curve and the target angle of the
reversed curve are different, so they cannot be used for generating avoid
steps. The allowed angle difference (in radian) can be set here to consid-
er the two curves as a matched pair.
as.bezier-curve.imported-curve-angle-tolerance: 0
• Unit: Radian
• Scenario: Modify this parameter when importing an xmap file, instead
of drawing the map manually.
The following robots support xmap usage:
• AM15
• BasiclineA42-SLAM
• KMC 30-5
• KMP 140i-CL-AWG50
• KMP 300i-1 diffDrive
• KMP 600i-XG-1 diffDrive

• SFL-MP10S
• KMF 1500i-PJ
• KMF 2000i-RT

5.6.10 Obstacle avoidance
Description
When a robot meets an obstacle based on the OA plan but the safety
PLC of the robot is not triggered:
• If the obstacle is removed, it can continue its path.
• If the obstacle is not removed, it has two ways to avoid the obstacle
and continue its task automatically:
‒ One is to generate a local path which is not on the map to bypass
the obstacle.
‒ The other is to find another path based on the map starting by
moving backward or rotating to another direction.
When the robot is too close to the obstacle and its safety PLC is trig-
gered, manual intervention is required to restore it to a normal state, as it
cannot recover automatically.
Method 1: Bypass with a Local Curve Not on the Map
Precondition:
• In the robot type parameters, support bypass obstacles  = Yes.
• The obstacle is met during movement on an edge with the parameter
Auto Obstacle Avoidance  = enabled.
Method 1 doesn't handle obstacles encountered during rotation.
Procedure:
1. The navigation module on the robot generates the path from the cur-
rent location to the next node in the original path based on the detec-
ted surrounding spaces.
2. The algorithm service calculates dependency relations and verifies the
path for errors.
• If no error is found, the robot follows the path based on the de-
pendency relations.
• If there are errors, navigation module will regenerate the path.
3. While moving, if meeting an obstacle again, the navigation module will
generate the bypass path again.
Method 2: Find Another Path Based on the Map
• Meeting an obstacle while rotating : The next edge will be marked
as blocked and added to the edge blacklist. Then the new path only
uses other edges. However, the path may still start with a rotation in
the same direction, potentially encountering the obstacle again. Subse-
quent replanning may find no viable path, so there is a possibility of
no path using this method.
• Meeting an obstacle on an edge : This edge is marked as blocked
and added to the edge blacklist. If the robot rotates, it may get closer
to the obstacle. Therefore, the first step of its path is to move on the
reversed blocked edge in the opposite direction to the node before en-
countering the obstacle, thus moving further away from the obstacle.
Configuration in docker-compose.yml
• Configuration under the environment of krcs-algorithm-serv-
ice:
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 339/719
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 340 ===
as.topology-graph.move-backward-on-reversed-blocked-edge: 
"true"
‒ Meaning: Whether to move backward on the reversed blocked
edge when encountering an obstacle.
‒ "true": Use the method.
‒ "false": Don't use the method.
as.topology-graph.create-reversed-blocked-edge: "true"
‒ Meaning: Whether to create a reversed blocked edge of the
blocked edge when it does not exist.
‒ "true": Create one.
‒ "false": Don't create one.
• Configuration under the environment of krcs-mission-manager:
mission.planning.obstacle.optimization.enable: "true"
‒ Meaning: Enable method 2 or not.
‒ "true": Enable.
‒ "false": Disable.
mission.planning.obstacle.optimization.timeout: 10000
‒ Meaning: The time (in ms) between meeting an obstacle and using
method 2, used to wait for the obstacle to be removed or for meth-
od 1 to solve the problem.
mission.planning.obstacle.optimization.retry.max: 10
‒ Meaning: The max replan times for one task to use method 2. Ob-
stacles may still be encountered on the path generated by method
2, requiring multiple uses of the method.
mission.planning.obstacle.optimization.ignore-obstacle-
avoidance: "true"
‒ Meaning: If the method 1 is used and the robot meets obstacle
again, the second bypass path of method 1 may fail in its genera-
tion or verification. The current position of the robot may be far
from the previous node and edge. This setting decides whether
the method 2 is still used or not.
‒ "true": Ignore the method 2 in this situation (don't use method 2).
‒ "false": Don't ignore the method 2 in this situation (still use method
2).
5.6.11 Max cost for crossing an obstacle
When planning a path, the current node of static robots or the target node
of other robots will be considered as an obstacle, and crossing them will
add extra cost to consider the possible waiting. Therefore, a robot may
choose a path with less obstacles. However, the added cost can also be
set under the environment of krcs-algorithm-service with the unit
in seconds.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
340/719 | www.kuka.com KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 341 ===
as.mission-graph.max-cost-for-cross-obstacle: null
• Unit: second
• Default value: null means that no value is set, and the system will
use a value larger than the total edge length of the map. This results
in a large cost, so robots only choose it when it is the only path.
• Scenario: When one path is short and other paths are much longer,
for example, the map has a large loop and the short path is a small
section of the loop, choosing the current path to push the obstacle ro-
bot when it becomes idle or wait for the obstacle robot to plan first,
may not cost much time. In such situation, the value can be set to a
small number, like 15. It can be modified based on the extra cost of
the longer path, which users do not want the robot to use.
5.6.12 Moving between different sections of the same floor
Different sections of the same floor may be connected directly or via other
floors.
Configure the following settings for different situations in the docker-
compose.yml file under the environment of krcs-algorithm-serv-
ice:
as.graph-planner.check-single-graph: "true"
• "true": In most cases, different sections of the same floor are connec-
ted directly and the algorithm only needs to explore nodes in the
same floor to find a path.
• "false": In some buildings, different sections are not connected directly.
For example, a robot needs to take a lift L1 from section S1 of floor
F1 to floor F2 and then take lift L2 from F2 to section S2 of floor F1.
So, with this setting, the algorithm can explore nodes in other floors to
find a path.
5.6.13 Tugger path
For a tugger, if no trailers are attached, it can move forward and back-
ward. If trailers are attached, the robot can't move backward and the fol-
lowing parameter in the docker-compose.yml file under the environ-
ment of krcs-algorithm-service can be configured for path plan-
ning.
as.graph-planner.tugging-planning.soft-link: "true"
• Meaning: Whether the trailers are linked to each other and to the
head robot softly or not. Soft link means the attached point of trailers
is not fixed and can rotate so the heading of each trailer and the head
robot can be different on a curve.
• "true": Soft link. All existing tugger use soft-link.
• "false": Hard link.
as.graph-planner.tugging-planning.tugging-padding: 0.2
• Meaning: Extra padding for the tugger in special situations.
‒ The tugger may start on a curve, but it is always considered
straight in the initial state. Use padding to create a larger rectan-
gular envelope that covers the curved trajectory.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 341/719
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 342 ===
‒ The tugger may need to recover to an edge. As the recovery
movement is unknown, use the padding to increase the envelope
of the robot to cover the envelope of the actual movement.
• Scenario:
‒ It needs to be changed larger if the tugger finds a path but detects
other robots as an obstacle. It may happen when the tugger is
long.
‒ It needs to be changed smaller if two robots can move together as
designed but due to the large padding, robot cannot plan a path.
as.mission-graph.tugger-recover-angle: 10
• Meaning: The maximum recoverable angle difference between the tug-
ger angle and the target orientation. A larger angle difference will re-
quire the tugger to rotate to recover, but a tugger cannot rotate so that
angle cannot be used. Modify the value based on the tugger's ability.
5.6.14 Node accuracy
The accuracy when a robot stops on a node.
Default settings
as.mission-graph.trans-tracking-accuracy: 0.1
• Meaning: Distance between the robot and the node in meter. It should
be greater or equal to the control and localization error for SLAM and
QR code navigation that are 0.05 m and 0.03 m now.
as.mission-graph.rotatory-tracking-accuracy: 1
• Meaning: Angle difference between the robot heading and the availa-
ble angles on the node that collects settings on the node and all pos-
sible angles from its incoming and outgoing edges.
Settings on the graph
These settings are used when the robot needs to exceed the default set-
tings. It happens when racks/goods are manually put on the ground or
shelves inaccurately. As the robot needs to move to the center of the car-
go by aligning with its bottom QR code, the robot will not be at the node
accurately either.
as.topology-graph.use-node-accuracy.enabled: "true"
• "true": Use the setting on the graph.
as.topology-graph.use-node-accuracy.node-types: 
SINGLE_POINT_WORKSTATION, SHELF_POINT, HOLDER_POINT
• Meaning: The list of the node types that will use the node accuracy on
the graph. The default node types are used because racks/goods are
usually put on those nodes.
5.6.15 Ignorable angle differences between connected edges
as.mission-graph.ignorable-angle-difference: 3
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
342/719 | www.kuka.com KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 343 ===
• Meaning: If the angle differences between two connecting edges are
less or equal to the setting, no rotation will be generated and the robot
will align its angle to the second edge gradually during movement,
which decreases the translation time.
• Scenario:
‒ The corridor is curved, which can be simulated by small straight
edges with a little angle difference. Without rotation, the robot can
move without stop.
‒ The common node of the two edges does not allow rotations, but
they have an angle difference. For example, the shelf node.
• Change: Increase the value if a larger angle difference needs to be ig-
nored.
5.6.16 Pre-allocation
Description
Pre-allocation refers to the process after the mission is issued, where
KUKA.AMR Fleet, based on the conditions of the mission issuance,
matches the robot with the specified robtType and robotId that has the
lowest path cost, appending the mission to this robot so that after com-
pleting the previous mission, the robot can continue to execute the current
mission, thereby improving the operational efficiency of the robot.
Related function configuration
Configuration in the docker-compose.yml file under the environment
of krcs-mission-manager:
• Switch for the pre-allocation function. "true" means on, "false" means
off. Default is on.
mission.dispatching.resource.pre-allocation.allowed: 
"true"
• Whether the mission appended to the robot can be replaced by higher
score missions. Default value is true, indicating that replacement is al-
lowed.
mission.dispatching.resource.queue-replace.enable: "true"
• The weight of the mission creation time element (one of three ele-
ments) in calculating mission score when the robot replaces an addi-
tional mission. The default value is 0.1. The configurable value range
is 0~1, where a higher value indicates a greater weight.
mission.dispatching.queue-replace.weight.createTime: 0.1
• The weight of the specified mission priority element (one of three ele-
ments) in calculating mission score when the robot replaces an addi-
tional mission. The default value is 0.5. The configurable value range
is 0~1, where a higher value indicates a greater weight.
mission.dispatching.queue-replace.weight.priority: 0.5
• The weight of the minimum path cost element (one of three elements)
in calculating mission score when the robot replaces an additional mis-
sion. The default value is 0.4. The configurable value range is 0~1,
where a higher value indicates a greater weight.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 343/719
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 344 ===
mission.dispatching.queue-replace.weight.pathCost: 0.4
Priority
Based on the above configurations, the system defaults to enabling the
pre-allocation function, and only supports adding one additional mission to
the robot while it is performing a mission.
When a new mission is issued, if the robot that has been appended is the
most suitable, the system supports replacing the mission already appen-
ded to that robot with the new mission, making the new mission the ap-
pended mission for the robot.
Whether a new mission should replace a mission already assigned to a
robot depends on three factors:
• Mission creation time
• Specified mission priority
• Minimum path cost for the robot to reach the mission's target node
By default, the system prioritizes missions based on the following order:
• Specified mission priority: Missions with higher priority are considered
first.
• Minimum path cost: Missions with lower path consumption are consid-
ered next.
• Mission creation time: Missions created earlier are considered last.
The formula for calculating the total score of a mission is as follows:
Mission Total Score = (Mission score for Mission Priority  x Weight of
Mission Priority) + (Mission score for Path Cost  x Weight of Path Cost)
+ (Mission score for Creation Time  x Weight of Creation Time)
The score of a single mission for a specific factor depends on its ranking
within the current batch. Assuming the batch size is n, the mission ranked
first will receive a score of n, while the mission ranked last will receive a
score of 1.
If the business requirement is to strictly follow the rule of executing mis-
sions in the order they were created, it is recommended to adjust the con-
figuration as follows:
mission.dispatching.queue-replace.weight.createTime: 1
mission.dispatching.queue-replace.weight.priority: 0
mission.dispatching.queue-replace.weight.pathCost: 0
mission.dispatching.batch.size.max: 1
Restriction
When a robot is executing a mission or job with uncertain duration, the
system will not automatically add missions to that robot. That is, pre-allo-
cation is not applicable in the following scenarios:
• In Workflow Config, the "Trigger Handling" is not set to "automatic de-
lay trigger".
• In Workflow Config, the "Lock robot after workflow finished" is selec-
ted.
• In Workflow Config, the "Trigger Handling" is set to "automatic delay
trigger" while the "Delay Time" is set to a value greater than 0.
• In Workflow Config, "Device Command" with "callback required" is con-
figured at the final node.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
344/719 | www.kuka.com KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 345 ===
• The workflow has multiple nodes. When the robot is executing mis-
sions at non-final nodes, new missions will not be appended.
• When the missions enable the append function, the pre-allocation
function is not applicable.
Case
• Job 1: Schedule the robot to move to position 23, and release the ro-
bot after execution. This can be broken down into the robot's mission:
moveTo23.
• Job 2: Schedule the robot to move to position 25, lift the container,
move to position 39, and place the container, then release the robot
after execution. This can be broken down into the robot's mission:
moveTo25, liftUp, moveTo39, liftDown.
Job 1 is triggered first, and the system schedules AMR#1 to execute. Dur-
ing the execution of moveTo23 by AMR#1, Job 2 is triggered.
When assigning robots for Job 2, through calculation, AMR#1 is found to
be closer to point 25 than AMR#2, so the mission moveTo25 from Job 2
is appended to AMR#1.
After AMR#1 completes the moveTo23 mission and is released from Job
1, it will continue to execute the missions from Job 2.
5.6.17 Re-allocation
Description
Re-allocation refers to the situation where, after missions have been as-
signed to a robot, other robots in the field become idle or the current ro-
bot encounters an exception (such as path planning failure/robot reports
an advanced exception). The system, through calculation, attempts to re-
assign the missions to a more suitable robot.
Related function configuration
Configuration in the docker-compose.yml file under the environment
of krcs-mission-manager:
• Switch for re-allocation function. Default is “true”, indicating that the re-
allocation function is enabled.
mission.dispatching.review.allowed: "true"
• The waiting time until robot reassignment after a high-level exception
for a mission-allocated robot. Default is 30000 milliseconds. If a robot
reports a high-level exception during mission execution, mission reas-
signment will be triggered after 30 seconds.
mission.dispatching.review.timeout: 30000
• When a robot is idle, it will be preferentially allocated to higher-priority
missions. This configuration is one of the factors for calculating mis-
sion score: the weight of “mission creation time”. The default value is
0.1. The configuration value can be 0~1, with a higher value indicating
a greater weight.
mission.dispatching.review.weight.createTime: 0.1
• When a robot is idle, it will be preferentially allocated to higher-priority
missions. This configuration is one of the factors for calculating mis-
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 345/719
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 346 ===
sion score: the weight of “assigned mission priority”. The default value
is 0.5. The configuration value can be 0~1, with a higher value indicat-
ing a greater weight.
mission.dispatching.review.weight.priority: 0.5
• When a robot is idle, it will be preferentially allocated to higher-priority
missions. This configuration is one of the factors for calculating mis-
sion score: the weight of the "Minimum Path Cost from Robot to mis-
sion Destination." The default value is 0.4. The configurable value
range is 0~1, with a higher value indicating a greater weight.
mission.dispatching.review.weight.pathCost: 0.4
• The number of times the system will attempt to re-plan the path for
the current robot after the initial mission path planning fails. If this
number is exceeded, the mission will no longer attempt to re-plan the
path. The default is 100 attempts.
mission.planning.retry.max: 100
Priority
By default, the system enables the mission re-allocation function based on
the above configurations.
When a robot is idle and selecting the next mission, priority should be giv-
en to missions based on the following criteria in order:
• Assigned mission priority: Missions with higher priority should be con-
sidered first.
• Minimum path cost from robot to mission destination: Missions with
lower path consumption for the robot to reach the target node should
be considered next.
• Mission creation time: Missions created earlier should be considered
last.
The formula for calculating the total score of a mission is as follows:
Mission Total Score = (Mission score for Assigned mission priority  x
Weight of Assigned mission priority ) + (Mission score for Path Cost  x
Weight of Path Cost) + (Mission score for Creation Time  x Weight of
Creation Time)
The score of a single mission for a specific factor depends on its ranking
within the current batch. Assuming the batch size is n, the mission ranked
first will receive a score of n, while the mission ranked last will receive a
score of 1.
If the business requirement is to strictly follow the rule of executing mis-
sions in the order they were created, it is recommended to adjust the con-
figuration as follows:
mission.dispatching.review.weight.createTime: 1
mission.dispatching.review.weight.priority: 0
mission.dispatching.review.weight.pathCost: 0
mission.dispatching.batch.size.max: 1
If a robot experiences an 'Advanced Exception' while executing a mission,
the system will automatically reassign the mission to another robot after
30 seconds.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
346/719 | www.kuka.com KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 347 ===
When the robot assigned to the mission fails to plan a path to the mission
destination, after attempting 100 times, if it still cannot plan a path, the
system will automatically reassign a robot for the mission after 30 sec-
onds.
Restriction
Precondition: The mission is the first mission in the job and is a Move
mission.
Trigger conditions:
• After the robot completes the mission and is released from the com-
pleted mission, other missions that meet the conditions will trigger ro-
bot reassignment.
• During the execution of the mission, if the robot reports an advanced
exception and this condition persists for a certain duration, the system
will automatically trigger robot reassignment.
• When the robot is planning a path and after a certain number of re-
tries it still fails to plan a path, the system will automatically trigger ro-
bot reassignment after a certain duration.
• After the robot enters the map, missions that meet the conditions will
trigger robot reassignment.
The following scenarios do not support re-allocation:
• Missions issued from the monitoring page.
• Non-Move missions.
• Missions in a paused, pausing, or planning state.
• If the robot assigned to the mission is in a loaded state, the mission is
not allowed to be reassigned.
• Missions that have been pre-allocated.
Case
The following examples are based on the condition that the pre-allocation
feature is disabled and the re-allocation feature is enabled.
• Job 1: Schedule a robot to position 39 to lift the container, move to
position 25 to drop the container, and release the robot after comple-
tion. Missions that can be broken down for robot execution:
moveTo39, liftUp, moveTo25, liftDown.
• Job 2: Schedule a robot to position 23 to lift the container, move to
position 37 to place the container down, and release the robot after
completion. Missions that can be broken down for robot execution:
moveTo23, liftUp, moveTo37, liftDown.
First trigger Job 1, the system dispatches AMR#1 to execute it. When
AMR#1 is moving the container from position 39 to position 25, Job 2 is
triggered, and the system dispatches AMR#2 to execute it.
When AMR#1 arrives at position 39 and places the container down, it is
released from Process 1. The system calculates whether there is a closer
robot available to execute the pending mission moveTo23 (this mission
meets the conditions for mission reassignment). Since AMR#1 is closer,
the mission moveTo23 will be reassigned to AMR#1 to execute.
5.6.18 Path cost in pre-allocation and re-allocation
When a robot has a current plan, its cost is weighted by a non-negative
value defined in the docker-compose.yml file under the environment
of krcs-algorithm-service.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 347/719
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 348 ===
as.selector-service.weight-for-current-plan: 1
• PathCost = (CurrentPlanCost × weight-for-current-plan) + CostFrom-
CurrentTargetToNextTarget
5.7 Workflow
Description
A workflow is a set of instructions (program) that one or more AMRs can
execute. KUKA.AMR Fleet provides a graphical interface for creating
workflows. Each workflow consists of a set of steps, which can be:
• A target map node: The AMR travels to the specified map node.
• A target area: The AMR travels to one of the map nodes in the area.
• A workflow: The AMR executes a pre-configured workflow.
• A charging node: The AMR travels to a charging node.
Map nodes and areas can be configured with the following:
• Component commands: Actions executed by the AMR itself, such as
raising or lowering the lift.
• Device commands: Interactions with external devices, such as open-
ing/closing doors, reading inputs, setting outputs, etc.
• Error handling strategy: Actions to be taken for abnormal situations,
such as an occupied target map node or no container at the first node
when a lift should be executed.
A workflow can be assigned to:
• One or more AMRs (by AMR ID).
• One or more AMR types.
5.7.1 Workflow management
Description
Workflow Management contains creating, configuring workflow steps, edit-
ing, copying, deleting, batch operations, inspecting, triggering and cancel-
ing workflows.
5.7.1.1 Adding a workflow
Precondition
• One layout is enabled.
Procedure
1. Navigate to Workflow to enter the workflow list page.

Parameter Description
Strategy ‒ Callback required: This strategy waits for
the previous command to finish before exe-
cuting the current command.
‒ No callback required: This strategy allows

d. Click the OK button to save the configuration; Click the Reset but-
ton to clear the configuration; Click the Cancel button to clear the
configuration and return to the command list page.
Configuring the cascading execution strategy
Description
A cascading execution strategy defines that when a command (main com-
mand) reaches a specified time, another command (sub-command) starts
execution simultaneously.
Cascading commands application scenarios
• Main command: A main command can trigger multiple sub-commands.
‒ Example scenario: When main command A starts execution, it trig-
gers sub command B; when main command A is executing, it trig-
gers sub command C.
• Sub-command: A sub-command can have multiple main commands.
‒ Example scenario: Sub-command C is triggered only when both of
the following conditions are met: Main command A starts execu-
tion, and main command B is in progress.
Cascading commands restrictions
Resctriction Description
Cascading commands can only be
configured for "Map Node" or
"Area" workflow step types.
Other step types do not support
command configuration.
If there are two or more com-
mands, the cascading relationships
must not form a loop.
Example: If there are three com-
mands (A, B, C), where A is the
main command of B, and B is the
main command of C, then C can-
not be set as the main command
of A, because this violates com-
mand execution logic.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 363/719
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 364 ===
Resctriction Description
At least one of the main com-
mands and sub-commands must
be a device command.
Currently, AMRs do not support
executing two component com-
mands simultaneously, so cascad-
ing execution cannot involve two
component commands.
The "Execute Phase" of the main
command must not be later than
that of the sub-command.
Each command has a trigger tim-
ing (Execute Phase) defined at the
step level. Commands execute in
the following order: before arrival,
when arrived, when departed, after
departure. Therefore, a command
with a later trigger timing cannot
be the main command of one with
an earlier timing.
Once a command is added, its
name cannot be modified.
To rename a command, delete the
current one and create a new one.
A command cannot be deleted if it
is bound as a sub-command by
another command.
To delete a bound sub-command,
first unbind it (remove the relation-
ship from the main command) be-
fore deletion. Alternatively, directly
delete the main command first,
then the sub-command.
Main command execution timings
Timing Description
Command Assigned The sub-command executes when
the main command is successfully
dispatched by WCS to RCS/ECS.
Command Started The sub-command executes when
WCS receives the "MIS-
SION_START" callback. (Only for
component commands)
Command in Progress The sub-command executes when
WCS receives the "MISSION_EXE-
CUTING" callback. (Only for com-
ponent commands)
Command Completed The sub-command executes when
WCS receives the "MISSION_FIN-
ISH" callback from RCS/ECS.
Configuration scencario
Roller AMR and Conveyor Interaction - Roller Pick & Conveyor Place:
To pick up goods, the roller AMR must start rotating first (main command:
roller pick), then the conveyor releases the goods (sub command: convey-
or place) to ensure that the AMR receives the goods successfully.
Procedure
1. Configure both commands (main and sub). Set the Execute Phase
appropriately, ensuring the sub-command’s timing is not later than the
main’s. Set the Strategy to "callback required".

Configuring the area step
If an area step is configured in the workflow, the AMR moves to an avail-
able node within that area.
Procedure
1. Select an area step on the canvas to display parameters that can be
configured. There are three tabs:
• BC (Basic Configuration)
• AS (Action Strategy)
• CC (Command Configuration)
Fig. 5-267: Basic configuration of an area step
Parameter Description
Config Name Step name.
The name must be unique (case-sensitive). It can
contain any character, including spaces and spe-
cial characters.
Step Descrip-
tion
Optional node description.
Area Name All added workflow areas are displayed in the
drop-down menu.
Refer to ( >>> 5.1.5 "Area management" Page 210 )
for information related to workflow areas.
Trigger Han-
dling
Defines how the node transitions to the next node:
‒ Manual Trigger: After completing the current
node, execution stops until an external trigger
is received.
‒ Automatic Delay Trigger: After completing the
current node, execution automatically resumes
after the delay time specified in the Delay Time
parameter.
External triggers include external device triggers or
upstream system triggers.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 373/719

=== PDF PAGE 374 ===
Parameter Description
Delay Time The wait time after completing a node task before
moving to the next node.
Associated with the Automatic Delay Trigger option
in Trigger Handling.
The default is 0 seconds, meaning the AMR
moves to the next node immediately after complet-
ing the current task.
2. Configure the action strategy of the area step.
3. Configure the commands of the area step.
4. Click the Ok button to save the configuration.
Click the Reset button to clear the configuration.
Click the Cancel button to clear the configuration and return to the
command list page.

through another workflow.
Procedure

be configured. There are three tabs:
• BC (Basic Configuration)
• AS (Action Strategy)
• CC (Command Configuration)

KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 377/719
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 378 ===
Parameter Description
Force charge ‒ Yes: When the charging conditions are met
(The AMR battery level is lower than the target
battery level), the AMR will wait in place if no
charging stations are available. Once a charg-
ing station becomes available, the AMR will go
to charge.
‒ If the AMR battery level is higher than the
target battery level, the charging task will
be completed in place without going to the
charging station.
‒ If the AMR battery level is lower than the
target battery level, it will go to the charg-
ing station to charge until the target battery
level is reached. If no charging stations are
available, the AMR will queue until a sta-
tion becomes available. 
‒ No: When the charging conditions are met
(The AMR battery level is lower than the mini-
mum battery level), the AMR is allowed to con-
tinue the task without charging if no charging
stations are available.
‒ If the AMR battery level is higher than the
minimum battery level, the charging task
will be completed in place without going to
the charging station.
‒ If the AMR battery level is lower than the
minimum battery level, it will go to the
charging station to charge until the target
battery level is reached. If no charging sta-
tions are available, the charging task will
be completed in place, and the AMR will
proceed to the next target node.
Target battery
level
When force charge is set to Yes, the AMR must
charge if the battery level is below the specified
level.
The AMR will reach the target battery level after
completing the charging task. The target battery
level must be higher than the minimum battery lev-
el.
Minimum bat-
tery level
When force charge is set to No, the AMR will only
charge if the battery level is below the specified
level. The minimum battery level must be lower
than the target battery level.
Robot Await When the AMR executes a charging task, whether
to wait for the AMR to finish charging is deter-
mined by the following settings:
‒ Checked: The entire workflow is completed by
the same AMR. The system waits for the AMR
to complete the charging task before continu-
ing the workflow.
‒ Unchecked: The entire workflow can be com-
pleted by multiple AMRs. When an AMR per-
forms a charging task, other AMRs can take
over the task to continue the workflow.
