=== PDF PAGE 379 ===
5.7.1.3 Creating a multi-branch workflow
Description
A workflow with multiple parallel branches can be set in KUKA.AMR Fleet.
Priorities must be set for parallel steps on the edges. The AMR will go to
the step with the highest priority first.
Procedure
1. Drag multiple steps from the left side of the canvas to the center of
the canvas (excluding workflow steps), and connect these steps to the
same parent step.
2. Configure the priorities for parallel branches:
• Click the edge.
• Enter the priority number. The smaller the value, the higher the pri-
ority of the step. (Value range: 0 ~ 99)
Fig. 5-271: Priority configuration for the multi-branch workflow
5.7.1.4 Editing a workflow
Precondition
• At least one workflow is created.
• The layout where the workflow is located is enabled.
• The workflow is in an editable status. If the edit button is grayed out,
the workflow cannot be edited. Click the Switch Status  button to acti-
vate the edit button and allow workflow modifications.
If the workflow is currently running, the status cannot be switched.
Procedure
1. Click the Edit button in the Action to enter the Workflow Config Editor.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 379/719
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 380 ===
Fig. 5-272: Workflow Config Editor
2. Modify step or workflow parameters.
3. Click the Save button to save the changes, or the Back button to can-
cel the modification.
Fig. 5-273: Saving or canceling workflow configuration
5.7.1.5 Copying a workflow
Precondition
• At least one workflow is created.
• The layout where the workflow is located is enabled.
Procedure
1. Click the Copy button in the Action.
Fig. 5-274: New workflow window
2. Select the layout to which the workflow is assigned.
The layout cannot be changed once the workflow is created.
3. Enter a workflow name. The name must be unique (case-sensitive). It
can contain any character, including spaces and special characters.
4. Select a robot type. It must be the same as the copied workflow and
cannot be changed.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
380/719 | www.kuka.com KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026
Programming and configuration


=== PDF PAGE 381 ===
5. Check the Virtual Button box if the workflow is to be triggered from a
virtual button. A similar checkbox is also available in the editing page.
6. Click OK. The configuration of the copied workflow will be displayed
for editing.
7. Modify step or workflow parameters.
8. Click the Save button to save the changes, or the Back button to can-
cel the modification.
5.7.1.6 Deleting a workflow
Precondition
• At least one workflow is created.
• The layout where the workflow is located is enabled.
• The workflow is in a deletable (enabled) status. If the delete button is
grayed out, the workflow cannot be deleted. Click the Switch Status
button to activate the delete button and allow workflow modifications.
If the workflow is currently running, the status cannot be switched.
Procedure
1. Click the Delete button in the Action.
Fig. 5-275: Deletion confirmation
2. Click OK to confirm the deletion, or click Cancel to cancel the dele-
tion.
3. After deleting the workflow, check for any related events in the work-
flow events or device events. If any are found, delete them as well.
5.7.1.7 Batch operations on workflows
Description
Batch operations on workflows, such as Batch Unrelease, Batch Release,
and Batch Delete, are available.
Precondition
• Multiple workflows are created.
Procedure
1. Click the checkbox in front of the workflow.
2. Click Batch Unrelease, Batch Release, or Batch Delete  buttons.
Clicking the checkbox next to the ID field can select all workflows.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 381/719
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 382 ===
Fig. 5-276: Batch operations of the workflow
Operation Description
Batch Unre-
lease
Click the Batch Unrelease  button, and the status
of all selected workflows will change to the edita-
ble (enabled) status.
If any selected workflow is being executed, the
batch operation will fail.
Batch Release Click the Batch Release  button, and the status of
all selected workflows will change to the
uneditable (disabled) status.
Batch Delete Click the Batch Delete  button, and a confirmation
dialog box will pop up. If confirmed, all selected
workflows will be deleted. If canceled, the delete
operation will be canceled.
If any selected workflow is being executed, the batch operation will
fail.
5.7.1.8 Viewing the details of a workflow
Procedure
1. Click the Detail button to enter the Workflow Config Editor. The config-
uration of the workflow and each node can be viewed, but cannot be
modified or saved.
2. Click the Back button to close the workflow config editor and return to
the workflow list page.
Fig. 5-277: Viewing the details of a workflow

Cancel buttons and strategies
Button Strategy Description
Force Cancel Force cancel Applicable to the cancellation of all workflows created
by WCS.
When the Force Cancel button is clicked, KUKA.AMR
Fleet sends a cancel command to the AMR and the
device, then waits for the cancellation result:
• If KUKA.AMR Fleet receives successful cancellation
feedback, the workflow is marked as Canceled, and
the AMR stops at its current location.
‒ If the AMR carrying a container stops at a map
node, it puts down the container.
‒ If the AMR carries a container and stops on the
edge of the map, it keeps the container raised.
And the AMR and container should be handled
by the user.
• If KUKA.AMR Fleet receives cancellation failure
feedback, the workflow is marked as Warning, and
the Cancel Workflow button becomes Clear Job.
When Clear Job is clicked, the workflow is marked
as Canceled, and the AMR exception is left to be
handled by the user.
Clear Job Clear job If the force cancel fails, this strategy is used to clear
the job and the AMR status must be handled manually.
When KUKA.AMR Fleet receives cancellation failure
feedback from the AMR, the Cancel Workflow button
changes to Clear Job. Select the Clear Job button to
mark the workflow as Canceled, and the AMR excep-
tion should be handled by the user.
Cancel Workflow Stop at next
node
This cancellation strategy is only applicable to work-
flows with the executing status and is only supported
for lift AMRs. It is not supported for workflows in the
warning status or when executing a charging task.
When the AMR is moving to the target workflow node,
select the stop-at-next-node cancellation strategy. The
AMR will complete the movement task and clear all
commands at the target node. If the AMR carries a
container during cancellation, the container will be low-
ered at the target node.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 389/719

Button Strategy Description
Cancel Workflow Go to destination This cancellation strategy is only applicable to work-
flows with the executing status and is only supported
for lift AMRs. It is not supported for workflows in the
warning status or when executing a charging task.
The AMR first completes the current movement task
and then determines the final status of the container at
the target node of the current task.
• If the final status of the container at the target node
is raised:
‒ If the AMR is not carrying a container, it will
move to the target node, raise the container,
transport it to the workflow destination, and
place it there.
‒ If the AMR is carrying a container, it will move
to the target node without interacting with the
container there, transport the carried container
to the workflow destination, and place it there.
• If the final status of the container at the target node
is lowered:
‒ If the AMR is not carrying a container, it will
move to the target node and then proceed to
the workflow destination.
‒ If the AMR is carrying a container, it will place
the container at the target node, then move to
the workflow destination without the container.
• If there are no container-related commands at the
target node:
Regardless of whether the AMR is carrying a con-
tainer, it will move to the target node and then pro-
ceed to the workflow destination. Upon arrival, if
the AMR is carrying a container, it will place it
down; otherwise, no container operation will occur.

5.7.2.2 Condition step
Description
Condition steps are used to execute rules and determine the subsequent
branch based on the results.
To simplify the process, the following workflow diagram may exclude the
start and end nodes.
Matching results
The current condition results support true and false.
• True: The connection logic is set to 1.
• False: The connection logic is set to 0.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 395/719
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 396 ===
Fig. 5-302: Logic setup
Multiple condition steps can be combined to achieve the condition that
needs to match multiple results.
For example, if the Condition1 is met (container code = A), move to the
Drop1 node; if the Condition2 is met (container code = B), move to the
Drop2 node.
Fig. 5-303: Setting multiple conditions
Connecting condition steps
SAFETY INSTRUCTION
The following rules must be followed to connect condition steps.
• A condition step must have exactly two outgoing connections to sub-
sequent nodes.
‒ Connection 1: The logic must be set to 1, indicating that the condi-
tion is met.
‒ Connection 2: The logic must be set to 0, indicating that the condi-
tion is not met.
• If the child node is a condition step, then the child node must be
unique regardless of the type of node.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
396/719 | www.kuka.com KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 397 ===
If a condition step is involved in multiple branches, such as multiple
condition steps or a mix of condition steps and regular nodes, it be-
comes impossible to clearly define the selection logic between the
branches.
The configurations shown in the following figure are not allowed:
Fig. 5-304: Incorrect condition step connection
• When a condition is not met, if the desired effect is to wait in place
until the condition is satisfied, do not connect back to the parent node
unless there is a special requirement. Instead, connect directly to the
condition node.
‒ Waiting in place when the conditions are not met:
‒ Multiple conditions: Condition1 > Condition2 > Condition3
Fig. 5-305: Multiple condition loops
‒ Single condition: Looping the Condition node
Fig. 5-306: Single condition loop
‒ Connecting to the parent node: Connecting to the parent node is a
wrong configuration and brings the following results:
1. When the condition is not met, the AMR will be sent to the
Load node.
2. Then the AMR moves in place and executes the command at
the Load node.
3. If the AMR is already carrying a container, executing the lift
command again reports an error.
Fig. 5-307: Connecting to the parent node
Compromise solution: add a StayAtLoad node after the Load node.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 397/719
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 398 ===
‒ Advantage: The repeated execution of commands can be avoi-
ded.
‒ Disadvantage: KUKA.AMR Fleet keeps executing the loop Con-
dition > StayAtLoad, resulting in generating a large number of
StayAtLoad nodes in Job Board. These nodes affect the dis-
play of business information on the job board and degrades
performance of KUKA.AMR Fleet because they are not from
the real business needs.
Fig. 5-308: Adding a StayAtLoad node before the condition
However, although directly connecting to the parent node is gener-
ally a bad practice. It is reasonable to implement certain special
looping business logic through conditions.
Fig. 5-309: Reasonable looping
‒ Partial loop of multiple conditions: A special connection is shown
as below.
Fig. 5-310: Partial loop of multiple conditions
1. If the Condition1 is not met, move to the Condition2.
2. If the Condition2 is not met, move to the Condition3.
3. If the Condition3 is not met, keep looping Condition2 > Condi-
tion3 untile one of the three conditions is met.

The condition library is located on the left side of the rule configuration in-

Function

determine whether a node is occupied by a container, enter UUID of
the node parameter is used to specify the exact node.
There are two types of function parameters:
‒ Simple type: Simple-type parameters can be directly confirmed
through user input. For example, a node can be specified by di-
rectly inputting the node UUID.
‒ Complex type: Complex-type parameters are generally entities that
cannot be obtained through direct user input, such as a container.
Complex type parameters can only be obtained through functions,
which can be selected from dropdown menus. The return value of
the function selected from the drop-down menu is passed to the
current function.

402/719 | www.kuka.com KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026

For brevity in the following documentation:
‒ The function selected from the dropdown menu will be referred
to as the Secondary Function.
‒ The function selected from the Function column will be referred
to as the Primary Function.
As shown in the following figure, the secondary function is Get Con-
tainer at Node . The node is determined by entering Map-1-123 in
the Enter UUID of the point parameter. The secondary function
passes the container on node Map-1-123 to the Select Container
parameter of the primary function. Therefore, this configuration is
used to obtain the container code of the container on node
Map-1-123.
Fig. 5-318: Obtaining the container code
Complex parameters have a hidden property, which is the type. For
example, the type of the Select Container parameter is a container.
To avoid data errors, the system automatically matches the return
value type of the secondary function based on the parameter type of
the primary function, thereby limiting the range of selectable func-
tions.
When selecting complex type parameters, additional secondary func-
tion information is required. To prevent infinite nesting, secondary
functions currently do not allow further configuration of complex type
parameters.
If the function parameter is a simple type, it can be left blank when
configuring conditions. If left blank, it can be completed in the ex-
pression later. Users can decide whether to fill in the parameters
based on their needs. Generally, fixed parameters can be preset in
the conditions, while parameters that need to change frequently ac-
cording to the workflow can be left blank and decided each time the
expression is configured.
Refer to ( >>> 5.7.2.5 "Built-in function directory" Page 408 ) for all built-

Expression
Description
Expressions are used to calculate business data based on conditions.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15

Programming and configuration

Fig. 5-319: Expression configuration interface
Expression configuration
Conditions, operators, and input box form a logical unit. The output of a
logical unit is either true or false, and multiple logical units can be com-
bined using logical operators (and, or).
Fig. 5-320: Logic unit of expression configuration
1 Condition
2 Operator
3 Input text
4 Removing button
5 Adding button
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
404/719 | www.kuka.com KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 405 ===
Item Description
Condition Select the condition from the drop-down list. All condition in the condition
library can be selected.
After selecting a condition, any simple parameters it includes will be dis-
played below it. If preset values were saved with the condition, they will
appear here. Otherwise, a blank input box will be shown.
The included parameter can be edited. But it should be ensured that all
parameters are filled in before saving the rule.
Updating or adding parameter values for a condition in an expression will
not change the parameter values in the condition library or in other expres-
sions. Conditions in the condition library act as templates, which are con-
verted into instances in the expression. Modifying these instances will not
affect the original templates or other instances.
Operator Operators are used to perform logical judgments on the data output by
conditions. The return values of conditions are determined by their primary
function and can vary by type.
Operator options depend on the return value type.
• If the return value is a string (e.g., Container code), operators like
Equal To, Prefix, and Contains will be available.
• If the return value is a boolean (e.g., Container is full), the operators
will be True and False.
Refer to for all implemented operators and operation rules.
Input box Most operators require user input. For example, with the condition Contain-
er code and the operator Equal To, a specific value must be provided
through a user input box to match the container code. However, some op-
erators, like True or False, do not require user input and will be grayed
out.
Removing/
Adding button
Click the removing/adding button to remove or add a logic unit.
+Add group Click to add a new group.
Logical operators
in logical units
and groups
The results of logical units and relationship groups are either true or false,
and these results are connected using and and or. Therefore, when add-
ing a new logical unit or group, it is necessary to specify the logical oper-
ator between them.
Expression struc-
ture information
There are tips above and below the expression configuration to show the
general structure of the current expression.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 405/719
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 406 ===
Input constraints due to the chosen condition and operator
When configuring the logic unit, avoid entering inappropriate input be-
cause there are two input constraints:
• Type constraint: The input type needs to match the output of the
condition. For example, if the condition outputs a number and the
operator is Greater than, the user can only input numbers. Inputting
letters in this case would be meaningless.
• Business constraint: The input needs to align with actual business
scenarios. For example, if the condition is Container code and the
operator is Equal To, the user must input an actual existing contain-
er code for it to be meaningful.
Inverse operation of a logic unit
There is no expression used for reversing the result of a logical unit. The
inverse operation of a logic unit can only be achieved by modifying the
operator or condition.
For example:
• The inverse operation of number of containers in the area greater
than 3  is number of containers in the area Less than or equal to
3.
• The inverse operation of Container of current workflow is full True
is Container of current workflow is empty True .
There are some situations that cannot achieve inverse operations, such
as Container code Prefix C . Currently, there is no non-prefix operator
for strings. This situation requires re-evaluating and coming up with al-
ternative solutions based on the business needs.
Logic unit combination
Logic units enclosed in parentheses as a group are calculated as a single
unit. The following are two examples:
• Single group ( ① AND ②)
Fig. 5-321: Single group ( ① AND ②)
• Multiple groups ( ① AND ②) OR ( ③ AND ④)
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
406/719 | www.kuka.com KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026
Programming and configuration


=== PDF PAGE 407 ===
Fig. 5-322: Multiple groups ( ① AND ②) OR ( ③ AND ④)
5.7.2.4 Rule execution
Description
During the execution of the workflow, condition steps are not displayed on
the job board.
Since all conditions provided by the current system yield immediate re-
sults, there will be no instances where the process is stuck on a particular
rule.
Additionally, if conditions are not met and the process is queued, continu-
ous polling of logical nodes will occur, resulting in a large number of no-
des appearing on the job board.
Although the workflow won't get stuck at a certain node, there are situa-
tions where conditions are not met, leading to delays:
Fig. 5-323: Delay when conditions are not met
If the conditions are not met, the job board will alert rule verification
failed, rule name: {condition step name} . Meanwhile, KUKA.AMR Fleet
will check the conditions every second until they are satisfied.
Fig. 5-324: Step detail alert on the job board
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 407/719
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 408 ===
5.7.2.5 Built-in function directory
Primary function
Container
Container code
• Label: Container
• Code: ContainerCode
• Description: Get container code from the selected container. Null rep-
resents container does not exist.
• Return Value: [String] Container code.
• Select container (complex param):  [Container Type] Select a con-
tainer.
Container is carrying
• Label: Container
• Code: ContainerCarrying
• Description: Get container carry status from the selected container.5
• Return Value: [Bool] True: container is carrying; False: container is
not carrying or container does not exist.
• Select container (complex param):  [Container Type] Select a con-

Workflow branch loop count
• Label: Common, Workflow
• Code: WorkflowBranchCounter
• Description: Count the number of cycles of the current node. For de-
tail, each time the workflow arrives at the beginning node of the cur-
rent branch, the number will increase by 1. When the workflow arrives
for the first time, the initial count number is 1.
• Return Value: [Long] Loop number.
Branch trigger time
• Label: Common
• Code: TriggerTime
• Description: Record the time of the workflow to trigger the current
condition step, usually used in combination with the "Earlier than cur-
rent time" operator. If there exists a cycle in the workflow, it will re-
fresh the trigger time when triggering the node again. (Since the trig-
ger time is always earlier than the time when the rule is triggered,
please do not use the operator "later than the current time", as it is
meaningless!)
• Return Value: [Time] Rule trigger time.

Equal To String, Integer, Long If the condition output
exactly matches the
user's input, return
true; otherwise, return
false.
Not Equal To String, Integer, Long If the condition output
does not match the
user's input, return
true; otherwise, return
false.

greater than the user's
input number, return
true; otherwise, return
false.

Equal To
Integer, Long If the output number is
greater than or equal
to the user's input
number, return true;
otherwise, return false.
Less Than Integer, Long, Double If the output number is
less than the user's in-
put number, return
true; otherwise, return
false.
Less Than Or Equal
To
Integer, Long If the output number is
less than or equal to
the user's input num-
ber, return true; other-
wise, return false.
Is Null String, Integer, Long,
Double, Time
In the condition config-
uration, Is Null will be
specially noted and is
generally used to indi-
cate non-existence.

Double, Time
In the condition config-
uration, Is Not Null will
be specially noted and
is generally used to in-
dicate existence.

5.8 Event configuration: Device event
Description
The device event configuration page can be found under Workflow >
Event > Device Event.
Device events include receiving events  and triggering events.
• Receiving events indicate actions triggered by ECS, with WCS per-
forming operations based on configurations and signals from ECS.
For example, pressing a button on a button box triggers a workflow.
• Triggering events indicate actions triggered in ECS when certain con-
ditions in the system are met and detected by WCS.
For example, if all nodes in an area are occupied, the light on the but-
ton box is turned off.
Common configurations of a device event
To add a device event, the following three common parameters should be
configured first:
Parameter Description
Config Name Enter the name of the event configuration. The name
must be unique (case-sensitive).
Device Name Select a device from the drop-down menu.
Device Event Select a device event from the drop-down menu to
trigger the operation. It's related to the selected de-
vice.
Fig. 5-325: Common configurations of a device event
5.8.1 Adding a receiving device event
The receiving mode of a device event can be set to general or special.
Precondition
• At least one device is configured in ECS.
Procedure
1. Navigate to Workflow > Event.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 415/719

Fig. 5-326: Device event list
2. Select Device Event.
Fig. 5-327: Device event tab
3. Select the Receiving mode.

4. Enter the configuration name. It must be unique and case-sensitive.
5. Click the Device Name  text box and select a device from the drop-
down menu.
6. Click the Device Event  text box and select a device event from the
drop-down menu. It's related to the selected device above.
7. Select corresponding configuration items based on needs.

The workflow is triggered when the selected device event (action) occurs.
Precondition
• The Config Name is filled in, and the Device Name and Device Event
are selected.
• A workflow is created and enabled.

5.8.1.2 Workflow - release workflow
When a workflow reaches a node configured with Manual Trigger, the
AMR stops until a signal is received by WCS. Once the selected device
event occurs, the workflow is released, and the AMR continues executing
the workflow.

5.8.1.11 Workflow - cancel and start
When the AMR executing the workflow arrives at the node configured with
the Trigger Code, the event will let the workflow waiting for release to be
cancelled. The AMR will be sent to execute another workflow selected in
the Workflow Config Name.
Precondition
• The Config Name is filled in, and the Device Name and Device Event
are selected.
• There is at least one workflow configured on an enabled map.
• At least one AMR is inserted into the enabled map.
Procedure
1. Click the Special button.
2. Select Workflow as Business Type.
3. Select Cancel And Start  as Business Command.
4. Select Node as Trigger Mechanism.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 429/719
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 430 ===
Fig. 5-341: Device event - workflow - cancel and start - basic con-
figuration
5. Click the Next button enter the detailed configuration page.
6. Click the Forbidden Area  text box to select a forbidden area from the
drop-down area.
7. Click Confirm to save.
Use case
• Workflow 1: A (lift up container) -> B (inspection point, waiting for re-
lease) -> C (lower down container)
• Workflow 2: B (lift up container) -> D (lower down container)
When Workflow 1 reaches point B and waits for release, after manual in-
spection, if there is an abnormality, press the button (trigger the event) to
cancel Workflow 1 and start Workflow 2, then carry the container to point
D.

Fig. 5-349: Querying a device event
5.9 Event configuration: Workflow event
Description
The device event configuration page can be found under Workflow >
Event > Workflow Event.
A workflow event in the WCS listening system triggers actions based on
specific conditions. Workflow events can be set to:
• General: General workflow events monitor the status of a node (free
or occupied), the status of a container (inserted or not), the status of a
workflow (completed or not), and the state of a workflow area
(whether all nodes in the area are occupied) to determine whether to
start or release a workflow. For example, when node A is occupied, a
workflow is triggered.
• Special: Special workflow events are related to workflow queuing,
which is enabled by monitoring the occupancy of the associated node
or the workflow association.
Procedure
1. Navigate to Workflow > Event.
Fig. 5-350: Device event list
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 435/719

Fig. 5-351: Workflow event tab

5.9.2 Configuring a special workflow event 
5.9.2.1 Queue up Workflow When Related Node is Occupied
Related Node is Occupied is used to control the execution order of work-
flows based on node occupancy. When triggering a configured workflow,
KUKA.AMR Fleet first checks whether the related nodes in the current
workflow are occupied by other workflows.
• If not, the nodes are occupied by the current workflow and released
once the workflow leaves the nodes.
• If the nodes are occupied, the workflow is queued until the nodes are
free.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 449/719
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 450 ===
Precondition
• Two workflows are created on the enabled layout.
• Both workflows share the same node.
Procedure
1. Enter the Config Name. The name must be unique (case-sensitive).
2. Click the Special button.
3. Select Queue up Workflow  as Business Command.
4. Select Workflow as Trigger Mechanism.
5. Select Node as Monitor Mechanism.
6. Select Related Node is Occupied  as Judegement Mechanism.
Fig. 5-374: Workflow event - queue up workflow - related node is
occupied - basic configuration
7. Click the Next button to enter the detailed configuration page.
8. Select two workflows as Trigger Code.
9. Select the common node of two workflows configured in Trigger Code.
Fig. 5-375: Workflow event - queue up workflow - related node is
occupied - detailed configuration
• For example, there are three workflows:
‒ Workflow 1: A->B->C->E
‒ Workflow 2: B->C->D->F
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
450/719 | www.kuka.com KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 451 ===
‒ Workflow 3: C->D->A->G
• The Trigger Code is configured with these workflows, and the
Code is configured with four nodes: A, B, C, and D. Initially, no
workflow is executed.
• When Workflow 1 is triggered, it will first occupy nodes A, B, and
C. If Workflow 2 is then triggered, it will attempt to occupy nodes
B, C, and D. However, since nodes B and C are already occupied
by Workflow 1, Workflow 2 will be queued. Once Workflow 1
leaves node C, the occupation of that node will be released, allow-
ing Workflow 2 to start executing.
10. Click Confirm to save.
5.9.2.2 Queue up Workflow When Workflows are Correlated
Workflows Are Correlated is used to prevent unexpected problems caused
by concurrent execution between workflows.
Precondition
• At least two workflows are created on the enabled layout.
Procedure
1. Enter the Config Name. The name must be unique (case-sensitive).
2. Click the Special button.
3. Select Queue up Workflow  as Business Command.
4. Select Workflow as Monitor Mechanism.
5. Select Workflows Are Correlated  as Judegement Mechanism.
Fig. 5-376: Workflow event - queue up workflow - workflows are
correlated - basic configuration
6. Click the Next button to enter the detailed configuration page.
7. Select one workflow as Workflow Config Name.
8. Select at least one workflow at Code.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 451/719
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 452 ===
Fig. 5-377: Workflow event - queue up workflow - workflows are
correlated - detailed configuration
• For example, Workflow Config Name is set to workflow A1, and
Code in the Monitor Mechanism is set to workflows {X1,...,Xn}.
When A1 is triggered, it checks if any instances of workflows
{X1,...,Xn} are currently executing.
‒ If none are executing, workflow A1 will execute immediately.
‒ If any are executing, workflow A1 will be queued until all work-
flows {X1,...,Xn} have completed execution.
9. Click Confirm to save.

5.10 Equipment configurations
Description
Equipment refers to real devices associated with edges on the map. It can
be configured on map edges to facilitate information transfer between de-
vices and KUKA.AMR Fleet. Devices such as automatic doors, elevators,
and PLC grids are typically configured on map edges.
In the current version, once a device is created in ECS, the correspond-
ing device interactions and commands will be synchronized to WCS.
Device interactions can be configured on the map as needed, without
managing their creation, modification, or deletion. However, the process-
es for adding, deleting, modifying, and querying device interactions are
introduced below.
5.10.1 Adding an equipment configuration
Precondition
• At least one device is created in ECS.
Procedure
1. Navigate to Workflow > Device to enter the equipment configuration
list.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
454/719 | www.kuka.com KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026
Programming and configuration


=== PDF PAGE 455 ===
Fig. 5-381: Equipment configuration page
Fig. 5-382: Adding an equipment configuration
2. Select the Name from the equipment list in ECS. The Equipment ID
will be automatically populated based on the selected equipment.
3. Select the Device Event corresponding to the equipment, sourced from
the event in the rule chain in ECS (e.g., open and close the door).
4. Select the Command List, which is populated based on the selected
device event.
5. Click OK to create an equipment configuration.
5.10.2 Editing an equipment configuration
Precondition
• At least one equipment configuration is created.
Procedure
1. Click Edit.
2. Modify the equipment configurations.
3. Click OK to save the changes.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 455/719
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 456 ===
Fig. 5-383: Editing an equipment configuration
5.10.3 Deleting an equipment configuration
If the equipment configuration is deleted, the edge configuration on the
map will also be deleted. A prompt will appear when editing the map after
the equipment configuration is deleted.
Fig. 5-384: Removed equipment configuration prompt
Precondition

configuration, it will request the corresponding equipment.

5.10.5 Disabling an equipment configuration
Precondition
• At least one equipment configuration is in the enabled status.
Procedure
1. Click Disabled to disable the equipment configuration.
Fig. 5-387: Disabling an equipment configuration
Once an equipment configuration is disabled, it cannot be configured on
the map. If a configured equipment configuration is disabled, an error
will occur when the AMR attempts to pass through the edge where the
equipment configuration is set. The AMR will display in red, and the er-
ror message device disabled will indicate that the equipment is not
available.

5.10.6 Configuring equipment configurations on the map
Description
The equipment configuration on the map can be set as Device Before
and Device After  actions.
• Device Before refers to the equipment configuration operated when
the AMR is at the starting node of an edge before traveling over it.
• Device After refers to the equipment configuration operated after the
AMR has traveled over the edge and reached the other node.
For example, in the interaction between an AMR and a light curtain, the
AMR requests entry at the starting node, travels over the edge upon re-
ceiving the signal, and then informs the light curtain PLC upon reaching
the end node. The following describes how to configure the equipment
configuration on the map for this scenario.
Precondition
• A map is created.
• At least two equipment configurations are configured.
Procedure
1. Navigate to Scheduling > Layout > District to enter the interface of
area management.

=== PDF PAGE 459 ===

3. Right-click the edge in the desired direction for equipment configura-
tion, then select the Edit button to enter the edge configuration win-
dow.
Fig. 5-390: Editing the edge

10. Click Save [Device Before]  to save the Device Before configuration

5.11 Workflow step command
Component command
Refer to ( >>>  "Component command" Page 368 ).
Device command
Device commands are used to trigger device-related operations within a
workflow or when an AMR is on an edge. Generally, after completing the
device-related configuration in the device module, device commands are
automatically synchronized, requiring no manual configuration. However, if
custom parameters need to be included when sending device commands,
the automatically synchronized commands must be adjusted.
Fig. 5-396: Device command list
Precondition
• The device module has completed the device and rule chain configu-
ration.
Adding a device command
1. Click New Node Command  to enter the configuration page. Due to
the synchronization mechanism of device commands, the
synchronized commands can generally be directly queried and modi-
fied. For special requirements, new configurations can be added.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 463/719
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 464 ===
Fig. 5-397: Device command configuration page
2. Fill in the following parameters.
• General configuration:
‒ Command Name: The name of the device command. The
name must be unique among all commands.
‒ Command Code: Used to identify the command. It must be
unique among all commands.
‒ Device Selection: Select the device to be triggered.
‒ Device Type: The type of device, automatically populated after
selecting the device.
‒ Select the event that the device needs to receive.
• Event parameters:
‒ Id: Automatically populated event ID after configuring the selec-
ted event.
‒ Index: Automatically populated event index after configuring the
selected event.
‒ Code: Automatically populated event code after configuring the
selected event.
‒ Name: Automatically populated event name after configuring
the selected event.
• Parameter configuration:
‒ Type:
‒ Constant: Carrying a fixed parameter each time the device
command is triggered.
‒ Enumeration: Generally used for on-site business customi-
zation, not typically used.
‒ Business Data: Carrying internal workflow parameters when
the device command is triggered, such as the current con-
tainer number in the workflow.
‒ Value:
‒ Type - Constant: Parameter value.
‒ Type - Enumeration: Select a specific parameter bit, and
the parameter value of the bit will be passed to the device
when the device command is triggered.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
464/719 | www.kuka.com KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 465 ===
‒ Type - Business Data: Select the corresponding business
data for the value.
‒ Robot Category: The robot category of the robot cur-
rently executing the task.
‒ Workflow Instance ID: The ID of the workflow instance
currently being executed.
‒ Container Number: The container number currently be-
ing transported by the robot.
‒ Container Stop Angle: The container stop angle at the
target node.
‒ Current Task Start Node: The start node of the current
robot movement task.
‒ Current Task Target Node: The target node of the cur-
rent robot movement task.
‒ Robot Task Number: The task number of the current ro-
bot.
‒ Workflow Start Step: The start step of the current work-
flow.
‒ Workflow End Step: The end step of the current work-
flow.
‒ Parameter name: The parameter of the device receiving event
configured in the rule chain.
‒ Code: The code of the event parameter, automatically popula-
ted after selecting the parameter name.
3. Click OK to complete the current parameter configuration.
Editing a device command
Click the Edit button on the list page to modify the device command con-
figuration.
Deleting a device command
Click the Delete button on the list page to delete the device command
configuration.
Querying a device command
Click the Search button on the list page to query the device command
configuration. Supports fuzzy search by command name, or command
code, or selected event.
5.12 Switching floor
Description
Switching floor include a series of AMR actions:
• Entering the elevator
• Switching the floor at the elevator node
• Completing localization and exiting the elevator
The floor switching process requires coordination with elevator control, in-
cluding external and internal calls, and elevator release.
There are two methods for floor switching:
• Elevator control by AMR ( >>> 5.12.1 "Elevator control by robot"
Page 466)
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 465/719
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 466 ===
• Elevator control by ECS ( >>> 5.12.2 "Elevator control by ECS"
Page 469)
5.12.1 Elevator control by robot
Description
The elevator control by robot process is as follows:
• The robot moves to a normal node outside the elevator and calls the
elevator externally.
• Once the door opens, the robot enters, moves to the elevator node,
and makes an internal call in the elevator.
• The robot then switches floors and updates its location information of
the target floor.
• Upon reaching the target floor, the robot exits the elevator, releases it,
and moves to a normal node.
In actual scenarios, after exiting, the robot needs to move to a wall code
for positioning on the target floor.
Precondition
• The applicable robot should be equipped with elevator control module
for floor switching, such as like KMP 600i 1-5G-H1 (robot type code is
"HCS-600I"). Currently it is only for the Chinese market.
Graph editing
1. Navigate to Scheduling > Layout > Building Layout  > New
Building to add a new building.
2. Navigate to Scheduling > Layout > Map to upload a basemap.
The AMR elevator control requires the map orientations between
two floors to be consistent. This means that the elevator orientation
needs to be the same on both maps.
Fig. 5-398: Uploading a basemap
3. Navigate to Scheduling > Layout > District > New District  to add
two districts corresponding to two floors. Associate the basemap with
these two floors.
The district number needs to be consistent with the floor number.
Fig. 5-399: Adding two districts
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
466/719 | www.kuka.com KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026
Programming and configuration


=== PDF PAGE 467 ===
4. Click Edit Graph  to edit the graph for two floors. Set the correspond-
ing elevator node and keep the labels of the elevator node consistent
on both floors.
The graph editing for the floor 1 is shown in the following figure:
Fig. 5-400: Graph editing of the floor 1
5. Set the number 7 node as the elevator node.
Fig. 5-401: Setting the elevator node
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 467/719
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 468 ===
Fig. 5-402: The node label of the elevator node
In practical scenarios, wall codes should be configured near the elevator
exit. At a node close to the elevator, set a node label consistent with
the wall code ID for robot positioning. Additionally, the elevator control
node for the robot should be selected outside the actual elevator, as
shown in the figure below:
Fig. 5-403: Setting the elevator node
1 Node
2 Elevator node
3 Elevator

1. Navigate to Workflow > New Workflow Config  to add a new work-

Fig. 5-404: Adding two nodes
3. Click Save and return.

to the elevator control by robot.
action.elevator.robotControl: "true"
5. Enter the robot to the floor 1. Click to trigger the workflow.
5.12.2 Elevator control by ECS
Description
The elevator control by ECS process is as follows:
• The robot moves to a normal node outside the elevator and the CEA
(Custom Edge Action) calls the elevator externally.
• Once the door opens, the robot enters, moves to the elevator node,
and the CEA makes an internal call in the elevator.
• The robot then switches floors and updates its location information of
the target floor.
• Upon reaching the target floor, the robot exits the elevator and moves
to a normal node. The CEA releases the elevator.
During this process, the robot inside the elevator will receive a map
switching command containing the pose of the target node from
KUKA.AMR Fleet.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15

5.13.1 Rule chain library
Description
The rule chain is the logical combination of rule node.
The rule chain library is used to store rule chains of various device types,
and supports operations such as adding, querying, editing, deleting, im-
porting, and exporting rule chains.
Adding a rule chain
• Navigate to Equipment > Rule Chain Library .
• Click the New button on the rule chain library page.
• Enter the rule chain name. It only supports numbers, letters, and Chi-
nese characters.
• Select the device type from the drop-down list.
• Enter the description of the rule chain.
• Click OK to enter the rule chain detail page.
Fig. 5-421: Adding a rule chain
• Click Template to select a template from the drop-down list.
If the Custom Device is selected as the device type when creating a
rule chain, the device template drop-down list will display rule chain
templates for all device types.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 477/719
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 478 ===

• Adjust the rule chain according to actual needs, such as adding, mod-
ifying or deleting nodes.
• Click the Save button to save the rule chain.
Querying a rule chain
• Search: Manually enter the rule chain name and click on the Search
button.
• Reset: Clear the input query conditions after clicking the Reset button.
Fig. 5-423: Searching a rule chain
Editing a rule chain
1. Select a rule chain in the rule chain library, and click Detail to enter
the rule chain editing panel.
Ensure all associated devices are disabled before clicking the Edit
button.

Page 491)

Page 493)

Page 494)

Page 496)

variable node" Page 498 )

5.13.2.1 Start node
Description
All messages entering the rule chain must first enter the starting node.
They are then dispatched to different branches of the rule chain for pro-
cessing based on the message type.
The two common message types are uploading device properties and re-
questing devices.
Procedure
1. Click the default start node on the canvas.
2. Modify the node name as needed. The default node name is Start
Node.
Fig. 5-432: Filling in the node name
3. Drag a node from the left panel to the canvas.
4. Connect the start node with the next node and set the link label.
There are two common link labels:
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
482/719 | www.kuka.com KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 483 ===
• Post attributes (Read device attributes and post them to rule
chain): Used to connect the start node with the Save Attribute
node.
• Request to Device (WCS requests interaction with the device):
Used to connect the start node and the Receive Event node.
Fig. 5-433: Connecting with the next node
5.13.2.2 Save attribute node
Description
The save attribute node is used to save the latest read data to the data-
base.
The save attribute node is only applicable to Modbus, S7 and Ethernet
IP docking protocols.
Procedure
1. Drag the save attribute node from the left panel to the canvas.
2. Modify the default node name as needed.
Fig. 5-434: Filling in the node name
3. If there is no next node, skip this and the following step. If there is a
next node, drag the next node from the left panel to the canvas.
4. Connect the save attribute node with the next node and set the link la-
bel.
• Success: The device attribute data has been saved successfully.
• Failure: The device attribute data failed to be saved.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 483/719
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 484 ===
Fig. 5-435: Connecting with the next node
5.13.2.3 Receive event node
Description
The receive event node is used to receive the task events issued by
WCS.
Only one receive event node is allowed on the same branch.
Events initiated by AMR include:
• requesting entry into a workstation
• entering a workstation
• requesting pick-up
• requesting drop-off
• pick-up completed
• drop-off completed
• requesting door open
• requesting door close
Procedure
1. Drag the receive event node from the left panel to the canvas.
2. Fill in the node name.

5.13.2.4 Trigger event node
Description
After reading the trigger signal from the device/PLC, ECS generates a trig-
ger event and sends it to the upstream system (e.g. WCS), which then in-
itiates the process.
The trigger event node is used to trigger the WCS process, including:
• pressing a button on the button box to initiate shelf entry or exit
• PLC notifying the AMR to pick up or deposit a pallet
• PLC triggering the emergency stop of the AMR in a restricted area.
Only one trigger event node is allowed on the same branch.
Procedure
1. Drag the trigger event node from the left panel to the canvas.
2. Fill in the node name.

• Parameter name
• Parameter description
• Parameter type

fixed value.

5.13.2.5 Read data node
Description
The read data node is used to determine whether the attribute data
matches the expected value and read the following signals:
• the allowed pick-up/deposit signals from the device/PLC
• the allowed entry/exit signals
• the door open/close position signals
• the container number
The read data node is only applicable to Modbus, S7 and Ethernet IP
protocols.
Procedure
1. Drag the read data node from the left panel to the canvas.
2. Fill in the Node Name.
3. Fill in the Polling Cycle Time , and it is recommended to be no less
than 200 milliseconds.
4. Fill in the Timeout (optional). If the execution time of the read node
exceeds the set value, the node will time out.
The timeout value should be set to >=1. If it is set to 0, then the
system will automatically change it to 1.
Fig. 5-441: Filling in the node information
5. Set the following parameters (optional):
• Name
• Type
‒ Static: If the parameter type is static, the value should be a
fixed value.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 487/719

ue needs to be filled with the dynamic value of ${} . For exam-
ple, parameter name "doorId", if the parameter type is dynam-
ic, then the parameter value needs to be filled with ${doorId}.
Fig. 5-443: Filling a dynamic parameter
• Value
6. Drag the next node from the left panel to the canvas and connect it
with the read data node. Set the link label:
• True (default): The current read data node matches the expected
value.
• False: The current read data node doesn't match the expected val-
ue.
• Cancel: The current read data node is canceled.
• Failure: The current read data node fails.

• drop-off completed

• requesting door close

5.13.2.7 Delay node
Description
The delay node is used to delay the execution of the next node, such as
the reset signal with a delay of two seconds.
The delay duration can be customized.
Procedure
1. Drag the delay data node from the left panel to the canvas.
2. Fill in the node name.
3. Enter the delay time. It is recommended to be no less than one sec-
ond.
• Method 1: Enter the number using the keyboard.
• Method 2: Set the number by clicking the up and down arrows
with the mouse.
Fig. 5-447: Filling in the node information
4. (Optional) Drag the next node from the left panel to the canvas and
connect it with the delay node. Set the link label:
• Success (default): The set delay succeeds.
• Failure: The set delay fails.

Description
The API call node is used to call or callback API of other systems, includ-
ing:
• Callback the API of WCS (Workflow Control System)
• Call the API of Interface (Interface System)
• Call the API of an external system

5.13.2.9 Expression calculate node
Description
The expression calculate node is used to convert or calculate the expres-
sion, and then pass the converted or calculated value to the next node.
Basic calculations include:
• conversion
• arithmetic operations (addition, subtraction, multiplication, division)
• logical operations (AND, OR, NOT)
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 491/719
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 492 ===
Procedure
1. Drag the expression calculate node from the left panel to the canvas.
2. Fill in the node name.
Fig. 5-449: Filling the node information
3. Set parameters, including name, type, expression, and description.
Fig. 5-450: Setting parameters
The format for dynamic parameter in expressions should be ${prop-
erty}. For example, if the property is command1, the dynamic pa-
rameter would be ${command1}. An expression can only contain
one type of dynamic parameter.
The currently supported functions in expressions are:
‒ fromHex(a): which converts hexadecimal to decimal
‒ toHex(a): which converts decimal to hexadecimal
‒ pow(a, b): which performs exponentiation, where a is the base
and b is the exponent.
Functions can be nested. For example: fromHex(pow(2, 3) - 1).
‒ logical operation, such as +, -, *, %, /, >, =, < : for example:
3*{command1} - 1
4. Click the + on the left side of the attribute to expand it. After entering
the data, click the Test button to see the test result.

5.13.2.11 Outbound byte data node
Description
The outbound byte data node is used to send data to external TCP/UDP
services.
The outbound byte data node is only applicable to TCP and UDP proto-
cols.
Procedure
1. Drag the outbound byte data node from the left panel to the canvas.
2. Fill in the node name.
3. Enter the timeouts. The unit is seconds, with a default of 600.
If polling request for external data of the node exceeds the timeout
without obtaining the expected value, a timeout handling will be execu-
ted and the polling will end.
4. Enter the max retry times and the retry time intervals.
5. Configure the request body and the response body.
Both the request body and response body should be configured with
parameters according to the data structure of byte arrays. The config-
ured data is a string of hexadecimal arrays separated by commas.
• Request body: The request data sent to the TCP server. The re-
quest data must be configured.
‒ ETX Separator Mode: The request body must end with the
ETX parameter as the data terminator.
‒ Header Body Mode: The request body consists of header and
body parameters.
‒ Header: 4 bytes, defining the length of the body in bytes.
For example: 0, 0, 0, 8 represents 8 in decimal.
‒ Body: The message data of the request. For example: 75,
6e, 6c, 6f, 61, 64, 65, 64.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
494/719 | www.kuka.com KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026
Programming and configuration


=== PDF PAGE 495 ===
• Response body: The response data received from the TCP server.
If response data validation is required, the expected response val-
ues to be matched should be configured.
‒ Each address can have multiple response values selected.
‒ If the response is not of concern, it can be empty.
The value supports multiple selections.
Fig. 5-454: Filling in the node information
Fig. 5-455: Request body configuration
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 495/719
Programming and configuration

Fig. 5-456: Response body configuration

without obtaining the expected value, a timeout handling will be execu-
ted and the polling will end.
4. Enter the max retry times and the retry time intervals.
5. Configure the request body and the response body.
Both the request body and response body should be configured with
parameters according to the data structure of byte arrays. The config-
ured data is a string of hexadecimal arrays separated by commas.
• Request body: The data sent to the HTTP API server. The request
data must be configured.
• Response body: The response data received from the HTTP API
server.
The value supports multiple selections.

5.13.3 Device management
Description

play key device information, including name, type, rule chain name, proto-
col type, status, and modification time.
Basic operations include adding, editing, enabling, debugging, copying,
querying, deleting devices.

from the drop-down list.

the drop-down list.

Rule Name Select the corresponding rule chain from the drop-
down list.

5.13.3.5 Enabling/Disabling a device
Enabling a device
• Click Enable on the right of the device. The device will be in an ena-
bled status.
If there is an error with the rule chain, enabling the device will fail. In
this case, check the cause of the rule chain error, correct it, and then
attempt to enable the device again.
Fig. 5-478: Enabling/Disabling a device
Batch enabling devices
• Check the checkboxes of devices to be enabled on the Device Man-
agement main interface, then click the Batch Enable  button to enable
the selected devices.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
506/719 | www.kuka.com KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026
Programming and configuration


=== PDF PAGE 507 ===
Fig. 5-479: Batch enabling devices
Disabling a device
• Click Disable on the right of the device. The device will be in a disa-
bled status.
‒ If the device is running, clicking Disable will first attempt to disable
it normally.
‒ If the device is executing a rule chain task, it cannot be disabled
immediately.
‒ If the disable process does not complete within 3 seconds, the de-
vice status will change to Stopping and the Disable button will
change to Force Disable. Clicking Force Disable  again will forcibly
interrupt the rule chain task and disable the device, changing its
status to Disabled.
Fig. 5-480: Disabling a device
Batch disabling devices
• Check the checkboxes of devices to be disabled on the Device Man-
agement main interface, then click the Batch Disable  button to disa-
ble the selected devices.
Fig. 5-481: Batch disabling devices

rule chain.

5.13.3.9 Testing a device event
Description
A configured device event can be tested to check if the configuration is
correct.
Tested device events include:
• Trigger event: Used to manually simulate WCS sending "receive
event" to ECS.
• Receive event: Used to manually simulate device/PLC signals in ECS,
generate a "trigger event," and send it to WCS, which then initiates
the specified workflow.
Precondition
• The device/PLC is turned on.
• The device is enabled in the device management and the device mon-
itoring shows that it is connected.
In the device monitor panel, the connection status of Http protocol devi-
ces is not displayed.
Testing a receive event
1. Enable the device on the device management page.
(>>> 5.13.3.5 "Enabling/Disabling a device" Page 506 )
2. Click Edit on the right of the device and switch to the Event Test tab.
3. Select an event from the drop-down list in Event Select or perform a
fuzzy search for the event name.
4. (Optional) Enter the parameter value.
• For the event with parameters:
‒ Static parameters: Skip this procedure.
‒ Dynamic parameters: Enter the parameter value.
• For the event without parameters: Skip this procedure.
Fig. 5-488: Receive event with parameters

5. Click the Test button.
6. Check the result.
• Success: If successfully issued, check the device actions and mon-

• Failure: Manually handle any error based on the error message
prompt.
Fig. 5-489: Checking execution progress

configuration.
When the rule chain and protocol type are the same, it is recommended
to create the device by copying.
Precondition
• There is a device template.
Coping a single device
1. Click Copy on the right of the device template.
2. Select Single and click OK.

5.13.3.14 Manual synchronization
Description
Events can be synchronized manually to the Workflow/Device and Work-
flow/Command/Device Command page of WCS.
If the automatic synchronization event for the added device fails, or if the
event in the Workflow/Device and the command in the Workflow/
Command/Device Command page are manually deleted, the data can be
synchronized manually using this button.
Procedure
To manually synchronize a device:
1. Navigate to Equipment > Device.
2. Click the Synchronize action on the device list page.
Fig. 5-502: Manual synchronization action
To manually batch synchronize multiple devices:
1. Check the checkboxes of devices to be synchronized on the Device
Management main interface, then click the Batch Synchronization
button to synchronize the selected devices.

5.13.3.15 Connection test
Description
The connection test is used to check if the IP and port of the device or
PLC are available.
Only Modbus, S7, Http, OPC-UA, TCP, and UDP protocols support the
connection test.
Procedure
1. Navigate to Equipment > Device.
2. Click the Detail action on the device list page.
3. Switch to the Connection Test tab.
4. Click the Connection Test button.
5. Check the test status. If it shows Test has been sent , check the test
result. There are three types of test results:
• IP is not available.
• IP is available, but Port is not available.
• IP and Port are available.
Fig. 5-504: Connection test page

Description
Device monitor is used to monitor external devices, including the connec-
tion status and the execution progress of the devices.

=== PDF PAGE 519 ===
5.13.4.2 Exception task handling
Description
When a task on the monitor page displays an exception, the specific rule
node where the exception occurred can be viewed.
Retry or skip operations can be performed on the exception node.
Precondition
• The device task status is abnormal.
Procedure
1. Click the execution progress to open its details and check the rule
node position and the error.
2. Click Cancel Task to cancel all tasks on the device.
If the device with multiple groups is executing multiple tasks, all the
tasks will be canceled.
Fig. 5-507: Execution progress details
3. Click the exception rule node to select the handling method:
• Retry: Re-execute the failed task at the current node.
‒ If successful, it moves to the next node.
‒ If it fails, it remains at the current node.
• Skip: Skip the logic of the current node and directly proceed to the
execution of the next node.
Fig. 5-508: Manual handling the exception node
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 519/719
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 520 ===

Description
If an abnormality occurs during the operation of the device, the number of
alarms will be displayed. Clicking the alarms will show detailed alarm in-
formation.
Procedure
1. Click the small red dot to display the warning information.
2. Click the close button on the warning information popup to clear the
warning status.
Fig. 5-509: Warning information
5.13.4.4 Viewing the charger status
Description
The real-time status of a charger can be viewed, including connection sta-
tus, fault, output voltage, output current and charging duration.
Connection status includes: Idle, Charging, Charging Complete, Emergen-
cy Stop, and Disconnected.

5.13.5 Log management
Description
Logs of interactions between ECS and WCS, as well as between ECS
and devices, are recorded with timestamps accurate to the millisecond.
Logs can be viewed online in detail and exported as log files.
5.13.5.1 Searching logs
Search by log type
1. Select the log type and click Search.
Fig. 5-511: Searching logs by the log type
Search by multiple conditions
1. Click the Advanced Search  icon and enter filter conditions, including
device name, device type, ECS mission ID, WCS mission ID, connec-
tion status, execution status, start time, and end time, as well as rule
node name.

Event group configuration Description
Address Enter the decimal register ad-
dress according to the signal
agreements specified in the
docking protocol.
For different control devices, the
register address offset may vary,
affecting the register address in
the connection settings.
‒ If the device register address
offset is 0, then ECS register
address = device register ad-
dress.
‒ If the device register address
offset is 1, then ECS register
address = device register ad-
dress - 1.
Bit Address ‒ Default: -1 (not used).
‒ A bit address of -1 means
extracting the entire data
without specifying a bit.
‒ If the bit address is greater
than -1, it specifies which bit
to extract.
Example:
‒ The decimal value of the at-
tribute allow_enter is 65 (bi-
nary value 01000001).
‒ If the bit address is -1, the
extracted data is 65.
‒ If the bit address is 6, the
extracted data is 1.
Byte Order Select the mode from the drop-
down list.
Default: big-endian mode
Event Name It will be auto-filled, but can also
be manually updated.
Copy the event node name and
add a prefix or suffix, such as
"#1 Button - xx Process".
Disable Disable the entire group configu-
ration.
Corresponding register address-
es and events will also be disa-
bled.

5.13.6.4 TCP protocol
Description
The TCP protocol requires configuring the device communication IP and
port. Device events are generated based on the rule chain and do not
need to be added to the device configuration. Data interaction relies on
the Outbound byte data node and Inbound byte data node in the rule
chain.
Precondition
• The TCP signal document is agreed upon.
• A rule chain is created.
Procedure
1. Configure the device connection.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 531/719
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 532 ===
Fig. 5-522: Connection configuration
Connection configuration Description
Timeout(s) Enter the timeout
Unit: seconds
IP Enter the actual IP of the device/
PLC, and this IP must be a
static IP.
Port The port of the device/PLC.
Frame Decoder ‒ ETX delimiter mode (short
connection):
Data stream unpacking is
based on the ETX delimiter,
so the response body must
end with the ETX parameter
in the Outbound byte data
node.
‒ Header Body mode (long
connection):
Data stream unpacking is
based on the data byte
length defined in the Header
(4 bytes) and the Body data
composition.

side
Property read and write types -
Attribute tag name Defined by ADS protocol side
Property type In bytes
‒ BOOL: 1 byte
‒ SINT / USINT: 1 byte
‒ INT / UINT: 2 bytes
‒ DINT / UDINT: 4 bytes
‒ LINT: 8 bytes
‒ REAL: 4 bytes
‒ LREAL: 8 bytes
5.13.6.8 MQTT Protocol
Description
KUKA.AMR Fleet serves as the MQTT broker, and the PLC, as the MQTT
client, connects to KUKA.AMR Fleet to exchange data and communicate
via the MQTT protocol.
The IP of the KUKA.AMR Fleet MQTT broker is the server IP, and the de-
fault port is 2883. This can be modified using the environment variable
MQTT_BIND_PORT.
The device configuration supports multiple groups, and the topics for all
device configurations are globally unique.
The MQTT protocol supports the following rule chain nodes:
• Subscribe data
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 537/719
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 538 ===
• Read data
• Write data
• Trigger event
• Receive event
• Grpc api call
Precondition
• The MQTT signal document is agreed upon.
• A rule chain is created.
Procedure
1. Configure the device connection.
Fig. 5-527: MQTT rule chain
Fig. 5-528: Connection configuration
The parameters defined in the read and write nodes of the rule chain
will be received and sent in JSON format via MQTT messages. The
MQTT message supports QoS 0/1/2.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
538/719 | www.kuka.com KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 539 ===
Topic type Description
PUB_TO_PLC The parameters in the write node will be sent to
the PLC using this topic.
SUB_FROM_P
LC
The parameters in the read node will subscribe to
data from the PLC using this topic.
The subscription node subscribes to the
SUB_FROM_PLC type of device configuration top-
ic.
It must be used as the first node after the start
node and is later used in conjunction with the trig-
ger event.
5.13.7 Application cases
Description
The following device application cases are supported, including:
• Charging station (Modbus) ( >>> 5.13.7.1 "Charging station (Modbus)"
Page 539)
• Fire alarm device (S7) ( >>> 5.13.7.2 "Fire alarm device (S7)"
Page 542)

• Door (Modbus) ( >>> 5.13.7.5 "Door (Modbus)" Page 564 )
• Button box (Modbus) ( >>> 5.13.7.6 "Button box (Modbus)" Page 570 )
• Robotic arm (Modbus) ( >>> 5.13.7.7 "Robotic arm (Modbus)"
Page 584)
5.13.7.1 Charging station (Modbus)
Description
There are two kinds of charging stations:
• Conductive charging station
• Inductive charging station
The following application case uses an inductive charging station as an
example.
Precondition
• The device/PLC/simulator is turned on.
• The device is enabled in device management, and the device monitor-
ing shows connected.
Viewing rule chains of charging stations
The following two charging stations are built in and cannot be deleted.
When creating a charging station device, one of these two rule chains
must be associated.

5.13.7.3 Sending the AMR status to a device/PLC
Description
When any of the following AMR status data changes, the AMR status can
be sent to a device/PLC at regular intervals.
• robId: AMR ID
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 545/719
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 546 ===
• robStatus: AMR Status
• robTaskCode: AMR Task Code
• isLoaded: 1 means the AMR is loaded, 0 means the AMR is not loa-
ded
• passedNode: AMR stop position node code or pass-through position
node code
• robWarningCode: AMR Warning Code
• batteryLevel: Battery Level
Currently, only S7 and Modbus can be used to send AMR status data.
Precondition
• The device/PLC/simulator is turned on.
• The device is enabled in device management, and the device monitor-
ing shows it is connected.
Adding a rule chain
1. Navigate to Equipment > Rule Chain Library .
2. Click New to enter the Add page.
3. Enter the rule chain name. Only numbers, letters, and Chinese are
supported.
4. Select R-AMR from the device type drop-down menu.
5. Enter the description of the rule chain.
6. Click OK to enter the rule chain editing page.
7. Click Template and select Send AMR Status Data(S7_Modbus)  from
the drop-down list.
8. Click OK to save.
Adding a device
1. Navigate to Equipment > Device.
2. Click the New button to enter the Add/Edit Device panel.
3. Fill in the main device information.
4. Click the Setup button to configure the device connection.
5. Fill in the AMR ID for the event group name.
6. Fill in the data address and other information.
Fig. 5-539: Taking the S7 protocol as an example
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
546/719 | www.kuka.com KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 547 ===
Fig. 5-540: Taking the Modbus protocol as an example
7. Click Confirm to add a device. The device will be enabled by default.
Enabling subscribing/sending AMR status data on swagger
1. Log in ECS swagger.
2. Find the app/device/switchSubscribeAndPublishRobotInfo API from
the Test Controller.
3. Fill in the device name.
4. Select true for enableSubscribeRobotInfo.
5. Select true for enablePublicRobotInfo.
6. Select SCHEDULE for publishRobotInfoMode.
7. Click the Execute button.
Fig. 5-541: Enabling subscribing/sending AMR status data
Debugging
1. Ensure that the device is enabled and connected.
2. Assign a task to the AMR.
3. Check whether the Device/PLC receives the AMR status data.

6
Goods-receiving process
Fig. 5-548: Goods-receiving process
1. Before the AMR enters the receiving node. WCS sends the receive
event to start the goods-receiving process and waits for the callback.
2. ECS writes notify receive  signal to the PLC.
3. ECS reads the Is receive allowed  signal from the PLC.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 551/719
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 552 ===
4. If the allow receive  signal is read as true, the WCS is called back to
notify that receiving goods is allowed.
5. WCS receives the callback, enters into the receiving area to execute
the receive action, and sends the receiving event receive finished
event when the receiving is completed.
6. Upon receiving the receive finished event , ECS writes the receive
finished signal to the PLC.
7. When ECS reads the allow receive has been reset signal from PLC,
it writes notify receive  and receive finished signals to false.
8. ECS calls back WCS, and the receiving goods process is over.
Goods-sending process
Fig. 5-549: Goods-sending process
1. Before the robot entering into the sending area, WCS sends the send
event to start the sending goods process and to wait for callback.
2. ECS writes notify send  signal to the PLC.
3. ECS reads PLC Is send allowed  signal from the PLC.
4. If the allow send  signal is read as true, the WCS is called back to no-
tify that sending is allowed.
5. WCS receives the callback, enters into the sending area to execute
the sending action, and sends the receive event send finished event
after finishing sending.
6. Upon receiving the send finished event , ECS writes the send finish-
ed signal to the PLC.
7. When ECS reads the allow send has been reset  signal from PLC, it
writes notify send  and send finished  signals to false.
8. ECS call backs WCS, and the sending goods process is over.
Conveyor line (S7)

2. Click New to enter the Add page.

nese characters.

5.13.7.5 Door (Modbus)
Description
There are two door device events:
• Door opening event
• Door closing event
The door is controlled by device events on the edge. When the AMR rea-
ches an end node, it sends an open door  event. After crossing the edge
and reaching the other end node, it sends a close door  event.
Precondition
• The device/PLC/simulator is turned on.
• The device is enabled in device management, and the device monitor-
ing shows connected.
Adding a rule chain
1. Navigate to Equipment > Rule Chain Library .
2. Click New to enter the Add page.
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
564/719 | www.kuka.com KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026

=== PDF PAGE 565 ===

nese characters.
4. Select Door as the device type from the drop-down list.
Fig. 5-574: Adding a rule chain

6. Click OK to enter the rule chain editing page.
7. Click Template and select Door(Modbus) from the drop-down list.
Fig. 5-575: Selecting a template
8. Click OK to save.
9. Set the rule chain parameters.

Equipment configuration for AMR to cross the door
When the AMR needs to cross the edge that has a door (from node 65 to
node 74), it sends an open door event.
After the door opens, the AGV crosses the 65->74 edge, reaches end
node 74, and sends a close door event.
The open door event is a device before event for the 65->74 edge, and
the close door event is a device after event for the 65->74 edge.

• Set Name to R-ARM.

• Set Device Event to feedback AMR left_R-ARM_position .
• Click Save [Device After] .
5.13.7.8 Device occupied
Description
By configuring a pair of receive events in the rule chain, one event is re-
sponsible for occupying the device, and the other is responsible for releas-
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15 V2 | Issued: 09.04.2026 www.kuka.com | 591/719
Programming and configuration

=== PDF PAGE 592 ===
ing the device. In this way, each device group (for devices that support
grouping) has an occupation and release events.
During the operation of the device, if one of the groups triggers the occu-
pation event, the requests of the receive events of the other groups will
be rejected before this group executes the release event. This kind of
mechanism is device exclusivity.
Procedure
1. Create two new receiving events "Occupy Device" and "Release De-
vice" in the rule chain.
2. Configure the "robotId" and "ruleChainType" parameters in the events.
Fig. 5-620: Device occupying config
Fig. 5-621: Device release config
3. Devices that have triggered the "Occupied Device" event can be re-
leased from occupation by canceling the task on the device monitoring
page. Alternatively, the occupation can be released by manually exe-
cuting the "Release Device" event on the device's event testing page.

Description
The KoM UTV-3 Series are KUKA Omnimove robot types that operate
KUKA.NavSolution. The robot types are:
• KoM UTV-3 E375 3000
KUKA.AMR Fleet 2.15

=== PDF PAGE 593 ===
• KoM UTV-3 E375 7000
• KoM UTV-3 E575 7000
• KoM UTV-3 E575 12000
• KoM UTV-3 E575 17000
• KoM UTV-3 E575 25000
Omnimove robots support the following features:
• Customized action ( >>> 5.3.3 "Customized actions" Page 295 )
• Edge configuration omnimove settings on advance settings
(>>> 5.14.2 "Omnimove settings of layout" Page 596 )
Precondition
• Map is ready for KoM UTV-3.
5.14.1 KoM UTV-3 simulator
Description
The simulator with iiQKA.Mobility is different from the robot with
KUKA.AMR OS.
Changing the platform configuration and simulated configuration
1. Go to /opt/server/simulation/config.
$ cd /opt/server/simulation/config
2. Edit platform-config.yml as shown in the example below.
- type_name: 'Omnimove'
drive_type: 'HOLONOMIC'
mass: 500                        # Mass of the vehicle. [kg]
max_translational_velocity: 1.0  # Translational velocity (might be slower 
than the norm of x and y components). [m/s]
# Settings for max_velocity, max_acceleration, max_decelaration:
# x, y, theta are non-empty: this case is only valid for a holonomic 
platform that can control each dimension independently.
# x, theta are non-empty, y is empty: this case is valid for differential 
platforms.
max_velocity: [1.0, 0.5, 0.4]     # Maximum velocity in x and y direction 
and around the theta axis. [m/s] [rad/s]
max_acceleration: [0.2, 0.2, 0.1] # Maximum acceleration in x and y 
direction and around the theta axis. [m/s²] [rad/s²]
max_decelaration: [0.2, 0.2, 0.1] # Maximum deceleration in x and y 
direction and around the theta axis. [m/s²] [rad/s²]
backward_motion_permitted: true
shape_reference: '0.775, 0.425, 0.775, -0.425, -0.775,-0.425, -0.775, 0.425' 
# Corner points of the vehicle [m]
height: 0.35                      # Height of the vehicle. [m]
odometry_center_offset: [0, 0]    # Odomoetry center offset in x and y 
direction. [m]
mass_center_offset: [0, 0, 0]     # Mass center offset in x, y and z 
direction. [m]
3. Edit simulated-robot-config.yml as shown in the example be-
low.

5.14.2 Omnimove settings of layout
Description
Robot direction can be set on the edge to support horizontal and vertical
movement for the omnimove robot.
Default robot direction
If the robot direction is not set in Advance properties, it will default to the
direction set in Regular properties on edge. By default, this direction is
based on the map and set to 0 degrees.

5.15.1 Custom Carry
Description
Custom Carry is used to create simple carry workflows, including a lift-up
node and a lower-down node. These workflows can be triggered as cus-
tom buttons on the PDA.
Configuration
Click on + to add a configuration.

node.
• Custom Area: Group the nodes. The actual start-
ing node must be selected when triggering cus-
tom carry on the PDA. The start node of the
workflow generated by Custom Area is a node.

triggering custom carry on the PDA. The start

Container
Source
The way that the AMR recognizes a container from
the start node includes the following methods:
• Up Vision Camera Recognition : The AMR will
activate the up-vision camera to recognize the
QR code on the shelf to obtain shelf information.
• Auto Add Virtual Container : The WCS will gen-
erate a container with an automatically generated
container code and enter it at the corresponding
angle. The container model depends on the first
Rack Model configuration on the Rack Node. The

tainer.
